## Snapshot
Mega-cap software + hyperscale cloud. **🆕🔴 SEGMENT STRUCTURE CHANGED EFFECTIVE FY27 (announced 2026-09-03): TWO reporting segments, not three — **Agents and Infra** (Microsoft Cloud, productivity and server licensing, consulting and support; reported product/service lines: Azure · Microsoft 365 cloud · Productivity and server licensing · Industry solutions · Frontier and support services) and **Devices and Consumer** (Windows, XBOX, advertising; lines: Search and advertising · XBOX · Windows OEM and devices)** (Microsoft "FY27 Segments and Investor Metrics" — company primary, 2026-09). ⚠️⚠️ **THE OLD THREE-SEGMENT DESCRIPTION (Productivity & Business Processes / Intelligent Cloud / More Personal Computing) IS RETIRED TO `## Changelog` (2026-09-03) — AND **AZURE ITSELF IS REDEFINED**, SO EVERY AZURE GROWTH NUMBER ELSEWHERE ON THIS PAGE (the +43% F4Q26 print, the ~45% cc F1Q27 guide, every broker bogey and FY27/FY28 Azure estimate) WAS COMPUTED ON THE **OLD** DEFINITION AND IS NOT COMPARABLE TO WHAT MICROSOFT WILL REPORT FROM F1Q27 UNLESS EXPLICITLY MARKED.** Also retired as separately disclosed investor metrics: **LinkedIn revenue growth** and **Dynamics 365 revenue growth**. In the AI stack MSFT sits across every layer it can monetize: own datacenters + first-party silicon (Maia 200 accelerator, Cobalt 200 CPU), the agent app platform (Foundry, Agent 365, IQ/Work IQ context layer), and high-value agentic apps (M365 Copilot, GitHub Copilot, Security Copilot, Dragon). Anchor relationship with OpenAI (equity stake + frontier-model IP rights to 2032 + Azure compute customer), plus Anthropic as both a model on Foundry and an Azure compute customer. Microsoft Cloud cleared $54.5B in revenue in the latest quarter; AI ARR >$37B, +123% y/y (Q3 FY26, 2026-04-29). OpenAI is the spine of the Azure AI story: UBS estimates ~half of Azure's 39% cc growth came from AI workloads (16pts disclosed in 3Q/Mar) and pegged run-rate Azure AI revenue at ~$18.6B as of 4Q/Jun-25, with substantially all of it OpenAI-tied (UBS OpenAI deep-dive, 2025-09-02); MS models OpenAI explaining 49% of Azure AI growth in CY25 and 100%+ in CY26 (MS MSFT/OpenAI, 2025-05-21). OpenAI's training supercluster spend is off-P&L (netted against MSFT's equity stake, not in Azure revs/COGS/capex), while inference compute and the rev-share land in reported Azure (UBS, 2025-09-02).

No rating, no price target and no estimate anywhere in it.** ⚠️ **SAME ROOM AS THE 09-09 BLOCK BELOW, WHICH STANDS AS THE PRIMARY. Everything already carried there is NOT re-logged: the cautious-to-aggressive denial, the land / cold-shell / powered-shell staging, the FY28-30 land framing, dock-to-live down ~50%, the 4x first-party token throughput, ~11,000 models, Cobalt in 25 data centres, the GitHub COGS-before-revenue mechanism, the equity-and-leases answer, and Maia 100/200 complete with Maia 300 in development.**
- ⚠️🔴🔴 **THE ONE CONFLICT, AND IT IS LOGGED RATHER THAN RESOLVED — THE JALAPEÑO IP TERM.** These notes record that Microsoft has full IP access to [[OPENAI]]'s Jalapeño chip for its own data centres, that the **rights run through 2032**, and that **anything developed in that window remains available in perpetuity** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Microsoft small group, 2026-09-14). ➤ **The 09-09 transcript of the same room says the opposite about what happens at expiry:** _"We give back the IP come 2032."_ ⚠️ **A PERPETUAL LICENCE TO EVERYTHING CO-DEVELOPED BEFORE 2032 AND A HAND-BACK AT 2032 ARE DIFFERENT ASSETS, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE ENTIRE TERMINAL VALUE OF THE SECOND SILICON LEG — on the first reading Microsoft exits 2032 owning a chip line, on the second it exits owning only what it built itself. NEITHER IS ADOPTED: the 09-09 version is a verbatim transcript quote, the 09-14 version is the analyst's own note on the same meeting. This is an IR question, not a modelling input.**
- 🔴 **HOW MICROSOFT PRICES A TOKEN INTERNALLY — THE ROUTING HIERARCHY, WHICH THIS PAGE HAS NEVER HAD IN ORDER.** The harness routes each request to the best model on **quality and task completion first, then cost**, and the three model classes carry three different economics: **OpenAI models are royalty-free, Microsoft's own AI models are effectively free, and third-party models carry external economics.** Azure offers roughly **11,000 models** with manual selection or auto-routing, Foundry lets developers A/B test on performance, cost, latency and quality, and **frontier models are reserved for frontier problems** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Microsoft small group, 2026-09-14). ➤ **This is the mechanism underneath the tokenmaxxing debate on this page: Microsoft's cheapest marginal token is its own or OpenAI's, so a mix shift toward first-party and open-weight models is margin-accretive BY CONSTRUCTION and does not require the DeepSeek arrangement to work. It also means quality-first and cost-discipline are not in tension at the harness layer — cost is the tie-breaker, not the filter.** ⚠️ **No mix, no share and no cost-per-token figure was given. This is the ORDERING of the decision, not its economics.** Cross-ref [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).
- 🔴 **CO-WORK — WHAT IS NET-NEW IS THE GOVERNANCE CONSOLE AND THE ABSENCE OF A PRE-EXISTING USAGE BASE.** Consumption revenue is **already inside the M365 acceleration guidance**, which management expects to accelerate through FY27 under both the old reporting structure and the new one that includes GitHub — but management was explicit that Co-Work is early-stage, uncertain and should ramp gradually. **Unlike GitHub, which had months of heavy agent usage before monetisation was switched on, Co-Work has no pre-existing usage base**, so Microsoft and its customers are learning the model together. E7 / Agent 365 is framed as **AI cost governance**: a new M365 console shows budget owners **tokens by user, task types, cost and projected monthly spend**, positioned as avoiding the Azure spend-then-optimise-then-cut cycle, and consumption is framed as **incremental TAM** although licences retain usage limits (GS Communacopia 2026 — Microsoft small group, 2026-09-14). ⚠️ **NO Co-Work revenue, seat, token or attach figure was given and none should be inferred — already inside the guide is a statement about the guide, not a disclosure of size.** ➤ **Modelling consequence, and it cuts against this page's own 09-09 read: the GitHub COGS-before-revenue mechanic CANNOT repeat for Co-Work, because there is no unmonetised usage stock to burn through first. That removes one identified source of cloud-GM noise and replaces it with a harder one — a consumption ramp with no observable leading indicator.**
- **SUPPLY-CHAIN LOCK-IN, STATED AS A POSTURE RATHER THAN A NUMBER: long-term agreements have been signed with much of the supply chain, mostly in the last 12 months** (GS Communacopia 2026 — Microsoft small group, 2026-09-14). ⚠️ **No counterparties, no volumes, no dollars and no tenors — it is the company-side corroboration of Hood's LTAs-securing-full-sets-of-kit remark (09-09 relay, `Full log`) and of the memory-LTA squeeze this page tracks, and nothing more.** **Management also named four moats — a complete platform, the sales distribution network, customers valuing security, sovereignty and IP protection, and a sophisticated technical stack — and said customer emphasis on IP PROTECTION has changed the sales conversation in recent months, with enterprises wanting their own learning loop.** ➤ **The IP-protection clause is the one with a read-through: it is a hyperscaler saying enterprises are now buying ISOLATION, which is the commercial argument for sovereign and dedicated capacity that [[CRWV]] and [[NBIS]] make from the other side of the market.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-07 — A 4x EFFICIENCY NUMBER ON COPILOT, AND WHY IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT MSFT DATAPOINT OF THE WEEK.**
- 🔴 **"Microsoft recently said it INCREASED THROUGHPUT FOR COPILOT WORKLOADS BY ROUGHLY 4x SINCE THE BEGINNING OF THE YEAR through optimization across SILICON, SYSTEMS AND SOFTWARE"** (cited by Gaetano Research, "Fiber Optics Boom Bust: How Similar Is Today? Part 2", 2026-09-07 — *independent Substack author, NOT a broker: no rating, no price target, no house model*).
- ➤➤ **The author's frame is why this matters, and it is the sharpest bear mechanism published this week: he asks what the AI-cycle equivalent of DWDM is — the innovation that made already-installed fibre far more productive and thereby destroyed the incremental-demand case in 2001 — and answers EFFICIENCY. "Better GPUs, better models, quantization, caching, better software, better scheduling, higher utilization and more efficient inference can all allow the INFRASTRUCTURE WE ALREADY HAVE to produce more useful AI output." A 4x throughput gain on a flagship workload inside eight months is the best available measurement of that force, and MICROSOFT PUBLISHED IT ITSELF.** ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED as a capex signal — a 4x throughput gain on one workload says nothing about total demand elasticity, and Microsoft has simultaneously said demand exceeds capacity. But it belongs on this page as the quantified version of the efficiency argument, sourced to Microsoft.**
- ➤ **Read it alongside [[UBER]]'s CFO on the same theme the same week (cost per token DOWN, total AI spend held broadly stable by moving tasks to lower-cost/open-weight models) — the buyer and the supplier are describing the same deflation from opposite ends.** Cross-ref [[themes/tokenmaxxing.md]].

**🆕 2026-09-07 — AN EXPERT CALL ON THE PART OF THE DATACENTRE NOBODY MODELS: POWER-FLEXIBILITY CONTROL.** (AlphaSense Expert Insight · **Principal Design Engineer at Microsoft Corporation**, "Power Management Strategies in AI Data Centers", 2026-09-07, 2pp — *paid expert network; the expert is a serving employee speaking in a personal capacity, and the summary carries no company data*)
- **Architecture described: a three-tier hierarchy — SITE controller, CLUSTER controller, NODE controller — each with distinct power-budgeting and orchestration roles. Node level = telemetry (GPU/CPU power, utilisation, temperature), DVFS adjustments, power capping and workload optimisation "to respond to RAPID POWER REDUCTION EVENTS WITHOUT VIOLATING SLAs." Policy layer = SLA-based prioritisation, power-budget allocation, admission control, curtailment, placement and renewable-aware policies.**
- 🔴 **The binding constraint, stated twice: "CONTROL LOOP LATENCY IS PRIMARILY LIMITED BY INFRASTRUCTURE AND TELEMETRY POLLING INTERVALS (BMC, PDU, BMS), NOT BY GPU FIRMWARE OR NVML POWER CAP COMMANDS, which can respond at SUB-SECOND levels"**, and **"the main technical challenge is ALIGNING THE FAST RESPONSE OF IT LOADS (GPUs) WITH SLOWER FACILITY-SIDE CONTROLS (cooling, power distribution), requiring careful orchestration and RATE LIMITING TO AVOID OSCILLATIONS AND INSTABILITY."**
- ➤ **Investable content, stated plainly: the bottleneck in demand-response-capable AI datacentres is NOT the accelerator — it is the BMC/PDU/BMS telemetry layer. That is a direct read-through to the management-controller and power-distribution vendors ([[themes/800v-dc-power.md]]), and it is the technical reason hyperscalers can credibly offer grid flexibility at all.** ⚠️ *One expert, two pages, no numbers. Logged for the mechanism, not for sizing.*

**🆕 2026-09-06 — NADELLA ON THE PRODUCT DIRECTION: "AUTOPILOTS" FOR LONG-RUNNING WORK.** @satyanadella, 2026-09-06: **"It was a great week for innovation across the model ecosystem. We're bringing THESE NEW MODELS into Copilot, enabling it to take on increasingly complex work, from quick questions to DELEGATED TASKS and, increasingly, COMPLETE LONG-RUNNING JOBS THROUGH AUTOPILOTS"** — with a worked example of an Opal-powered Autopilot on a secure Windows 365 Cloud PC processing a month of trail-cam footage into a highlight reel, labelled catalogue, spreadsheet and PowerPoint. ➤ *"These new models" is model-agnostic language from the company with the most to lose from single-vendor dependence — consistent with the multi-model posture this page already tracks.*

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-03/04 — THE SEGMENT OVERHAUL LOGGED ON 09-03 CAME WITH A NUMBER THIS PAGE DID NOT CARRY: MICROSOFT PUT AZURE'S ABSOLUTE REVENUE ON THE RECORD FOR THE FIRST TIME — $101.9bn FOR THE TWELVE MONTHS TO JUNE, +40% Y/Y — AND WILL REPORT IT QUARTERLY FROM HERE.**

- 🔴🔴 **THE DISCLOSURE: *"Microsoft's cloud unit **Azure's revenue was $101.9 BILLION in the twelve months ending in JUNE**, Microsoft disclosed Wednesday, **UP 40% from the year prior**. Microsoft made the disclosure, and revealed it **will start reporting Azure's revenue QUARTERLY**, as part of a broader overhaul… **Microsoft previously included Azure's results in a broader business segment… While it reported Azure's PERCENTAGE growth, it didn't break out the revenue regularly.** The new accounting structure will make Microsoft's Azure disclosures **DIRECTLY COMPARABLE to similar revenue reported by [[AMZN]] and [[GOOG]]**."* **Microsoft will also start disclosing total quarterly revenue for "M365 Cloud"** (Office 365 plus the Copilot suite). (The Information · Aaron Holmes, 2026-09-03.) ➤ ✅ **This completes the 09-03 entry. Nadella had told shareholders in July that Azure passed $100bn for the year to June; the exact figure and the commitment to quarterly disclosure are new.**
- ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE TRAP THE 09-03 ENTRY ALREADY FLAGGED APPLIES TO THIS NUMBER TOO: the $101.9bn is on the **OLD Azure definition** — before GitHub cloud, other developer cloud services and Security Copilot move to M365 commercial cloud, and Healthcare & Life Sciences moves to the new Industry solutions cloud. **Do not use $101.9bn as the base for a FY27 growth calculation against what Microsoft will report from F1Q27.** Management's own restated bridge (Azure ~45% cc → 44-45% cc; M365 commercial cloud ~15% → ~17% cc) is the only compliant way to span the change.**
- **THE SEGMENTS, RESTATED FROM THE SAME REPORT FOR THE RECORD: **two segments — "Agents and Infra"** (enterprise software plus Azure) **and "Devices and Consumer"** (Xbox, Surface, consumer Windows, plus advertising from Bing and LinkedIn). Nadella's stated rationale: *"give investors more transparency and demonstrate how new AI products are changing its business."*
- 🔴 **THE FRONTIER-MODEL FACT OF THE WEEK, AND IT IS FIRST-PARTY: [[OPENAI]]'s **GPT-6 Astra ships on Azure day one** — *"Excited to see early customers already using **Astra on Azure**!"* (@satyanadella, corpus, 2026-09-04T03:21Z), corroborated by @gdb and by UBS's desk (*"available… through GPT, OpenAI API, **Azure**, and AWS Bedrock"*, Dwyer, 2026-09-04). ⚠️ **The Information's launch piece named only AWS; Nadella's own post settles it.** ➤ **The read-through is competitive symmetry, not exclusivity: the newest frontier model is available on BOTH Azure and [[AMZN]] Bedrock at launch. Azure no longer has a timing advantage on OpenAI's flagship.**
- 🔴 **REGULATORY — A NAMED, DATED RISK THAT IS NEW TO THIS PAGE: *"Brussels will **VERY LIKELY designate Microsoft Azure and [[AMZN]] Web Services under the DIGITAL MARKETS ACT THIS FALL**, following a preliminary decision in late June"* — part of a broader push to bring AI into the EU's platform-regulation toolkit, alongside the DSA designation of [[OPENAI]]'s ChatGPT. ⚠️ **The DMA is the more serious of the two regimes: *"unlike the DSA which imposes risk-management, transparency and procedural obligations, the DMA REGULATES COMPANIES' BUSINESS MODELS."*** (Eurasia Group · Nick Reiners, "EU: ChatGPT designation sets AI chatbot precedent", 2026-09-04.) ➤ **Precedent for how far this can go: in June the Commission ordered [[META]] to reinstate third-party AI assistants' access to the WhatsApp for Business API.**
- **POSITIONING, FOR CONTEXT ONLY: UBS's prime-brokerage crowding screens put the hedge-fund pecking order at **[[META]] > [[ORCL]] > [[AMZN]] > MSFT > [[NVDA]] > [[GOOG]]**, with *"very little pushback"* on the ranking from clients (UBS TMI desk · Christina Dwyer, 2026-09-04). **Microsoft is mid-pack — less crowded than META/ORCL/AMZN, more than NVDA/GOOG.**


**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-03 — MICROSOFT COLLAPSES THREE REPORTING SEGMENTS INTO TWO FOR FY27 **AND REDEFINES AZURE** — THEN QUANTIFIES WHAT THE REDEFINITION COSTS ITS OWN GUIDE. THE ANSWER IS THE WHOLE INVESTOR QUESTION AND IT IS **MUCH SMALLER THAN THE PLUMBING SUGGESTS: AZURE ~45% cc → 44-45% cc, A 0-1pt HAIRCUT** — AGAINST A MIRROR-IMAGE **+2pts TO M365 COMMERCIAL CLOUD**.** (Microsoft "FY27 Segments and Investor Metrics" — company primary, 2026-09) · 22-page investor deck; Satya Nadella letter pp.4-5; restated history pp.15-18; re-based outlook pp.20-21. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260903_Microsoft_FY27_Segments_and_Investor_Metrics.html)
- **THE STRUCTURE, VERBATIM: *"Beginning with FY27, we will transition from our current three reporting segments, Productivity and Business Processes, Intelligent Cloud, and More Personal Computing, to two segments: **Agents and Infra** and **Devices and Consumer**. Within each of these segments, you will have full transparency of quarterly revenue across each of our key businesses, including Azure, M365 Cloud, Industry solutions, and ads."* (Nadella letter, p.4).** **Agents and Infra** = Microsoft Cloud + productivity and server licensing + consulting and support, reported as **Azure · Microsoft 365 cloud · Productivity and server licensing · Industry solutions · Frontier and support services**. **Devices and Consumer** = Windows, XBOX and advertising, reported as **Search and advertising · XBOX · Windows OEM and devices**. Management's own framing of the Azure line: *"Under this reporting structure, **Azure becomes more purely our consumption-based platform and infrastructure business**"* (p.5) — i.e. the apps/agents layer is deliberately being pulled OUT of Azure and put next to M365.
- 🔴 **WHAT ACTUALLY MOVES — THE AZURE REDEFINITION (pp.7, 11). OUT of Azure: (1) **GitHub cloud and other developer cloud services** and (2) **Security Copilot** → both into **Microsoft 365 commercial cloud**; (3) **Healthcare and Life Sciences cloud** → into the new **Industry solutions cloud**.** **Industry solutions cloud is a NEW metric = Dynamics 365 + LinkedIn Talent Solutions and Sales Solutions (previously inside LinkedIn) + Healthcare and Life Sciences cloud (previously inside Azure).** **Search and advertising (ex TAC) now ADDS LinkedIn Marketing Solutions and LinkedIn Premium Subscriptions.** **The M365 commercial SEAT metric now includes paid GitHub seats.** On the licensing side: **Server products now EXCLUDES the on-premises portion of GitHub/other developer services (→ M365 commercial products) and the on-premises Healthcare and Life Sciences piece (→ Industry solutions products)**; **"Frontier and support services" was previously "Enterprise and Partner services," otherwise unchanged**; **M365 consumer products was previously "Office consumer products," otherwise unchanged**; **XBOX unchanged**; Windows OEM and devices unchanged except a small amount of revenue previously in "Other."
- 🔴🔴 **THE MOST ACTIONABLE NUMBER IN THE DECK (p.21) — MICROSOFT RESTATED THE **JULY 29, 2026** GUIDANCE INTO THE NEW STRUCTURE, *"mechanical adjustments only"*, HOLDING EVERYTHING ELSE CONSTANT. THAT ISOLATES THE DEFINITION EFFECT PER LINE:** **Azure: OLD "growth of approximately **45%** in constant currency" → NEW "growth of **44% to 45%** in constant currency, with FX expected to decrease revenue by less than 1 point."** **M365 commercial cloud: OLD ~**15%** cc (~**16%** cc adjusting for prior-year in-period revenue recognition) → NEW ~**17%** cc (~**18%** adjusted).** **Productivity and server licensing: decline in low-single digits** (replacing OLD "Server products revenue — decline in low- to mid-single digits" + "M365 Commercial products — growth in mid-single digits"). **Industry solutions cloud: growth in high-single digits** (replacing OLD Dynamics 365 low-teens + LinkedIn high-single digits). **Search and advertising (ex TAC): OLD mid-single → NEW mid- to high-single digits.** **NO CHANGE to: commercial bookings, Microsoft Cloud gross margin percentage, M365 consumer cloud, XBOX, Windows OEM and devices, total-company revenue, COGS, opex, other income/expense, operating margin, tax rate or capex.** ➤ **THE READ, AND IT IS THE WHOLE POINT: despite GitHub cloud, Security Copilot AND Healthcare all leaving Azure, the headline Azure growth rate falls by only ~0-1pt — materially less than a reader would fear from the volume of plumbing that moved. The redefinition is NOT a way of flattering Azure and it is NOT a way of hiding a deceleration; it is roughly growth-neutral at the Azure line and it hands the +2pts straight to M365 commercial cloud.** ⚠️ **But the levels are NOT comparable: the FX framing also moved (OLD "roughly 1 point in Productivity and Business Processes, less than 1 point in Intelligent Cloud" → NEW "roughly 1 point in Agents and Infra, and no meaningful impact in Devices and Consumer"), and the segment revenue guide is re-cut as **Agents and Infra $75.15-75.75bn** and **Devices and Consumer $14.7-15.2bn** against an unchanged total-company **$89.85-90.95bn** (p.20).**
- **THE F1Q27 OUTLOOK LINES THAT DID *NOT* MOVE, restated here because the page needs them on one line (p.20, "As provided on July 29, 2026"): COGS **$29.6-29.8bn**; opex **$16.8-16.9bn**; other income and expense **~$(100)m excluding any impact from our investments in OpenAI**; operating margin *"expected to remain relatively flat year-over-year"*; effective tax rate **~20%**; **capital expenditures "expected to be over $50 billion including the impact of the useful life update."** ⚠️ **That >$50bn Q1 capex mark is on the POST-reclassification basis (finance→operating lease shift + 15→25yr useful life) already logged on this page from the 07-29 call — it is not comparable to the old-definition FY27 bogeys (BofA $243.5bn, UBS $255-260bn, JPM ~$260bn, MS/Altimeter CY27 $276bn).**
- ✅ **AND THE ONE-LINE OPENAI CALLOUT WORTH LIFTING OFF THE OLD COLUMN, because it bears on this page's OpenAI-concentration thread: commercial bookings guidance was framed as *"healthy growth on a growing expiry base **when adjusted for OpenAI contracts in the prior year**"* — i.e. management is itself pre-normalising the bookings comp for OpenAI, which is the disclosure this page has been asking for. **The line is carried UNCHANGED into the new structure ("No change to outlook"), so the OpenAI comp problem survives the segment change intact.**
- **RESTATED SEGMENT HISTORY, $ in millions, FY26 / FY25 (p.15). **Agents and Infra**: revenue **$268,127 / $218,783**, operating income **$136,365 / $110,814**. **Devices and Consumer**: revenue **$63,712 / $62,941**, operating income **$18,872 / $17,714**. Total revenue **$331,839 / $281,724**, total operating income **$155,237 / $128,528** — totals identical to the as-reported page (p.16), as they must be.** ➤ **The shape this exposes in one line: **Agents and Infra is 80.8% of FY26 revenue and 87.8% of FY26 operating income**, on **+22.6% y/y revenue growth**, while Devices and Consumer grew **+1.2%** and carries 12.2% of the profit. The new structure makes the two-speed company impossible to miss — which is presumably the intent, and is also the strongest argument that the change is a disclosure upgrade rather than a smoke screen.** (Segment splits computed from the disclosed restated table.)
- **RESTATED PRODUCT/SERVICE REVENUE, FY26 / FY25 ($m, p.17): **Azure $101,938 / $72,610** · **Microsoft 365 cloud $100,299 / $84,605** · **Productivity and server licensing $37,285 / $35,391** · **Search and advertising $24,835 / $22,171** · **XBOX $21,790 / $23,455** · **Industry solutions $20,345 / $18,417** · **Windows OEM and devices $17,087 / $17,315** · **Frontier and support services $8,260 / $7,760**.** ✅ **CROSS-CHECK THIS PAGE ALREADY NEEDED: the 4QFY26 print put "FY26 Azure revenue surpassed $100B for the first time" on the OLD definition. **The RESTATED FY26 Azure is $101,938m — so Azure clears $100bn on the NEW definition too.** The $100bn milestone survives the redefinition; it is not a casualty of it.** **Restated Azure by quarter, FY26: Q1 $22,384 · Q2 $24,129 · Q3 $26,008 · Q4 $29,417; FY25: Q1 $16,021 · Q2 $17,337 · Q3 $18,540 · Q4 $20,712.** **Implied y/y COMPUTED from the restated table (not a company-stated growth rate): Q1 +39.7% · Q2 +39.2% · Q3 +40.3% · Q4 +42.0% · FY26 +40.4%.** Two integrity checks that pass: **Frontier and support services $8,260 / $7,760 is byte-identical to as-reported Enterprise and partner services**, and **XBOX $21,790 / $23,455 is identical to as-reported** — confirming the deck's "otherwise unchanged" claims. Windows OEM and devices restated $17,087 vs as-reported Windows and devices $17,084 — a **$3m** difference, which is exactly the "small amount of revenue previously reported in Other" the deck flags (p.8).
- 🔴 **THE HAIRCUT PROVEN ON ACTUALS, NOT JUST ON THE GUIDE — the restated-vs-reported FY26 investor metrics (p.18; format is GAAP % / cc %): **Azure revenue growth RESTATED FY26 40% vs AS REPORTED 41% / 40%**, and **Q4 FY26 restated 42% vs as reported 43%**; Q1/Q2/Q3 are unchanged at 40%/39% · 39%/38% · 40%/39%. **M365 commercial cloud RESTATED FY26 18% / 15% vs AS REPORTED 17% / 14%**, with **Q4 restated 16% / 15% vs reported 14%**. **M365 commercial SEAT growth RESTATED FY26 7% vs REPORTED 6%** (the GitHub paid seats). **Search and advertising ex TAC RESTATED FY26 14% / 12% vs REPORTED 12% / 10%** (the LinkedIn Marketing/Premium addition). **Industry solutions cloud RESTATED FY26 12% / 10%** (no as-reported equivalent; the nearest old lines were Dynamics 365 18% / 15% and LinkedIn 11% / 9%).** ➤ **So the FY26 actuals say the same thing the FY27 guide says: Azure loses ~1pt, M365 commercial cloud gains ~1-2pts, seats gain ~1pt, ads gain ~2pts. The definition change is a REALLOCATION, and its direction is consistently AWAY from the Azure headline and TOWARD the apps/agents and advertising headlines.**
- ⚠️ **DISCLOSURE QUALITY — BOTH DIRECTIONS, AND THE PAGE SHOULD CARRY BOTH (p.12). **GAINED:** a standalone quarterly **Azure revenue DOLLAR line** (Azure has never before been a disclosed revenue line — it sat inside "Server products and cloud services," $129,425m FY26 as reported), plus dollar lines for **Microsoft 365 cloud** and the new **Industry solutions**. That is a genuine and large transparency upgrade and it is what Nadella is selling on p.4. **LOST:** **"LinkedIn revenue growth" and "Dynamics 365 revenue growth" are BOTH RETIRED from the FY27 investor-metric list**, replaced by the single blended "Industry solutions cloud revenue growth." **LinkedIn ceases to exist as a disclosed revenue line entirely** (it was **$19,817m FY26 / $17,812m FY25** as reported, p.17) — split between Industry solutions cloud (Talent + Sales Solutions) and Search and advertising (Marketing Solutions + Premium). **Dynamics 365, similarly, can no longer be tracked on its own.** ➤ **Net: Microsoft is trading TWO mature-asset disclosures for THREE AI-era ones. Bullish on the AI story's legibility, and it also permanently removes the ability to audit the Dynamics/CRM bookings moderation this page logged on 07-30 — the exact line [[CRM]] is being read against.**
- ➤ **WHAT THIS DOES TO EVERY MODEL ON THIS PAGE, stated plainly so no one nets old and new: (1) BofA's **Azure +41.8% FY27E / +37.7% FY28E** (09-01), MS's **Azure 40.0 / 42.3 / 43.0% FY26-28e** (07-27), UBS's 42-43% exit rate and the Street's 40.6% initial 1Q27 bar are **ALL OLD-DEFINITION** and mechanically ~0-1pt too high against what will actually print. (2) Every M365 commercial cloud estimate is **~2pts TOO LOW** against the new basis — including UBS's FY27 16.2% (07-26) and, most sharply, **Jefferies' 09-02 quarterly path (13.9% → 16.6%), which was published ONE DAY before this deck and is therefore entirely on the old definition.** (3) The **Productivity & Business Processes = ~one-third of revenue / ~45% of company profit** framing (JPM · Chatterjee, 08-13) no longer maps to a reported segment at all. (4) **No house on this page has yet re-based.** The first broker note that publishes on the NEW definition is the one to read, and the gap between a re-based and a non-re-based Azure estimate will look like a disagreement when it is a units problem.**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-09-01 — THE BofA **PRIMARY PUBLISHED NOTE** BEHIND THE 09-01 DESK RELAY — AND IT CARRIES FOUR THINGS THE RELAY DID NOT: THE PO METHODOLOGY, THE MAI COST LADDER IN NUMBERS, A FULL FY27-29 FORECAST SET WITH **FY27E FCF −51.7%**, AND AN FY27 EPS THAT SITS **BELOW** CONSENSUS — SO THE $600 IS A **MULTIPLE** CALL, NOT AN ESTIMATE CALL.** (BofA "AI strategy and execution support higher valuation; raise PO to $600", 2026-09-01) · Tal Liani / Eden Vacnich / Kevin Niederpruem, BofAS; timestamp 01 September 2026 01:00AM EDT. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/research1mlcom_20260901_BofA_MSFT_raise_PO_600.html)
- **THE MOVE, AND THE "KEY CHANGES" TABLE HAS EXACTLY ONE LINE IN IT: *Price Obj. — Previous **500.00**, Current **600.00***. Reiterate **BUY**; price at the time **$507.29**. ⚠️ **No estimate line was changed in the Key Changes block — do not read the PO raise as a numbers raise.**
- **THE HEADLINE DETAIL, from the primary: *"Azure growth **accelerating from 39% in 3Q26 to 43% 4Q26 and guidance of 45% in 1Q27**"*; *"Paid **M365 Copilot seats exceeded 30mn, with net additions MORE THAN DOUBLING QoQ**"*; *"**RPO increased 84% YoY**"* — and the level is given as **$678bn** (*"convert its **$678bn RPO** into revenue"*). ⚠️ **THIS CORRECTS THE RELAY: the 09-01 spec-sales row on this page carries BofA's RPO as *"~$700bn"*. The primary says **$678bn**, which also matches this page's own 07-30 marks (BofA callback ~$680B; FundaAI $678bn). Use $678bn.** BofA also puts the Street's initial 1Q27 Azure bar at **40.6%**.
- **THE PO METHODOLOGY, VERBATIM AND NET-NEW: *"Our price objective of **$600** is based on **28x our CY27E P/E**, **a premium to the peer group of 18-25x**. Sustained revenue growth and margin profile warrants the multiple above the high end of the peer group range."* (previously 24x). ➤ **The peer-group band is the part worth keeping: 28x is not a re-rate to the group, it is a stated premium ABOVE the top of it.**
- 🔴 **AND HERE IS THE TENSION THE RELAY HID — BofA IS *BELOW* CONSENSUS ON THE EARNINGS IT IS PAYING 28x FOR. BofA FY27E (Jun) adj EPS **$19.55** vs **Bloomberg consensus $19.78** and **Visible Alpha $19.68**; FY28E **$22.89** vs BBG **$23.50** / VA **$23.39**; FY29E **$27.61** vs VA **$28.33** (BBG NA). EPS path (Jun FY, adj): 2025A **$13.64** → 2026A **$17.94** (+31.6%) → 2027E **$19.55** (+8.9%) → 2028E **$22.89** (+17.1%) → 2029E **$27.61** (+20.6%).** ⚠️ **BASIS: BofA is FY-JUNE. The Consensus snapshot at the top of this page is CALENDAR (CY27E EPS $21.32) — the two are not comparable and must not be netted.**
- **THE FULL FORECAST SET (US$mn, FY Jun): Sales **281,724 / 331,839 / 388,537 / 460,839 / 557,817** (FY25A→FY29E; +14.9% / +17.8% / +17.1% / +18.6% / +21.0%) · Gross profit **193,893 / 225,465 / 257,969 / 300,821 / 355,898** · EBITDA **162,681 / 198,685 / 240,463 / 291,752 / 362,482** · Adj net income **101,823 / 133,790 / 145,408 / 169,856 / 204,452** · Operating margin **45.6% / 46.8% / 46.1% / 46.1% / 45.8%** · D&A **34,153 / 43,448 / 61,391 / 79,424 / 106,898** · ROE **33.3% / 34.0% / 29.1% / 27.0% / 25.9%** · ROCE **24.2% / 24.8% / 22.1% / 21.8% / 21.6%**. Valuation on $507.29: P/E **37.2x / 28.3x / 25.9x / 22.2x / 18.4x**; EV/EBITDA **24.1x / 19.7x / 16.3x / 13.4x / 10.8x**; FCF yield **1.9% / 1.8% / 0.9% / 1.2% / 2.1%**.**
- 🔴 **THE CAPEX AND FCF LINES ARE THE SHARPEST THING IN THE NOTE AND THE DESK RELAY DID NOT MENTION THEM: capex **(64,551) / (115,948) / (205,638) / (215,724) / (250,452)** and **FCF 71,611 / 66,987 / 32,376 / 44,564 / 79,749** — i.e. **FY27E FCF −51.7% y/y to ~$32bn**, then +37.6% and +79.0%. Net debt/equity **3.8% / 4.4% / 5.4% / 4.1% / −0.9%**; asset replacement ratio (capex/depreciation) peaks at **3.3x in FY27E**.** ➤ **Read against the relay's line that Microsoft is *"committed to POSITIVE FCF in '27"*: BofA's own model has FY27 FCF positive but **cut in half** — the two statements are consistent only because the bar is zero.**
- **QUARTERLY EPS PATH (BofA): FY26 Q1 **3.72A** · Q2 **5.16A** · Q3 **4.27A** · Q4 **4.81A**; FY27 Q1 **4.69E** · Q2 **4.80E** · Q3 **4.88E** · Q4 **5.18E**. DPS 3.23 / 3.55 / 3.63 / 3.64 / 3.65 (dividends paid held flat at $27,032mn from FY27E).** ⚠️ **UNRECONCILED AGAINST THIS PAGE: BofA carries F4Q26 EPS as **4.81A** while this page carries the reported **$4.74** (of which $0.27 discrete → clean ≈$4.47). A ~$0.07 basis difference, flagged not resolved.**
- 🔴 **THE AZURE MODEL, AND IT CONTRADICTS THE H1-ACCELERATION FRAMING ON THIS PAGE: BofA models **Azure +41.8% in FY27E**, up from **39.9% in FY26** (FY24 33.3%, FY25 34.2%), then **decelerating to 37.7% in FY28E**.** ➤ **Management guided **1Q27 at ~45%** and said H1 FY27 would accelerate. A 41.8% full-year FY27 against a 45% first quarter is an implicit call that Azure growth **fades through the back half of FY27** — that is a falsifiable disagreement with management's own framing and it is the single most useful number in the note.**
- **THE MAI COST LADDER — the "AI economics" leg of the 28x, now with the numbers attached (all from the primary): **MAI-Code-1-Flash** delivers *"comparable performance to **GPT-5.6** for common Excel tasks at a lower cost"* (built first for GitHub Copilot); **MAI-Code-1.1-Flash** in GitHub Copilot *"uses **25% fewer tokens per task** and is offered at **one quarter of the price** of the prior version"*; other MAI models reduce GPU consumption costs *"by as much as **84% in PowerPoint** and **89% in Dynamics 365**"*; **MAI-Cyber-1-Flash** inside **MDASH** (Microsoft's multi-agent vulnerability system) handles *"up to **90% of tasks**"* with the combined system *"delivering comparable performance at **50% of the cost** of leading models"*. Portfolio: **MAI-Thinking-1** (reasoning/math/coding → Foundry + enterprise agents) · **MAI-Image-2.5** (PowerPoint/Bing/OneDrive) · **MAI-Voice-2** (Dynamics 365 Contact Center, Foundry) · **MAI-Transcribe-1.5** (Dragon Copilot).** ➤ **The strategic claim is the *harness*, not the models: Copilot is built as a **model-independent harness** (enterprise context + memory, data/tool connection, action governance, output evaluation) so *"Copilot's value does not depend exclusively on MAI, OpenAI, or any other single model family."* Cross-ref [themes/tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md).
- **THE FOUR INFRASTRUCTURE LEVERS, as BofA enumerates them: **①** engineering + software optimisation across the CPU/GPU fleet lifted **Copilot workload throughput 4x since the beginning of the year**, monetised immediately because *"demand continues to exceed supply"*, and *"contributed to Azure's 4Q outperformance"*; **②** time to **operationalise new GPUs in its largest regions cut by nearly 50%** over the past year; **③** **Maia 200** — MAI models co-designed with it *"deliver **40% better performance per watt**"*, Maia *"is designed to support multiple model families, including OpenAI and MAI models"*, and Microsoft is *"reportedly planning a significant production ramp for its next-generation **Maia 300** chip in 2027"*; **④** **Fairwater** — *"**approximately 1GW** of capacity added in 4Q26"*, *"on track to roughly **DOUBLE total capacity over the next two years**"*, first Wisconsin Fairwater **completed June 2026** with a second facility at the campus *"expected to be completed in **2028**"*.** ✅ **The 40% perf/watt figure CORROBORATES the ~40% claim already on this page from the 07-30 BofA callback; the Maia 300 "2027 ramp" is consistent with the 08-11 TrendForce/The Information 300k-unit story already logged.**
- **THE RISKS, as stated: *"1) **near term gross margin pressure** as the Azure business grows as a percentage of overall revenue. 2) **AI applications and model providers could innovate at a faster rate than Microsoft**, potentially limiting the company's growth profile. 3) **Enterprise application spending has proven to be highly cyclical**, given the more discretionary nature of applications projects."*** ⚠️ **Note what is NOT in BofA's risk list: capex/FCF, OpenAI concentration, or power/shell supply — the three risks this page actually carries. The omission is worth holding against the 28x.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-27 — A COMPETITOR PUTS A COMMERCIAL PRICE ON THE EU TEAMS REMEDY, ON THE RECORD, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME THIS PAGE HAS A NAMED BENEFICIARY SAYING THE UNBUNDLING IS WORKING.**
- 🔴 **SALESFORCE'S DEPUTY CFO, ASKED WHY SLACK'S MOMENTUM INFLECTED: *"engagement is starting to open up a bit more. We're seeing a lot more customers come to the table being willing to experiment with Slack **even if they're a TEAMS SHOP**. Obviously, there's **ANTITRUST PRESSURE ON MICROSOFT and they're doing some breakup, especially in EUROPE and STANDALONE SALES OF TEAMS. So that's certainly HELPING.** But the experimentation coming from the customer base, especially in the ENTERPRISE space, of willing and wanting to try Slack has really started to **OPEN A LOT OF DOORS, EVEN IF THEY ALREADY HAVE TEAMS in the ecosystem**"* (Mike Spencer, Deutsche Bank Technology Conference, 2026-08-27).** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Salesforce_Inc_Conf_Presentation_Call_2026827_DN000000003117875164pdf.html)
- ➤ **WHY THIS IS WORTH LOGGING RATHER THAN FILING AS COMPETITOR NOISE: the EU Teams unbundling has been discussed on this wiki as a regulatory event with no measured commercial consequence. A direct competitor has now attributed part of an inflection to it — and the same week [[CRM]] disclosed **Slack's fastest quarterly NNAOV growth since acquisition** and **Slackbot users +150% q/q**, with Slack listed FIRST among the quarter's growth drivers by Salesforce IR. That is a claim with corroborating numbers attached, even if the numbers belong to Salesforce rather than to Microsoft.** ⚠️⚠️ **CAVEATS, AND THEY ARE REAL: (1) this is a self-interested competitor explaining its own good quarter, and the same executive attributes most of the momentum to Slackbot's AI capability rather than to regulation — the antitrust point is explicitly secondary ("*that's certainly helping*"); (2) no seat, revenue or displacement number is given, on either side; (3) the speaker is a former Microsoft executive, which cuts both ways on credibility. **Nothing here sizes a Teams share loss, and it should not be used to.**
- ⚠️ **A second, unrelated read-through from the same source: Salesforce **hired Rohan Kumar FROM MICROSOFT** to run its platform business, following Steve Fisher's retirement. Logged only as a talent-flow datapoint.** **And on the demand side, the standing 07-30 read runs the other way and is unchanged — Microsoft's own commentary that Dynamics 365 CRM bookings *"continued to MODERATE with LONGER SALES CYCLES"* remains the most negative competitive datapoint of the quarter for [[CRM]], and it was not revisited this week.**

**🆕🔴 2026-09-18 — NSCALE'S S-1 PUTS A FILED NUMBER ON MICROSOFT'S OFF-BALANCE-SHEET NEOCLOUD CAPACITY: UP TO $43.8BN OF STATEMENTS OF WORK THROUGH DECEMBER 2033, 5-6 YEAR TRANCHES — AND MICROSOFT TOOK THE STARGATE NORWAY CAPACITY OPENAI WALKED FROM.** (Nscale Limited S-1 + exhibit 10.19 "Partner Statement of Work", Microsoft Ireland ↔ Nscale, Kvandal North, Narvik; filed 2026-09-18; full page: [[NSCALE]].)
- ✅ **Filed facts: SOWs signed Sep-2025→Apr-2026 "provide for payments to us of up to approximately $43.8 billion through December 2033", GPU service terms "five to six years from acceptance of each tranche", no renewal beyond a 6-month option at Microsoft's election. Named deployments: SINES Portugal 12,600 GB300 (building 1, active) + >66,000 Vera Rubin NVL72 from late-2027 (SIN02, 200 MW owned); Kvandal North Norway VR200 (3 tranches; the SOW's own example puts Tranche 2 delivery at 2027-09-28); Texas (200 MW leased, NVIDIA-guaranteed) and UK per press. *"In April 2026, OpenAI withdrew from the Stargate Norway and Stargate UK partnerships, with Microsoft subsequently expanding its agreement with us to take the extra compute capacity at Stargate Norway."*** ➤ **Two capacity-discipline datapoints now sit side by side on this page: Microsoft RELEASED Monarch (08-27 block below) and ABSORBED OpenAI's Norway capacity — it is optimising sites, not shrinking the neocloud lane.**
- **Contract mechanics for the Azure lease/capex debate: Microsoft pays an upfront amount at signing and a second 90 days before each delivery (Nscale's deferred revenue is $6.5bn at Jun-30-2026 against $137m of drawn debt), gets 100% daily-bill delay credits, may terminate a tranche >60 days late, and holds step-in rights over the operating SPV on insolvency; NVIDIA-caused delays extend the term to preserve a 6-year weighted duration rather than cut the contract value.** **Per GW: Nscale's pre-August book (Microsoft-heavy Blackwell, $51.3bn / 5.5 yrs / 831 MW IT) prices at ~$11.2bn per IT-GW per year (~$11.8bn on the contracted-only part) — consistent with the ~$12M/MW/yr SemiAnalysis quoted for Microsoft's own IaaS to OpenAI (08-07 block) and with [[NBIS]]'s $12M/MW 2026 base. Microsoft buys wholesale at roughly half the merchant 1-3 yr term rate. Concentration on the other side: Nscale's largest customer was 52% of 1H26 revenue (not named).**
**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-27 — MICROSOFT SIGNED A MARCH LOI ON A 1.35GW WEST VIRGINIA CAMPUS AND WALKED AWAY BY SUMMER. [[ANTHROPIC]] STEPPED IN AT $45BN OVER SIX YEARS. THIS IS THE SHARPEST CAPACITY-DISCIPLINE DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE THIS QUARTER.**
- **The facts: Anthropic *"has agreed to pay **Nscale $45bn over six years** to rent **460MW** of AI compute (on **[[NVDA]] Vera Rubin** chips, **online late next year**) at Nscale's **Monarch campus in West Virginia**, the first of three buildings totalling about **1.35GW**. 🔴 **The site was ORIGINALLY RESERVED BY MICROSOFT UNDER A MARCH LETTER OF INTENT, but Microsoft WALKED AWAY THIS SUMMER and Anthropic stepped in.**"* The campus carries **~$71bn of capex (~$47bn of it chips)** and *"will run **OFF-GRID on 2GW of on-site NATURAL GAS plus batteries**"*; for Nscale, *"landing Anthropic as anchor tenant is essential ahead of its own **IPO as soon as next month**"* (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27).
- ➤ 🔴 **THREE READINGS, ALL RELEVANT AND NOT MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE: (1) **capacity discipline** — Microsoft declined a ~1.35GW off-grid gas-powered site at a price a competitor accepted; (2) **lease-vs-build preference** — it may reflect Microsoft's stated shift in how it takes third-party capacity rather than a demand view; (3) **a deep secondary market** — a walked-away AI site was re-tenanted within months at $45bn, which is itself a bullish datapoint on the scarcity of powered shell. **This wiki has no Microsoft statement on the decision; all three readings are inferences and none is adopted.**
- ⚠️ **AND THE COMPETITIVE STING: the frontier lab that took the site is the one whose models Microsoft does not host, and which [[OPENAI]]'s own CEO now concedes has *"**overtaken it on revenue, over $65bn versus roughly $40bn**, and private valuation, and is **likely to IPO first**"* (Time feature, via Rothschild & Co, 2026-08-27).**
- **CAPEX AND THE FCF QUESTION: SIG has tracked 2026 industry capex **>$1T (+~111% y/y)**, with **~$815bn from the top five hyperscalers (+98% y/y)** — and ⚠️ **expects *"hyperscaler FCF to be NEGATIVE in 2027 AND 2028 and only modestly positive in 2029"*** (SIG · Rolland, 2026-08-26), a second house corroborating the 08-25 MS Castagno frame. [[NVDA]]'s own aggregate from its call: **top-five hyperscaler capex ~$800bn in 2026 → $1.3tn in 2027** against a **cloud industry backlog >$2 TRILLION.** See [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).**
- ⚠️ **A DEPLOYMENT GAP WORTH WATCHING, MEASURED: SIG's cloud-instance tracker finds **Azure has the highest Hopper concentration (~45% of regions for H100, ~50% for H200)** but — *"we have yet to find an **AVAILABLE BLACKWELL INSTANCE at Azure**"* — against **AWS B200 in ~16% of regions and GCP B200 in ~24%** (SIG · Rolland, 2026-08-26). ➤ **If accurate, Azure is a generation behind its two largest rivals on publicly available Blackwell capacity. That is a falsifiable claim and a direct read on where Microsoft's GPU allocation is going.**
- ✅ **ON THE PRODUCT SIDE: [[NVDA]] *"partnered with Microsoft to reinvent the Windows PC with **NVIDIA RTX Spark** — a **1-petaflop superchip** with the full CUDA and NVIDIA RTX ecosystem"*, plus **NVIDIA DGX Station for Windows** (NVDA product announcements, 2026-08-26). Vera Rubin racks are running at **Microsoft Azure** among the named launch partners.**
- **AND A POSSIBLE CHINESE OPEN-WEIGHT MODEL ON AZURE: *"Moonshot is negotiating revenue-sharing agreements with **Microsoft**, [[AMZN]] and [[GOOG]] to host its **Kimi K3** model… seeking **up to a 30% share** of K3-related services revenue"* — which *"would be **the FIRST big revenue-sharing pact between a Chinese AI firm and a major US cloud provider**"*, advancing despite US officials *"accusing it of distilling [[ANTHROPIC]]'s Fable and illegally acquiring Nvidia chips"* and a Treasury threat to blacklist it. **Talks early; Alibaba reportedly pursuing similar arrangements** (Reuters, via Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27).**
- **[[CRM]]'s Claudeforce puts **Claude inside Slack** as well as Salesforce (2026-08-26) — a competitive datapoint for Teams/Copilot in the enterprise-agent layer.**

**🆕🔴 2026-08-25 — MORGAN STANLEY NAMES MSFT AS "THE CLEAREST ACCOUNTING EXAMPLE" OF WHY REPORTED FCF NO LONGER MEASURES AI INVESTMENT INTENSITY.** *"**MSFT is the clearest accounting example: extending data-center useful lives from 15 to 25 years causes more 15-year leases to be classified as OPERATING rather than FINANCE leases. Because MSFT's FCF definition EXCLUDES operating leases, reported capex FALLS and FCF RISES without a corresponding change in the underlying economics.**"* The broader argument: hyperscalers will spend **>$1.2tn of cash capex in 2027 against ~$1tn of aggregate operating cash flow**; **hyperscalers alone carry >$2.7tn of undiscounted off-balance-sheet commitments — about three years of current operating cash flow** — and *"investors should economically treat many of these structures like debt-financed capex"* (MS · Todd Castagno, GVAT, via MS TMT · Thomas Wigg, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **A characterisation of an accounting-policy consequence, not an allegation of impropriety — and directly checkable against MSFT's own lease and useful-life disclosures, which this page should now do.**

**🆕 2026-08-25 — THE ROI EVIDENCE CUTS THE OTHER WAY.** JPM's semis team cites **expanding cloud operating margins at AWS, GCP and "MSFT IC"** as the key new evidence that the capex cycle is being validated rather than just extended, alongside **consensus C26 CSP capex of $950bn (+94% y/y) and C27 of $1.4tn (+47%)** (JPM · Harlan Sur, 2026-08-25).

**🆕 2026-08-25 — TWO SILICON DATAPOINTS.** **(1) Maia:** *"recent checks suggesting potential for **300K Maia 300 units in C27, representing a ~$1.5B revenue opportunity**"* for [[MRVL]] (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-25). **(2) A cautionary comparison from SemiAnalysis, made while praising [[OPENAI]]'s first-generation ASIC:** *"**[[META]] and Microsoft's AI ASIC programs not getting off the ground despite being at it for much longer** shows that cost is only one part of the equation"* (SemiAnalysis, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **An analyst's characterisation, no programme detail given.** **Also: Hatch, META's forthcoming consumer agent, "is being trained to operate across third-party services including… MSFT Outlook"** (The Information via MS TMT, 2026-08-25). **MSFT +1% on 08-25, "hanging in there better than the other hyperscalers."**

**NEW / BEAR 2026-08-18 — USAGE PRICING CAN BREAK THE THOUGHTLESS BUNDLE.** Ben Thompson uses Microsoft's ~$100/user E7-plus-usage structure to identify a new bear mechanism: a fixed per-seat license disappears into loaded headcount cost, but a variable monthly token bill forces customers to re-open the budget, inspect each component and consider point solutions. The strategy remains rational — an IBM-in-the-1990s middleware layer that manages changing models for enterprises — but it is also existential because AI is unusually good at migrating systems of record and can replace the document/email/UI artifacts on which Microsoft's interaction moat rests (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — THE MAIA 300 NUMBERS, AND THE UNFLATTERING COMPARISON THAT CAME WITH THEM: Microsoft is chasing 300k+ units for 2027 and "ultimately wants 1mn+", while [[GOOG]] targets 12–15 million TPUs by 2028 and [[AMZN]] already runs ~1 million Trainium2. On custom silicon Microsoft is "a distant third."**
- **The report, as relayed:** Microsoft plans to unveil **Maia 300 as early as September**, is in talks with [[TSM]] to secure capacity for **more than 300,000 units for 2027 delivery** — *"an order of magnitude higher than the tens of thousands for Maia 200"* — and *"ultimately wants to secure capacity of **1mn+ Maia 300 chips**"*, but *"**may be constrained by component suppliers and its ongoing capacity negotiations with TSM**"* (The Information / Aaron Holmes · Qianer Liu, via Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11). **Microsoft is also "courting customers like [[ANTHROPIC]]."**
- **🔴 The efficiency claim, which is the actual bull case: "Maia 200 already runs 30-40% CHEAPER than Nvidia's latest chips for OpenAI/Microsoft workloads"** (TrendForce, via JPM / Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-11). ⚠️ **A vendor-side claim relayed second-hand — no benchmark, workload definition or TCO basis given. It is the single most important number in the story and the least verified.**
- **🔴⚠️ THE SCALE COMPARISON, and it is the part to carry: "Microsoft's scale still lags badly behind rivals: [[GOOG]] is targeting 12-15 MILLION TPUs BY 2028 (3M+ this year alone), and [[AMZN]] already has ~1 MILLION TRAINIUM2 chips running Anthropic's Claude via Project Rainier — putting Microsoft's custom-silicon ambitions A DISTANT THIRD among the hyperscalers racing to reduce Nvidia dependence"** (TrendForce, via JPM, 2026-08-11). ➜ **Read against MS's own supply-chain forecast of 3.7M TPU units in 2026 and >7M in 2027 (MS / Charlie Chan, 2026-08-11): Google's 2027 TPU volume alone is ~20x Microsoft's entire 2027 Maia 300 target.** ⚠️ **Different sources, different bases (chips vs units vs targets) — do not treat as a like-for-like ratio, but the order of magnitude is not in dispute.**
- **What this means for the [[NVDA]] relationship, stated plainly: on these numbers Maia is a cost-optimisation lever on a slice of internal workloads, not a substitution programme.** The market read the same way — **[[MRVL]] opened +4% on the story and "faded very quickly," because 300k "is roughly in-line with expects with buyside" while "the 1mn # looks more aspirational than anything else"** (Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11).
- **Tape/flows context: MSFT +1.2% on 08-10, leading the Mag 7 with [[AMZN]] +1.3%, as "Hyperscalers have been the clear beneficiary of fund flows as investors grew more comfortable with ROIC following the AMZN/MSFT/GOOGL quarters and Brian Nowak's report + Satya Nadella retweet"** (MS PB content via MS TMT desk, 2026-08-11). **The mirror image: memory stocks continue to be de-grossed.** Also: **MS/Erik Woodring names MSFT and [[ORCL]] the most likely partners behind Everpure's second "top-five hyperscaler" storage win** (MS TMT desk; JPM / Joseph Cardoso leans [[ORCL]]).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-10 — MAIA IS BACK ON THE BOARD, AND THE NAMED TARGET CUSTOMER IS [[ANTHROPIC]] (The Information / Aaron Holmes · Qianer Liu, "Microsoft's Homegrown AI Chip Effort Shows Signs of Life After Slow Start", 2026-08-10).** *"Microsoft plans to **sharply ramp up production of its next-generation Maia AI chips next year**, hoping to persuade **big cloud customers like Anthropic** to use them."*
- **Why this is material beyond the headline:** the wiki's standing ASIC frame (BG2 panel via [NVDA.md](NVDA.md)) had **Microsoft's in-house program explicitly among the "underwhelming"** ones, and MSFT's compute crunch has been sourced to *buying* capacity, not building silicon. A 2027 Maia ramp aimed at an **external** anchor tenant is a different business than an internal cost-down.
- **⚠ What is NOT in the article as received:** volumes, node, packaging partner, HBM configuration, or any Anthropic commitment — the email carried the lede only (paywalled body). **Logged as a pointer to chase, not as a modelled datapoint.** ⚠️ Also note the tension with the wiki's [[ANTHROPIC]] page, where Anthropic's committed silicon is TPU + Trainium — a Maia win would be a third platform, not a switch.
- **Tape reaction, and it named the beneficiary:** *"Information article about **MSFT ramping AI chip spend (MRVL up 4% on this)**"* (BofA TMT desk / Brian Fenske, 2026-08-10). Cross-ref [MRVL.md](MRVL.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu.md](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).

**Q4 FY26 (reported 2026-07-29 AMC) — the print that resolved all three open debates at once; stock +9-10%.** Revenue **$90.0B, +18% y/y (+17% cc)** vs Street $87.62B (3% above); non-GAAP EPS **$4.74, +23%** vs $4.24 (11% above); operating income **$40.6B, +18%** (4% above Street); **GM 67.2%** vs 66.6% cons; **OPM 45.1%** vs 44.5%. Segments: Productivity & Business Processes $37.85B vs $37.62B, Intelligent Cloud $39.3B vs $38.16B, More Personal Computing $12.85B vs $12.71B. Microsoft Cloud **$59.3B, +27%**. Cash: OCF **$55.44B** vs $51.21B; capex $35.8B vs $36.13B est.; deferred revenue $75.71B vs $72.97B. (UBS · Robert Ruple desk, 2026-07-29; GS GIR + JPM · Samik Chatterjee, 2026-07-30)
- **Azure +43% cc**, accelerating from +39-40% — vs consensus +40.2%, UBSe +41% and a buyside bogey of 41-42% (the highest UBS heard was 42%): **~300bps above Street, ~200bps above the buyside**. **F1Q27 guided to ~+45% cc**, roughly 400bps above Street and a 2-point acceleration where investors expected 1. Management: "customer demand continues to exceed available capacity… revenue growth was ahead of expectations driven by **efficiency gains across our CPU and GPU fleet** as well as **process improvements to enable earlier delivery of new capacity**. That additional in-quarter capacity for Azure was **quickly monetized**. Results also benefited from stronger-than-expected **GitHub Copilot consumption** following the June business model change." "Even with the strong close to Q4, we continue to expect **H1 [FY27] growth to accelerate**." **FY26 Azure revenue surpassed $100B for the first time** (Satya). ⚠ The driver list is also the bear case — see the Debate section. (UBS/Ruple, 2026-07-29; TMTB, 2026-07-30)
- **Copilot vs the bar** (the seat marks themselves are in the call bullet below): **>30M paid seats = ~10M net adds against sell-side models looking for 25-28M TOTAL seats and a buyside bogey of ~5M adds** — the second-biggest surprise of the print after Azure. M365 Commercial Cloud **+14% reported** (investors wanted a point more) but **underlying growth 16%** vs Street ~14%; **F1Q27 guided +15% reported / +16% cc**, and "we expect to see acceleration in commercial cloud revenue growth through this fiscal year." (TMTB, 2026-07-30; JPM · Samik Chatterjee, 2026-07-30)
- **Demand cover is now broad, not OpenAI-dependent:** commercial bookings **+18% ex-OpenAI** vs +11% cc including OpenAI — against a **-6% cc decline last quarter** — and **all sequential commercial RPO growth came from customers outside frontier-model companies** (TMTB, 2026-07-30). Backs the FY26 10-K marks already on this page (**commercial RPO $678B, +84%**; datacenter leases not yet commenced **$329.1B**). Rothschild calls the lease line "probably the cleanest forward volume signal: these are signed, but not-yet-commenced leases, so **upstream of the classification change**, i.e. a purer demand read than the capex line," and cites MSFT "on track to **double capacity in 2yrs**" (Rothschild & Co Redburn · Rowan Adley, 2026-07-30).
- **Capex — the sell-side arithmetic on top of the reclassification** (the mechanic itself, in Hood's own words, is in the "THE CAPEX HEADLINE IS AN ACCOUNTING RECLASSIFICATION" call bullet below; TMTB's independent read agrees: ~$15B of spend moved outside reported capex rather than disappearing, so the clean comparison is **~$175B + ~$15B ≈ $190B on the prior basis, i.e. a reiteration** — TMTB, 2026-07-30):
  - Rothschild's apples-to-apples math: **FY27 ≈ $235-245B (+60-65% y/y) on the old basis, ~$205-215B (+40-45%) as reported** — "the **entire headline reduction is classification**, so no evidence of any slowdown in intent." Two tells: MSFT **omitted the word "significantly"** from its FY27 capex-growth phrasing versus prior wording, and the FCF-positive commitment "creates only a **soft ceiling**: it constrains cash PP&E, not capex incl finance leases (which don't touch FCF at commencement), and the shift to operating leases actually loosens it further over time." (Rothschild/Adley, 2026-07-30)
  - JPM's ceiling from the other direction: with FY27 FCF guided positive, if you believe Street FY27 OCF then **FY27 capex can be no larger than ~$215B — "definitely less than investors were thinking in the $250B to $275B range"** (JPM · Mark Schilsky desk, 2026-07-30). Schilsky's forward call: "MSFT won't be the last company to extend the useful lives of its datacenters and/or computing fleet. Mark my words."
  - GS on the ROI arithmetic: the inflation hit is "well understood (**~14% impact to CY26 capex**)" but **internal margin targets do not change even when component pricing is higher**, with monetisation offsets including **~50% faster dock-to-live times for GPUs**, ongoing cost work, and bundling/pricing across GitHub power users, PaaS and CPU services (GS GIR, 2026-07-30).
- **FY27 guardrails:** full-year **operating margin "down less than a point"** ("which really means she aims to keep them flattish" — JPM/Schilsky); **"we expect to remain free cash flow positive in FY27"** — read across the desks as *no equity raise*; F1Q27 implied **OPM ~48.5%** vs Street 47.5-47.6% on lower opex guidance. (TMTB + JPM/Schilsky, 2026-07-30)
- **Monetization model shift:** management is moving to **seat-plus-consumption** pricing across Cowork, GitHub Copilot, Dynamics and eventually more workloads — a recurring subscription floor with revenue scaling on agent activity and token usage; the **June GitHub Copilot usage-based repricing produced stronger-than-expected consumption revenue and improved margins through the quarter** (TMTB, 2026-07-30) — the mechanic UBS's 07-26 field checks had already flagged at 10-20x spend uplift for one large bank.
- **🆕 The transmission chain named end-to-end, with Copilot as the conversion step — the loop this page has been documenting piecewise, stated as one mechanism (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05).** FUNDA's framing of why cloud providers are the direct beneficiaries of the coding/coding-adjacent revenue wave: **"the transmission runs from rising token consumption to rising paid conversion for MICROSOFT COPILOT, AWS Bedrock and Vertex AI, to higher cloud revenue and profit, to further capex increases."** ⚠️ **Note where Copilot sits in that chain and why it matters for this page's own argument: FUNDA puts Copilot at the CONVERSION step, not the consumption step — i.e. the seat count is the monetisation of token growth that originates elsewhere (coding, BI/SQL, financial modelling, process agents), which is a cleaner statement of the >30M-paid-seat surprise than "Copilot is working" and is consistent with management's own move to seat-plus-consumption pricing above.** The coding-adjacent evidence base FUNDA builds it on is worth carrying because it is the demand under the Azure line: Block reports 75% of its engineers use AI agents to write SQL saving 8-10 hours/person/week; Snowflake Cortex Analyst and Databricks Genie have made text-to-SQL a standard warehouse feature; **Claude for Excel launched with adoption at Citi, RBC and Visa** (⚠️ read against the standing bear on this page that Anthropic's Office-suite apps pressure Copilot — the same datapoint is FUNDA's proof of category demand and this page's proof of competitive encroachment; both readings retained); and models now complete more than half the tasks on financial-analysis agent benchmarks autonomously. **NOT re-added here: Azure +43% and the $100B annual-revenue mark, which FUNDA cites as its return support — both are already on this page from the 4QFY26 print (2026-07-29) and the primary wins.** (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html)
- Street day-one: **MS Overweight PT $600** ("Growth Thesis Taking Shape," Adam Wood/Josh Baer); **Jefferies Buy TP $575**; Barclays (Raimo Lenschow) "Azure and Office Acceleration Create Better Outlook — we see investors revisiting MSFT shares post Q4"; JPM (Samik Chatterjee) on the balance of Azure acceleration + FY27 margin moderation <100bps + CY26 capex maintained. TMTB called it a "**Goldilocks print**" and an N&N (numbers & narrative) inflection, with a base case of 25x FY27 $22 = **$550** and upside 25x FY28 $25 = **$625+** vs ~10% downside to the pre-print level.
- **Street day-two — the full research notes, priced (2026-07-30 inbox ingest; the desk/spec-sales flow above was day-one).** Five published notes now on file, and the striking thing is how tight the FY27 EPS cluster is (**$19.40-19.68**) against a **$525-650 PT spread** — the disagreement is entirely multiple, not numbers:
  - **Wells Fargo · US Software — Overweight, PT $625 → $650 (Street high on this page), on higher EPS**, derived from **27.5x** forward-NTM EPS, "justified in our view by unprecedented scale + durability." FY27 EPS **$19.46 → $19.58**, FY28 **$22.79 → $23.53**; FY27 revenue **$388.4B → $393.85B**, FY28 **$468.6B → $481.21B**; FY27 FCF **$4.9B → $9.3B**, FY28 **$9.8B → $12.8B**. Shares at **22x NTMe in AH trading**. Its Exhibit 1 puts the **FY27 cash-capex outlook at a $218-232B range** — the fourth independent apples-to-apples FY27 capex estimate on this page (see the Capex Signal row). Software reads: AI infra (+) on 2x capacity in 2yrs and **Foundry revenue +100% y/y**; **ERP (+) / CRM (−)** across D365 with the callback highlighting the shift "from seats towards consumption"; data (+) on **PostgreSQL accelerating to +55% y/y and paid Fabric customers +60% y/y**. Also flags, from the callback, that the **"FCF positive" guide is "not a line in the sand"** — MSFT "remains committed to spend signals/meeting demand" — and that ongoing **COGS impacts from higher D&A including higher component costs** are now flowing into the model with the FY27 OM guide still <100bps y/y decline. (Wells Fargo "4Q26 Teardown — Incrementals from the Callback & Expanded Thoughts on the Print", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/0780ae6f-bca6-43bf-907f-6523e7fc7a72.html) · [WF model xlsx](../_inbox/_done/MSFT_WFS_7.29.26.xlsx)
  - **UBS · Karl Keirstead — Buy, PT $480 → $525** (prior mark on this page was the 07-26 cut $510 → $480; see `## Changelog`), on **19x CY28E non-GAAP EPS, changed from 21x CY27E** — a roll-forward of the valuation base, not a multiple expansion, "a deserved premium to the peer group." FY27 EPS **$19.26 → $19.68** (cons $19.40), FY28 **$22.12 → $23.17** (cons $22.77), FY29 **$25.56 → $26.94**. Frames the print as "each of the major debate points (Azure, capex, M365, margins) turned out in-line or better" and the stock at **20x CY27E GAAP / 18x non-GAAP**. Two disclosures UBS pulls out that the desk notes did not: **non-GAAP EPS ex one-time items was +16% cc in 4Q/Jun** (vs the +23% headline), and **MSFT disclosed $24B of FY26 revenue from commercial arrangements with [[OPENAI]] alone — company-wide, not just Azure, i.e. OpenAI is a ~7% revenue customer** (first-time 10-K disclosure, which MSFT attributed to a forced equity-method accounting rule). (UBS "Winning Formula — Azure Estimates Up, Capex Reaffirmed", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260730_UBS_MSFT_Microsoft_Corp_-Winning_Formula_-_Azure_Estimates_Up-.html)
  - **JPM · Samik Chatterjee — Overweight, Dec-27 PT $550 established.** FY27 EPS **$18.70 → $19.40** (+3.8%), FY28 **$21.10 → $23.15** (+9.7% — the largest FY28 revision of the five); FY27 revenue $392.2B, FY28 $473.0B; FY27 EBIT margin 46.2%. Note JPM's own **FCFF line goes $67.0B (FY26A) → $24.1B (FY27E) → −$10.9B (FY28E)** — i.e. JPM models FY28 free cash flow *negative* even while guiding to the FY27-positive commitment, which is the sharper version of Rothschild's "soft ceiling" point. Total capex incl. finance leases **tracked to $41B, modestly BELOW consensus $42B** — "pleasantly surprising investors." (JPM "F4Q26 Review: Finding the Right Balance", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260730_JP_Morgan_MSFT_Microsoft-_F4Q26_Review-_Finding_the_Right_Balance_in_Gr.html)
  - **Morgan Stanley · Adam Wood / Josh Baer — Overweight, PT $600** = **1.2x PEG and 25x P/E on base-case FY28e EPS $23.86**; ModelWare FY27e $19.67 / FY28e $24.07. The valuation anchor is the cheapness claim: **17.7x FY28 GAAP EPS**. OI **$40.60B beat consensus by 4.2%** on revenue 2.6% above ($2.2B above the high end of the $86.7-87.8B guide); segments PBP $37.85B (+1.5% vs cons), Intelligent Cloud $39.31B (+2.8% vs cons, $1.06B above the high end). Frames F4Q as "the first proof point across our Azure, Copilot and Margin thesis" — moving the thesis "from expectation to evidence." (Morgan Stanley "4Q26 Results — Growth Thesis Taking Shape", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260730_Morgan_Stanley_MSFT_Microsoft-_4Q26_Results_-_Growth_Thesis_Taking_Shape.html)
  - **Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick — Buy, PT $550 maintained**, on **~25x** updated CY27E EPS. Net-new vs the day-one DB row below: **FY27 GM estimate eases only ~10bps to 65.8%** despite guidance for sharper FY27 declines in high-margin **Windows (−high-teens % y/y), M365 Commercial Products (−MSD %) and Server Products (−MSD %)** — the mix-shift arithmetic behind the "margins down <1pt" guide, and the specific lines the FY27 bear case has to lean on. On Azure: the ~+45% cc F1Q guide **implies ~$2.8bn of net adds, +65% y/y**. On RPO: **weighted-average duration fell to 2.3yrs from 2.5yrs** with 30% recognisable in the next 12 months (+37% y/y) — a shortening-duration read on a growing backlog. Notes management "framed its success and various growth metrics **ex the contribution from frontier model companies**." (Deutsche Bank "F4Q — Harnessing the Power of AI", 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260730_Deutsche_Bank_MSFT_Microsoft-_F4Q_Harnessing_the_Power_of_AI.html)
- **The IR callbacks (2026-07-30) — where the incremental actually was, and it is not Azure.** Two hosted callbacks with Microsoft IR (**UBS/Keirstead with Rob Ruple, IR "Jonathan"**; **BofA**) produced the material new information of the day, most of it on the two questions this page has been carrying open:
  - **★ PRICING IS NOT LOCKED FOR THE LIFE OF LONG-TERM AZURE CONTRACTS — this retires a standing bear case on this page.** Keirstead put the fixed-price-contract-versus-hardware-inflation margin thesis directly to IR and "**he pushed back, arguing primarily that pricing is not locked in over the duration of long-term Azure contracts.**" The mechanics: **for frontier-lab deals like [[OPENAI]], pricing is locked only BY CAPACITY TRANCHE — "when the next chunk of capacity comes online, that is priced separately," so "there can absolutely be a pricing step-up as each tranche goes live over the course of a frontier lab commit"**; and for a normal enterprise deal "**the discount off list can alter throughout the contract.**" Combined with MSFT having "**secured hardware pricing via long-term deals**" (so it is "not as exposed to spot increases as one might think"), the conclusion is that MSFT has "**a lot more wiggle room than you might think to protect gross margins**." Keirstead adds, flagged as his words not IR's, that this extends to **"potentially raise pricing over contracts"** — and that he assumes the same tranche construct is **an industry norm rather than MSFT-specific, so it likely applies to [[ORCL]] too**. ⚠️ This is the direct rebuttal to the Redburn ~12-13% Gen-AI-margin thesis and to the memory-inflation-eats-AI-gross-margin framing carried on this page; log it as a *company/IR assertion*, not an audited fact — the falsifiable test is whether Azure gross margin holds as the FY27 tranches go live. (UBS callback, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_callback_2q26_msft_ubs.html)
  - **★ RPO visibility is now unprecedented, and BofA did the arithmetic nobody else did: RPO grew +$51B q/q to ~$680B; 30% converts to revenue in the next 12 months, UP FROM 25% last quarter; weighted-average duration 2.3 years. Run it through and the next 12 months of revenue are ~57% covered by signed RPO, and the following 15 months ~88% covered — "they've never had this kind of visibility."** The second-order point is the one that matters for the beat-and-raise setup: "if 88% of revenues is explained by current RPOs, every time you grow RPO, or you have better-than-expected numbers, that flows straight to a positive revenue surprise versus the forecasts." (BofA callback, 2026-07-30) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_30_msft_2q26_callback_bofa.html)
  - **Capex, the third apples-to-apples estimate — and it lands at the pre-print bogey, i.e. no change.** UBS's build: the $190B old-basis CY26 guide implies a **low-$60B December quarter**; grow that sequentially through March/June and **FY27 ex-accounting-change capex lands at ~$250-260B, "exactly where most of the investors I was talking to were modeling into the print"** — so "on an unadjusted basis, buy-side capex estimates actually do not change very much at all." Cross-check from the FCF side: UBS models **FY27 OCF $215B**, so FCF "modestly positive" implies **cash capex ~$200-210B vs UBS's own $209B pre-print estimate** — again no change. IR would not comment beyond CY26 except to say the "FY27 capex will grow" language "**was not intended to convey any material fall-off in capex post calendar-26**," and UBS reads the **material spike in total lease obligations disclosed in the 10-K** as a signal of continued high capex requirements *beyond* FY27. On why memory inflation did not move the guide: MSFT "**already contemplated this inflation in its original capex guide**" and "has **long-term hardware contracts that partly insulate it from spot memory increases**." Keirstead's balancing caveat is worth keeping: the fact the guide did not need raising again three months after March's material raise "**might have more to do with the fact that Microsoft is guiding appropriately than it does with any underlying market shifts**" — IR conveyed that Hood "still feels a bit burned from having to revise her capex guidance up in the past, such that she set it three months ago at an appropriately high level. **That may be, frankly, the whole story.**" **BofA independently models FY27 capex +52% y/y** and explains the reclassification cleanly: with facility useful life at 25 years, a 15-year lease is only ~66% of useful life — no longer "substantially all" — so it fails capitalisation and must be expensed; **net free-cash-flow impact is zero (one account cancels the other), the capex reduction is purely optical.** (UBS + BofA callbacks, 2026-07-30)
  - **GitHub Copilot, quantified: revenue +60% q/q** following the June 1 shift to usage-based pricing (MSFT disclosure) — against UBS field work that had seen a **10-20x spending increase** post-change. UBS calculates **the pricing change alone could be worth over a full point of Azure upside in the September quarter**, which is a mix caveat on the ~45% guide: part of the acceleration is a repricing, not new workload.
  - **★ Enterprises are already shifting from token-maxxing to token-*optimizing*, and Microsoft was surprised by the speed.** IR "was clear that Microsoft customers are indeed changing their behavior… **internally at Microsoft, they've been surprised at how quickly their enterprise customers have shifted from token-maxxing to token-optimizing**" — but that "any drag on Azure growth as a result of token spend optimization was offset by other tailwinds." Note this is the *company* confirming the behaviour shift that MS's 07-27 thematic argues against on the data (see [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md)); the two are reconcilable — per-seat spend can optimise while aggregate spend still compounds — but the direction of travel is now management-confirmed, not just a bear talking point.
  - **Quality-of-growth caveat on the whole hyperscaler cohort (Keirstead's own words, not IR's):** Azure and its peers are "**benefiting materially from their role as being front-end procurement engines for [[OPENAI]] and [[ANTHROPIC]] model access and 1P products**, as Azure and its hyperscaler peers, as well as Databricks and others, **rev-rec those transactions**" — i.e. some of the reported cloud acceleration is distribution/pass-through revenue rather than owned compute. He suspects it helped Azure in the quarter even though MSFT did not call it out. Directly relevant to the AWS/Bedrock rev-rec mechanic already documented on [AMZN](AMZN.md).
  - **Capacity cadence: this was the THIRD SUCCESSIVE quarter Microsoft added a full gigawatt** ([[ORCL]] also added a full GW in its May quarter), and Keirstead's rule of thumb is that **each new gigawatt adds ~$10-15B of revenue** — which is how he gets comfortable with the acceleration being physical rather than optical. He reads the willingness to guide Azure up in September and 1H as evidence of "**good visibility into the addition of warm shells or raw capacity going live**," and says the 1GW/quarter pace "**undermines a lot of the media stories about AI data center go-live bottlenecks**… the AI infra industry is powering through those bottlenecks."
  - **Azure December quarter — the reaffirmation is weaker than it reads.** IR "**did point out that Azure growth could even decelerate sequentially in the December quarter, and their guide for a second-half acceleration relative to the first half would still hold**," and "admitted that the reaffirm of the 2H/1H guide was not really all that helpful." UBS models December **flat at ~45%** "just because the sequential numbers get very, very large." Nothing in the callback suggested a deceleration is coming — but the guardrail is looser than the day-one flow implies.
  - **M365 was the quiet mixed number, not a clean positive:** F4Q M365 commercial cloud **+14% came in only at the high end of the guide and was "frankly a bit disappointing," since MSFT typically beats its M365 guide**; **seat growth +6%** (a point above UBS, and no evidence of seat compression), so the shortfall was **weaker ARPU**. The bull offset is forward: **+15% guided for September and accelerating through FY27** on Copilot seats, **co-work and E7 traction that landed too late in the June quarter to show up in revenue**, plus multiple M365 price increases layering in (which UBS notes management "did not want to bring up").
  - **Margin tailwinds, itemised:** headcount **−2% in the quarter**; **first quarter with the MSFT→OpenAI revenue share removed** (a direct COGS tailwind); a modest OM tailwind from the lease reclassification plus the longer facility useful life (IR: the operating leases land across other income and COGS, and net of the useful-life elongation it is "**a net modest tailwind to EBIT margins**"); and the GitHub Copilot repricing helping Azure margins specifically.
  - **Product scale marks (BofA callback):** **Foundry 10,000 customers with revenue more than doubled y/y**; **Fabric 40,000 customers, +60% y/y**; **PostgreSQL revenue +55%, accelerating for a third consecutive quarter**; **Maia 200 claimed at ~40% better performance per watt** (complements — does not replace — the ~30% better performance-per-dollar mark management gave on the call); MSFT "**among the first clouds to deploy next-generation AMD and NVIDIA architectures, including Helios and Vera Rubin**"; **5x YTD increase in customers building with multiple model providers**; and a **third-party sighting worth watching — Satya has said MSFT would "potentially look at DeepSeek" for the Foundry catalog** in future.
  - Read-throughs the callbacks volunteered: **positive for Datadog and Snowflake** (broad-based core/CPU enterprise Azure demand); **negative for [[CRM]]** — D365 "bookings growth in the Dynamics ERP side remains healthy, but bookings growth on the Dynamics CRM side continued to see longer sales cycles," which UBS says "is obviously not a great read to Salesforce and is consistent with Taylor's relatively muted HubSpot checks." Unverified third-party datapoint carried for the record only: **CNBC reported OpenAI's July ARR exceeded the entire sum of its June ARR** — Keirstead's own reaction was "that seems too good to be true. I'm not sure whether that's accurate."

**Q3 FY26 (reported 2026-04-29):** Revenue $82.9B, +18% (+15% cc); EPS $4.27, +21% y/y (ex-OpenAI impact); operating margin 46%; company GM 68%, down y/y on AI infra. Microsoft Cloud $54.5B, +29% (GM 66%). Vs. Street: adjusted EPS **$4.27 vs $4.05 est.**, revenue **$82.9B vs $81.4B est.** (Barron's "Up & Down Wall Street"/Andy Serwer, 2026-05-18).
- **Azure +40% cc**, ahead of consensus, capacity delivered earlier than planned (Fairwater Wisconsin online 6 weeks early). Q4 FY26 guide: Azure +39–40% cc, with management expecting **modest acceleration in 2H CY26** vs 1H; supply expected to remain **constrained at least through 2026** (Q3 FY26). JPM corroborates the supply backdrop — MSFT "expects to be capacity-constrained through at least the end of the fiscal year," demand "significantly ahead of capacity" (JPM AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10).
- **Azure AI economics:** MS estimates Azure AI revenue $18.2B/$30.5B in CY25/CY26, with OpenAI-related mix climbing from ~27% of Azure AI revenue (3Q24) toward ~62% (4Q26e); on MS math, OpenAI explains 100%+ of CY26 Azure AI growth, implying upside to the Street's ~27.5% CY26 Azure growth est. if non-OpenAI ("Other") rev holds (MS MSFT/OpenAI, 2025-05-21). UBS: the early-2025 renegotiation gives MSFT a ROFR on new OpenAI capacity, but there's "no evidence it's been exercised" as OpenAI signed $100B+ with ORCL/CRWV/Google — ROFR "may not have teeth" in practice (UBS, 2025-09-02).
- **M365 Copilot:** record seat adds, +250% y/y; **>20M paid seats** (vs 15M in Q2 FY26, 2026-01-28); customers >50k seats quadrupled y/y; Accenture >740k seats. Weekly engagement now "at the same level as Outlook" (Satya, Q3 FY26). Broader Copilot umbrella (Sales/Finance/Service/Security + GitHub) >100M MAU (SemiAnalysis MSFT AI Strategy, 2025-11-12).
- **Capex $31.9B** in Q3 (down sequentially from $37.5B in Q2 FY26); ~2/3 short-lived assets (GPU/CPU). **CY26 capex guided ~$190B, of which ~$25B is higher component pricing (memory)**; Q4 capex steps to >$40B (Q3 FY26).
- **OpenAI:** ~45% of $625B commercial RPO was OpenAI-related as of Q2 FY26; ex-OpenAI RPO grew 28%.
