- **BACKFILL / dated Sep-2025 benchmark — "Azure's AI outperformance over peers is entirely driven by its OpenAI partnership" (SemiAnalysis "Amazon's AI Resurgence: AWS & Anthropic's Multi-Gigawatt Trainium Expansion", 2025-09-03).** _Historical/structural context — an ~11-month-old datapoint, NOT a current read; it supersedes none of the CY26 Azure/capex figures above._ The exact framing: "No firm better illustrates this than Microsoft. **Azure's AI outperformance over peers is entirely driven by its OpenAI partnership. As of Q2 2025 (June 2025), all of OpenAI's >$10B cloud spending is booked by Azure**" (and, for scale, "as of Q2 2025, **Anthropic's cloud spending is over 2x smaller than that of OpenAI**"). Useful now precisely because the world has moved: it is a **clean dated baseline of 100% Azure capture of OpenAI cloud spend**, against which today's multi-sourcing (ORCL ~$300bn, AWS, GCP, CoreWeave — see the OpenAI compute table on [[OPENAI]]) and the API-exclusivity loss can be measured. Independent third-party support for the Azure-AI-growth-attribution debate already framed on this page by UBS (2025-09-02, ~half of Azure's cc growth AI, "substantially all" OpenAI-tied) and MS (2025-05-21, OpenAI = 49% of CY25 and 100%+ of CY26 Azure AI growth). (SemiAnalysis, 2025-09-03) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2025_09_03_ENC_Amazons_AI_Resurgence_AWS_Anthropics_Multi-Gigawatt_Trainium_Expansion_email.html)
- **Custom silicon / models — the structural weak spot:** SemiAnalysis ranks MSFT "dead last" among hyperscalers on custom ASICs — Maia 200 assessed an internal *failure* (MSFT halted Maia 200 software work), with the 2nm Maia 300 not deployable near internal targets until late 2027 vs Nvidia Vera Rubin; CoWoS bookings far below GOOG/AMZN/Meta (SemiAnalysis, 2025-11-12). On models, MAI-1 (~#38 LMArena, trained on 15k H100s) is not at the frontier; MSFT's explicit strategy is to under-invest in MAI given OpenAI IP access to 2032/AGI — capital-efficient near-term but risks not having a top-5 lab post-2032 (SemiAnalysis, 2025-11-12). The edge MSFT does have: distilling/post-training OpenAI models on proprietary Office data (e.g. the Excel Agent is a post-trained OpenAI reasoning model MSFT claims beats frontier labs), though MSFT loses access to OpenAI's *research* IP/"systems" at AGI or 2030 (SemiAnalysis, 2025-11-12). One sizing datapoint for the Maia program's design spend: SemiAnalysis's EDA primer names **Microsoft among the hyperscalers "each run[ning] multiple custom silicon programs with full EDA tool stacks at advanced nodes"** (Google TPU / Amazon Trainium / Microsoft Maia / Meta MTIA), and puts hyperscaler custom silicon at **$300-500M+ annually in EDA spend that is uncorrelated with the semiconductor cycle** and never paused during downturns — with systems companies now **~45% of EDA demand** (SemiAnalysis "EDA Market Primer", 2026-05-21). Note the primer sizes the *category*, not Maia specifically, so it is a floor-setting marker for the ongoing commitment, not evidence the program is working — the "dead last / Maia 200 a failure" read above is unchanged.
- **Where the sell-side/buy-side stands:** Skews positive. Jefferies (Thill) **Buy $575** reaffirmed (2026-06-06); MS **OW, PT $600** — **Adam Wood ASSUMED coverage on 2026-07-21, lowering the PT to $600 from $650** ("Battle of the AI Stack": Azure + Copilot both set to inflect — Azure growth acceleration in C2H26, durable >40% YoY, 5%/8% above consensus FY28/FY29; lowered gross-margin ests offset by operating leverage → >20% EBIT/EPS growth; 16x FY28 GAAP EPS "too cheap"; 1.2x PEG / 25x PE → ~50% upside to the new $600 PT). The **prior MS $650** (Weiss 06-01; reiterated in MS's 2026-07-12 cross-hyperscaler capex note (Nowak et al.) with base $650 / bull $860 / bear $310) is **superseded and moved to the Changelog** (2026-07-23); an earlier MS MSFT/OpenAI deep-dive carried **OW $482** (2025-05-21); DB (Zelnick) **Buy $550** post-Build (2026-06-03); Barclays (Lenschow) **OW $545** (2026-05-07/06-03). **UBS (Keirstead) Buy, PT $510 → $480** (2026-07-26; the $510 mark carried from 07-25 is superseded and moved to the Changelog) — 21x CY27 non-GAAP (prior 22x), FY27 capex raised $234B → $261B, FY27 M365 Commercial Cloud growth +70bps to 16.2%. Outliers cautious: Redburn/R&Co (Adley) **Neutral**, citing active+passive selling and Russell growth-to-value reclass (2026-05-26) — now with an explicit **PT $400** (px $401.10) in Rothschild & Co Redburn's "Hyperscalers — Margin Call" (Haissl/Han, 2026-07-20): Intelligent Cloud GM has fallen 680bp since the Dec-24 qtr (op margin only 280bp) so the Gen-AI book backs out to ~13% margin, "well below" the assumed 30% floor, and RDBN EPS FY26/27/28 $17.37/$18.66/$20.93 sits below consensus — "furthest along; the compression is visible and partly in the price." **On the credit side, JPM (Anmuth/Chatterjee/Speer, 2026-07-20) downgraded MSFT credit to underweight**, expecting MSFT to be the "next up" hyperscaler to tap the debt market for the first time in ~a decade (CY26 $190bn capex → ~$250bn FY27 run-rate). Buy-side counterweight: **Octahedron Capital built a new 5-10% MSFT position** (2Q26 LP call, 2026-07-15), calling it the "cheapest multiple I've seen in history" (~16.5x CY28 on their numbers vs ~17.8x consensus). Stock at 12-month relative lows alongside CRM/ADBE/INTU/ADSK (Rothschild, 2026-06-18). **GS (Borges) reiterates a positive risk/reward into the 7/29 4QFY print** — MSFT "best-positioned in coverage to compound AI product cycles" — expecting 4QFY Azure 40-41% cc (above the 39-40% guide), 1QFY guide 40-41% vs Street 41%, and raising FY28-30 capex ests; frames the stock's -5%-since-3Q-EPS underperformance (vs Nasdaq +5%) as three bear concerns (capex-up-without-Azure-revision, NVIDIA/memory exposure, Office 365 disintermediation from Claude Cowork with Copilot perceived to lag) that the print can neutralize (GS "Four Key Themes into 4QFY EPS", 2026-07-08). **🆕 The current JPM equity mark, which this line did not carry: JPM (Samik Chatterjee) is Overweight with a Dec-27 PT of $625 on 24x CY28 EPS $26.05 (2026-08-13) — the prior $550 is superseded and sits in the `## Changelog`. Note the split within the same house: JPM equity research is bullish on the mix / lower-external-capital argument (PT below WF's $650, Tigress's $680 and Bernstein's $646 marks above) while JPM credit is underweight on the expected debt debut (Anmuth/Chatterjee/Speer, 2026-07-20) — and Chatterjee is an author on both.**
- **Bear, quantified — the incremental cloud dollar and the cache-adjusted rebuttal to cheap open-weight models (R&Co Redburn · Alex Haissl, "Margin Call" roadshow feedback via Rowan Joseph's "Redburn 5," 2026-07-26):** five days of North American marketing off the note that already gave this page its **Neutral / PT $400** mark (07-20). Two numbers are new and both are stack-level, not MSFT-specific, which is why they matter: (1) **the Cloud 1.0 book earned ~66% gross margin; the all-in incremental cloud dollar today earns only ~40%; and once the traditional book is stripped out the pure Gen-AI slice backs out to just ~12-13%** — "well below the 30% floor investors treat as trough," with reported operating margins "resilient only because they're being propped up by aggressive cost cuts, not by improving AI economics." That is the same ~13% figure already on this page from the 07-20 note, now placed inside a three-tier ladder (66% → 40% → 12-13%) that makes the mix arithmetic legible. (2) The MSFT-specific sensitivity: **for every 100bps of mix shift toward Azure's Gen-AI, Microsoft's cloud gross margin falls 77bps** — the cleanest published elasticity on this page for the guided GM compression. Haissl's framing of the market's contract: **"we'll fund the capex so long as margins hold,"** with Alphabet's 07-23 sell-off on a capex step-up despite a revenue beat cited as the validation. **The counterweight Redburn itself supplies, and it cuts against the token-cost bears (including some already on this page):** on open-weight vs frontier, "this is about cost-per-task, not the sticker price of tokens" — in Haissl's own **Claude Code usage, stored-cache reads were ~98% of tokens with fresh input under 2%**, and open-weight models "repeat failed tasks such as trying to go to a blocked website multiple times, while frontier models don't," so **open-weight models are "only 2x cheaper when you adjust for cache,"** not the 10-40x the headline all-in benchmark costs imply (cf. the JPM Artificial-Analysis table now on [[ANTHROPIC]]). Logged as a genuine two-sided item: the same house is the most bearish mark on MSFT's cloud margin *and* the most dismissive of the token-substitution threat to the frontier labs MSFT resells. On stocks: **Neutral AMZN, Neutral MSFT, Sell ORCL**; "too early to buy," and the trigger is explicit — **"when capex guidance gets cut."** (R&Co Redburn, 2026-07-26)
- **MS answers the ROIC bear head-on — three bottom-up frameworks showing 25-50% incremental GenAI ROIC, and Azure is the one MS underwrites directly (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", Brian Nowak CFA / Stephen C Byrd / Adam Wood, 2026-07-27; Industry View Attractive):** the first attempt on this page to answer the ROIC question with **unit economics rather than sentiment**, and it lands two days before the print. Framing: "AI capex and model training spend are surging with investors questioning ROIC. We are bullish these investments" — built on MS's own 12-Jul note ($1.4trn+ of hyperscaler capex; compute capacity **quadrupling from '25 to '28 to ~120 GW**, both already on this page). All three frameworks are standardised on **1GW of NVDA GB300: 410,256 chips, $39bn of all-in capex/GW ($23bn IT on a 5-yr life + $16bn non-IT on a 15-yr life, at ~$4mn per GB300 rack per MS's Asia semis team's published rack BoM) → ~$6bn/yr of depreciation, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW of total opex (IT dep $5bn + non-IT dep $1bn + energy/other $2bn), 21% tax.** **(1) Hyperscaler GPU rental (IaaS) — the Azure-relevant one:** base case **$8.50/hr → revenue $22.9bn/GW, incremental EBIT $15.3bn (~67% incremental EBIT margin), NOPAT $12.1bn, ~31% ROIC**, with the $7.00-$10.00/hr scenario band spanning **23% → 39% ROIC** and **60% → 72%** incremental margins. MS's stated conclusion: ~60-70% incremental EBIT margins, 25-40% ROIC, and "we remain bullish **AWS, GCP and Azure**." **(2) Model-enabled API on owned infrastructure** (65% of capacity to inference, 2,750 tokens/sec/GPU, $1.75/mn blended tokens) → **$30bn/GW of revenue, $22.7bn EBIT (~75% margin), $17.9bn NOPAT, ~46% ROIC** (band 19% → 63%). **(3) The same API on 3P/rented infrastructure** at **$7.75/hr** → **$40.5bn/GW revenue less ~$28bn of compute cost = $12.6bn EBIT (~31% margin), $10.0bn NOPAT, ~25% NOPAT margin** (band -9% → 32%) — i.e. MS explicitly prices the "middle-man margin" the hyperscaler keeps, which is the other side of Azure's rent-and-resell exposure already on this page. ⚠️ **Carry MS's own caveat, because it is the bear's foothold and it maps exactly onto the Redburn short:** "while we believe cloud providers could see 60-70% incremental EBIT margins on GB300 inference capacity, **reported near-term incremental margins will remain below that range**" because of (a) near-term mix skewed to prior chip generations while GB300 capacity is still scaling, (b) traditional cloud workloads mixed in with AI-specific ones, and (c) opex not captured in the analysis (headcount, R&D). So the 67% is a **steady-state GB300-only** number, not something that shows up in the guided 66.2% 4Q/Jun GM or the FY27 65.5% mark — and Redburn's ~12-13% pure-Gen-AI margin and MS's ~67% incremental EBIT margin are **not measuring the same thing**, which is worth stating plainly rather than treating as a live disagreement. Methodology worth carrying: the **2,000-3,500 tokens/sec/GPU** range is **not a fixed hardware property** — it is a DeepSeek-V4-Pro 1.6T, FP4, 8K-input/1K-output workload at ~50 tokens/sec/user interactivity, and "GB300 can deliver materially higher throughput than GB200 at matched interactivity, but the precise result varies across software configurations"; larger models (2.8T Kimi-K3) price higher but throughput lower, so **higher pricing does not translate proportionately into higher revenue/GW**; and training cost is handled by **discounting the share of compute available for revenue-generating inference while still bearing all the costs**. MS closes with the competitive corollary the bulls should not skip: these returns are "an invitation for competition… further new entrants/open source offerings," which keeps the premium on product + compute-efficiency innovation and on **the value of compute infrastructure assets**. (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- **The refreshed MS risk-reward page for MSFT — scenario set rebased under the $600 PT, and the spot/consensus marks (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27):** **Overweight, PT $600.00** (unchanged from Adam Wood's 07-21 assumption of coverage — no drift), thesis titled **"Durability of Earnings Growth & AI Leadership Not Priced-In."** Base = **25x FY28e EPS of $23.86** (a slight premium to large-cap software peers, **1.2x PEG** a discount to MSFT's own history); **Bull $795 = ~29x bull FY28e EPS of $27.39** (Azure low-20s revenue CAGR, op margins to ~48.9% by FY28); **Bear $250 = ~12x bear FY28e EPS of $21.64** (mid-teens rev CAGR, op margins only to ~44.9% on gross-margin pressure). **These bull/bear levels are net-new on this page and replace nothing live** — the prior MS scenario set (bull $860 / bear $310, Nowak 07-12) was retired together with the $650 base on 07-23 and sits in the Changelog; MS has now rebuilt the ladder under the $600 base. ⚠️ **Spot correction worth logging:** the risk-reward page marks MSFT spot at **$381.70** with the **mean consensus PT at $561.57** (consensus PT range **$400-$870**) — verified off the exhibit's own implied returns ($795 = +108.28%, $600 = +57.19%, $250 = -34.50%, all consistent with $381.70). Key earnings inputs: **Azure & Other Cloud growth 34.4% (Jun-25) → 40.0% FY26e → 42.3% FY27e → 43.0% FY28e**; M365 Commercial Cloud **15.3 → 16.5 → 14.6 → 15.9%**; gross margin **68.8 → 67.7 → 65.7 → 64.4%**; operating margin **45.6 → 46.6 → 46.5 → 46.7%**; GAAP EPS growth **15.6 → 27.2 → 13.1 → 21.6%**. MS vs consensus FY Jun-2026e: revenue **329,533 vs cons 329,420** (range 327,677-333,071); EBITDA **196,643 vs cons 197,707**; net income **129,237 vs cons 125,680**; EPS **17.35 vs cons 16.80** (range 16.70-17.36). Note two cross-checks against marks already on this page: MS's **FY28e $23.86** ties exactly to the ModelWare figure logged 07-16, and MS's **FY27e Azure 42.3% / FY28e 43.0%** sits above UBS's 42-43%-exiting-CY26 path — MS is underwriting durability *past* the exit rate, not just to it. Ownership: Inst. owners 52.5% active, HF sector long/short 2.1x, net exposure 29.5%; MS Alpha Models 3/5 BEST (24m) / 1/5 MOST (3m). (Morgan Stanley "The Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC", 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260727_2024.html)
- 🔴 **MSFT GETS ITS OWN CAPEX SLIDE IN MS’S 99pp AI GUIDEBOOK, AND MICROSOFT IS THE ONE HYPERSCALER WHOSE ’28 DOES *NOT* STALL: DATA-CENTER CAPEX $175bn IN ’26, GROWING 43% IN ’27 AND 20% IN ’28.** The mechanism MS uses across all four names is **“pre-build”** — long-dated land, buildings, equipment and power bought in one year for capacity that opens in later years (MS estimates *“in some cases 30-40%”* of ’26 capex is pre-build across the group). For Microsoft: **~37% of ’26 capex is pre-build**, and MS sees it **“slowing to 14% in ’27 (or $36bn) and 8% in ’28 (or $24bn)”** — slowing, but **still GROWING in both years**. ➤ **THAT IS THE WHOLE CROSS-READ, AND IT IS WHY MSFT CARRIES THE HIGHEST ’28 GROWTH OF THE FOUR (+20%): every other hyperscaler’s pre-build REVERSES — [[AMZN]] contracts 35% (−$30bn) in ’28, [[META]] contracts 19% in ’27 then goes to “minimal,” [[GOOG]] grows ~6x in ’27 to $61bn off a tiny ~5% base and then shrinks. Microsoft’s ’28 is not a stronger demand call; it is the only pre-build curve that does not roll over.** Group frame: *“data center capex growing 60% ($550bn) in ’27 but… ~12% growth in ’28 ($170bn).”* (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak, CFA, with Stephen C Byrd / Adam Wood / Joseph Moore / Michelle M. Weaver / Ariana Salvatore / Lindsay A Tyler / Vishwanath Tirupattur / David Arcaro / Julian Herrera / Gregory Gao — “The Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook: Navigating the Age of Inference”, 2026-09-07, 99pp, Industry View Attractive.) ⚠️ **BASIS: DATA-CENTER capex, not total-company capex or Microsoft’s own fiscal-year capex line. That said, MS’s $175bn for ’26 lands exactly on Microsoft’s OWN guided CY26 figure (~$190B cut to ~$175B at the Q4 FY26 print, already carried in `## Intra-quarter`) — corroboration, not a second independent estimate.**

- **Where MSFT sits in the deck’s other debates.** (1) **Open-weights** — MS argues cheaper/open models *“reenforc[e] the value of the hyperscaler concierge layer (AWS Bedrock, MSFT Foundry, GOOGL Vertex) that optimizes compute efficiency by task,”* i.e. Foundry is framed as a BENEFICIARY of model commoditisation, not a victim; this is the same control-plane thesis this page already carries, now from MS’s flagship. (2) **Credit** — *“Despite ~$450bn of AI-related debt issuance globally year-to-date, we think there’s still significant capacity to absorb additional issuance, especially from high-quality hyperscalers… the market adjustment is likely to come through WIDER SPREADS”*, with **hyperscaler ’27/’28 operating cash flow “expected to be 7-8X larger than required debt”** and AMZN/GOOGL/META/MSFT together generating **“over $1tn of OCF in 2028.”** (3) **TAM** — **$50-$60tn**, split **$20-$30tn of global knowledge-work spend to be digitized through GenAI tools** plus **~$30tn of consumer spend** (retail, travel, autonomous, advertising).
