_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-05 (1 source: Microsoft CVP Bill Duff, quoted verbatim via @TheTranscript_ corpus, 2026-09-04) — ONE ROW MOVES, AND IT IS THE ONE THE BEAR CASE LIVES IN.** The **`Demand cover / bookings`** row has carried commercial bookings **+18% ex-OpenAI** as management's own evidence of breadth. Duff supplies the absolute: ***"we saw an INCREASE IN OUR BACKLOG of about $50 BILLION, a little bit more than $50 billion. That was ALL OUTSIDE OF [[OPENAI]] AND [[ANTHROPIC]]. So it is ALL NON-FRONTIER LABS, and that backlog is growing. So we are seeing a REAL BROADENING OF DEMAND."*** ➤ **`Demand cover / bookings` MOVES FROM ⚠ nuança TO ✓ CONFIRMA** — a percentage with the largest counterparty stripped out is suggestive; a **>$50bn single-quarter increase with BOTH frontier labs excluded** is the same claim with a denominator, and it is management stating it unprompted at a third-party venue rather than in a scripted call. ⚠️ **THE THREE CAVEATS TRAVEL WITH THE UPGRADE: (1) BACKLOG, not revenue — conversion timing is unaddressed; (2) "non-Frontier labs" is NOT "enterprise" — AI-native companies funded by the same capital cycle sit inside it, so this narrows the concentration risk without eliminating it; (3) the quote reaches this wiki through a CORPUS RELAY of a conference appearance, not a transcript on disk — venue and exact date unestablished. **No other row moves, and the `Azure` row's standing caveat is unaffected: the $101.9bn TTM figure logged 09-04 is on the OLD definition and remains an invalid growth base against F1Q27 reporting.**_

_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-09 (/run-inbox, 1 source: Morgan Stanley’s 99pp “The Morgan Stanley AI Guidebook: Navigating the Age of Inference”, Brian Nowak CFA + 10 co-authors, 2026-09-07 — the firm’s flagship AI “Foundation” deck). THE `Signal vs management` TABLE STANDS, WITH ONE ROW MOVING FROM ✓ TO A NARROWER ✓ AND ONE GAINING A NUANCE, AND THE SÍNTESE IS UNCHANGED.** ➤ **`Capex / capital` — CONFIRMED AND NOW EXPLAINED: management’s CY26 ~$175B cut is exactly MS’s ’26 data-center-capex mark, and MS puts the forward shape at +43% (’27) / +20% (’28). The new content is the DECOMPOSITION — ~37% of ’26 capex is “pre-build” and it keeps growing (+14%/+$36bn in ’27, +8%/+$24bn in ’28) while every peer’s pre-build reverses. Microsoft’s superior ’28 capex growth is a pre-build-timing artefact, not a demand differential, and anyone quoting “MSFT still growing 20% in ’28 while AMZN does 6%” as a demand signal is reading the wrong variable.** ➤ **`AI stack / model neutrality` — NUANCE ADDED, NOT CONTESTED: MS frames Foundry as a beneficiary of open-weights commoditisation (the “concierge layer”), which supports the row; but the same deck models >50% of Microsoft’s incremental ’27 owned capacity as still NVDA, i.e. MAIA is not carrying the ’27 build. That is a real constraint on the vertical-integration leg of the bull case and is logged as such rather than folded into the row.** **No other row moves — the deck carries nothing on Azure growth, Copilot seats, bookings/RPO or gross margin.** ⚠️ **DEDUP: the deck’s per-GW GenAI-ROIC economics are MS’s 2026-07-27 “Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC” work re-published — already on this page in full — with only cosmetic drift (IT depreciation $5bn → $4.6bn per GW; total opex relabelled $7.6bn → “~$8bn” per GW; the 3P/rented case rounded $40.5bn → “~$41bn” per GW). It is NOT independent corroboration of itself.**_

_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-09-15 (/wiki-ingest, 21:00 — the kill-switch/"pacing" act + Nadella at the All-In Summit + BofA's hyperscaler-credit initiation).** The row this moves is **AI stack / model neutrality**, and it moves it in the company's favour without a number: Microsoft answered the frontier-safety debate with a **shipped product commitment (kill switches, shutdown rules)** rather than a policy paper, and was cited by a sovereign regulator (South Korea) inside 24 hours — which is exactly the shape of the "model-neutral enterprise AI control plane" claim this table already carries, now with an enforcement hook attached. Two caveats keep the signal at **⚠ nuance rather than ✓ confirm**: (i) Nadella's own framing that there is a **"massive model capability overhang"** concedes that the constraint on AI revenue is diffusion rather than capability — the honest reading is that endorsing a frontier pause costs this P&L almost nothing, so the statement is weak evidence about demand; and (ii) the **funding** row is untouched and got harder, with BofA's credit desk putting Microsoft alone among the big four at **zero issuance and $75bn needed through YE28** (issuance, not capex). **Síntese do quarter unchanged: the Azure/Copilot/capacity trio still governs the print; today added positioning, not numbers.**_

RPO grew 84% Y/Y**."* ② *"**MSFT emerging as an ORCHESTRATION LAYER for Enterprise AI** — building a broad portfolio of internal and external models for clients to use, **in a token-cost-effective way**."* ③ *"**Internal innovation is LOWERING THE COST OF COMPUTE.** Perhaps a more subtle point, but Tal points to ongoing gains in internal efficiency across **hardware/software/systems design.** MSFT talked about **better Copilot throughput, performance per watt gains.**"* ④ *"Drivers of stock from here? Accelerating rev growth, sustainable margins helped by efficiency gains, **conversion of MSFT's ~$700B RPO into revs**, as capacity comes online."* **On the capex/FCF question: *"MSFT is a big capex spender these days, but **committed to POSITIVE FCF in '27** (Street was already modeling this to be clear, but there are obviously **other hyperscalers with less, or no, FCF**)."*** ➤ 🔴 **THE TWO NUMBERS TO CARRY: a **~$700bn RPO growing 84% y/y** is the largest contracted backlog on this wiki, and **30M paid Copilot seats with net additions MORE THAN DOUBLING sequentially** is the first genuinely inflecting seat number the page has had. ⚠️ Attack the third pillar: "internal innovation lowering the cost of compute" is asserted qualitatively (throughput, perf/watt) with no unit economics attached — and it is doing real work in the 28x multiple.** |
| **09-01** | 🔴 **HOT CHIPS — MAIA 200 IS A FULL TRAINING/INFERENCE STACK, AND THE POINT IS THE NETWORK, NOT THE CHIP** (@QQ_Timmy, corpus relay of the Hot Chips 2026 sessions) ⚠️ *corpus relay, not a Microsoft release* | **produto / capital** | **bull** | ★ *"**Microsoft's Maia 200 accelerator combines large SRAM, HBM and high bandwidth into a hyperscale training/inference stack — but the real point is UNIFIED ETHERNET SCALE-UP: using high back-end bandwidth to connect large numbers of accelerators and DRIVE DOWN SYNCHRONISATION OVERHEAD. Microsoft wants to VERTICALLY INTEGRATE silicon, packaging, memory and networking, but the payoff still depends on whether SOFTWARE AND CLUSTER SCHEDULING CAN KEEP UP with the hardware scaling.**"* ➤ 🔴 **Two things worth holding. (a) Choosing **unified Ethernet scale-up** rather than a proprietary fabric puts Maia on the same side of the architectural fight as [[AMD]]'s UALink/Helios and OpenAI's Jalapeño (which runs a *"scale-up Ethernet protocol… 200 gigabit"*) — i.e. the anti-NVLink camp now includes the second-largest cloud, the #2 merchant GPU vendor and the largest frontier lab. (b) The stated risk is **scheduling software**, which is the one place Microsoft's own advantage should be largest. ✅ Cross-reads to JPM's Hot Chips finding that the workload has moved *"from compute-FLOPS-bound / prefill-dominated to memory-bandwidth-bound / decode-dominated"* — Maia 200's large-SRAM-plus-HBM design is aimed at exactly that.** ⚠️ **Corpus relay of conference content: no volumes, no deployment dates, no cost comparison. Treat the architecture as disclosed and the economics as unknown.** |
| **09-01** | 🔴 **MICROSOFT IS NAMED AS ONE OF THREE BUYERS LOCKING ~70% OF SAMSUNG'S HBM CAPACITY THROUGH 2031 — WHICH IS A COST *AND* A MOAT** (Trendforce, via UBS sector sales and JPM, 2026-09-01) | **supply / margem** | **mixed** | ★★ *"**HBM3E spot prices have spiked to 4–5x LTA levels as Samsung locks roughly 70% of its capacity through 2031 for [[NVDA]], MICROSOFT, and [[GOOG]]** — squeezing out spot-market supply just as **HBM4 ramps toward mass production at ~$3,500/unit.** With **server DRAM contract prices projected to rise 13–18% QoQ in 3Q26**, the memory market's **shift toward long-term agreements is fundamentally reshaping WHO GETS ACCESS to capacity — and at what price.**"* ➤ 🔴🔴 **THIS IS THE MOST UNDER-DISCUSSED COMPETITIVE ADVANTAGE IN THE WINDOW. Being one of three names with contracted HBM through 2031 is a five-year supply guarantee at a moment when spot is 4-5x contract — it prices the capacity Microsoft can actually deploy, and it prices out everyone who did not sign. ⚠️ THE OTHER SIDE: a multi-year fixed-volume commitment made at 2026 prices is a cost risk if memory ever normalises, and it is capital committed ahead of demand. Both legs are real; neither is quantified for MSFT specifically.** ✅ **It also independently supports BofA's *"conversion of ~$700bn RPO into revs, AS CAPACITY COMES ONLINE"* — the memory piece of that capacity is contracted.** ⚠️ **And the counterweight to all hyperscaler capex plans, from MS's own policy strategist: MS analysts *"expect hyperscaler capex to EXCEED $1T next year"*, but data-centre political pushback is *"the KEY POLICY RISK to the AI infrastructure buildout"*, producing *"a 'CONDITIONAL BUILDOUT'… data centers continue to get built, but increasingly MIGRATE TOWARD JURISDICTIONS WILLING TO ACCOMMODATE THEM"* (Ariana Salvatore, 09-01).** |
| **09-01** | **MSFT OUTPERFORMED A WEAK TAPE, AND UBS'S CROWDING SCREENS DO *NOT* FLAG IT** | **positioning** | **bull** | **On a de-grossing session — QQQ −1.3%, Semis/Software −2%, *"Expensive Software −4.3%"*, Security SW −5.3%, with *"weakness across the board: SNOW −3%; SAP −4%; HUBS −4%; MNDY −5%; [[ORCL]] −5%; [[NET]] −6%"* — **MSFT was −1.3%, described as *"decent outperformance"*** (TMTB EOD Wrap, 09-01). **And notably MSFT is ABSENT from UBS's *"Heavily Long HF Crowded (score > +21)"* list, which that day contained [[AVGO]], [[HPE]], MDB, MDT and SNOW** (Josh Guilliard, PB data). ➤ **A 28x-multiple upgrade landing on a name that is neither crowded nor being sold is a better setup than the same call on a crowded long. Recorded as a positioning observation, not a view.** ⚠️ **Macro context for the whole complex: the 10Y hit **4.792%, its highest since January 2025**; 30Y Treasuries on their **worst run since 2006**; 10Y JGB through **3% (a 30-year high)**; and the market pricing **40bps of HIKES vs 25bps pre-Jackson Hole, with September odds near 70%** (@zerohedge; TMTB; JPM · Drew Tyler, who moved to a *"Tactically Cautious / Neutral"* view on US equities the same morning).** |
| **08-31** | 🔴🔴 **Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick / Bhavin Shah — "US Software: Highlights from the 2026 DB Tech Conference" (fireside + investor meetings with CVP & CFO of Microsoft Commercial Business Bill Duff, IR's Josh Elvidge and Danielle Criste)** — **MANAGEMENT VOICE, hosted by a broker: no rating, no PT, no estimates in this section** | demanda / capital / supply | **bull (with a cost caveat management volunteered)** | ★ **A company-primary, and the capex language is unusually direct.** 🔴 **CAPEX & ROIC — THE SENTENCE TO HOLD THE FY27 FCF GUIDE AGAINST: management is *"committed to investing what is required to service its massive backlog and meet the forward demand signal,"* while *"optimizing for QUALITY AND FUNGIBILITY OVER SPEED ALONE"* and maximising **revenue per dollar of capex invested**. And explicitly: *"guidance for POSITIVE FCF IN FY27 should be thought of as AN OUTPUT of these efforts AND NOT AN INPUT to their capital budgeting process."* ⚠️ **Read that plainly — FY27 positive FCF is not a constraint management is managing to. If demand rises, capex rises and the FCF line moves.** 🔴 **AND THE COST ADMISSION: *"they are also CLEAR-EYED THAT THE COST ENVIRONMENT IS NOT GETTING ANY EASIER RIGHT NOW, AND THEY DON'T GET A FREE PASS ON THIS. THERE IS NO MAGIC BULLET"* — levers named are negotiating supply contracts, optimizing BOM design, adjusting discounting, pricing of new SKUs, and further efficiency. Given the DRAM/HBM pricing marks logged elsewhere this run, memory is the obvious referent.** 🔴 **PRICING MECHANICS DISCLOSED, and this is genuinely new detail: *"on many larger long-term AI capacity contracts PRICING IS SET ON MORE OF A TRANCHE-BY-TRANCHE BASIS ON PREVAILING MARKET CONDITIONS CLOSER TO THE TIME OF DELIVERY."* Backlog is therefore NOT fully price-fixed — it marks to market as it delivers, which is upside in a rising-GPU-price world and the opposite in a falling one.** 🔴 **CAPACITY: despite bringing on **1GW+ of datacenter capacity in EACH of the past three quarters**, *"supply constraints will PERSIST THROUGH CALENDAR YEAR-END."* Stated build preference order: **self-builds > datacenter leases > capacity leases.** Desire for standardization/fungibility across the fleet, *"limiting their interest in bespoke or one-off designs."* **Allocation priority: first-party apps first, then internal R&D — both framed as NOT currently supply constrained; existing customers and workloads relatively unconstrained; *"most of the squeeze coming on ability to serve NET NEW customers and workloads."*** **DEMAND DIFFUSION: *"all incremental RPO in F4Q com[ing] from outside top AI labs"* — used to argue the need to match the full stack of Azure services region-by-region.** **M365 COPILOT: recent seat-add acceleration *"primarily driven by EXPANDING CUSTOMER DEPLOYMENTS,"* customers moving to **wall-to-wall deployments**, and the disclosure that **Copilot engagement is now ON PAR WITH TEAMS AND OUTLOOK.** ⚠️ **Consumption-based CoWork for longer-running agentic tasks is *"still much earlier days"* — customers still identifying high-value use cases and building governance/cost guardrails. Management frames hybrid seats+consumption as the right model and points to having run the Azure consumption transition before.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/20260831_Deutsche_Bank_NOW_US_Software-_Highlights_from_the_2026_DB_Tech_Conference.html) |

They were more than half of compute, but they now don’t generate cash. They actually spend everything on CapEx. And in addition, they raise debt and spend everything on CapEx, right? You’ve seen Meta do it, even Amazon, even Google, you know, MICROSOFT WILL BE THERE SOON. Everyone is raising debt to pay for their CapEx.”** ➜ **A forecast, not an observation — and it is a falsifiable one, which is why it is logged: it says Microsoft has not yet crossed into debt-funded capex but is expected to.** 🔴 **(2) THE STRUCTURAL ADVANTAGE OF A BALANCE SHEET, which is the more useful half and cuts the other way: “For compute that you can build WITHOUT FINANCING… If Meta can build compute, MICROSOFT, Amazon, SpaceX can build compute without finding a customer, just saying, fuck it, I’m gonna build this compute, and then turn around and wait till it’s already built, THEY NOW CONTROL WHAT’S GOING ON. So most compute is contracted well before it’s built” — and everyone else must first find a customer, then take that commitment to the credit markets. Patel’s base case is that most compute still transacts at “sub $20 billion a gigawatt” even at end-2027, while the labs must pay “25, 30, 50 million dollars a megawatt” to take 70-80% of incremental compute in 2028.** ⚠️ **UNIT AND SCOPE DISCIPLINE: every $/MW and $/GW figure here is REVENUE per unit of capacity, not build cost. The industry capex figures he gives (“a little bit over a trillion dollars… this year,” “more than $2 trillion” by 2028, “about $11 trillion… from 2024 to 2029” split ~$6tn cash / ~$5tn credit) are INDUSTRY-WIDE and must not be converted into GW, into $/watt, or into a per-hyperscaler split.** (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel podcast w/ Daniel Grozdea, 2026-08-25) |

MAIA IS MOVING TOWARD SELF-SUFFICIENCY AS IT RAMPS, BUT IT’S NOT AT THE STAGE OF REPLACING NVIDIA”** — against AWS “almost entirely in-house design across Layers 2 to 5 since acquiring Annapurna Labs,” Google with “the most visibly listed external partners across all 6 layers,” and [[META]] on a multi-vendor MTIA + AMD MI450 + NVIDIA + in-house Arm CPU portfolio. **Where Microsoft IS aligned with the field: an ARM host CPU (Cobalt) alongside Graviton / Axion / Vera; an IPU/SmartNIC seat filled by AZURE BOOST DPU (vs AWS Nitro, NVIDIA BlueField, Google+Intel); host-bypass data path via Azure GPUDirect RDMA; and AZURE MANAGED LUSTRE, converging with AWS FSx Lustre and Google Managed Lustre on a single parallel-file-system standard.** ➜ **Read four months on, the judgement has aged in Microsoft’s favour on ONE axis and not the other: the Maia programme has since produced a Maia 300 report and a supply-chain demand revision (MS Asia, 08-24, above), but nothing on this page yet contradicts “not at the stage of replacing NVIDIA.”** ⚠️ **One author’s scorecard, no data behind the layer rankings; logged as a frame, not as evidence.** (Damnang / “The Optical Edge”, “Google’s AI Infrastructure Layer Map”, 2026-04-28) |
| **08-20** | 🔴 **Morgan Stanley · Meta Marshall / Ryan Lountzis — “Cybersecurity: Agents on the Loose: Cyber Faces an Identity Crisis”** (Software – North America, Industry View **Attractive**; ⚠️ **a CYBERSECURITY note — it carries NO MSFT rating, price target or estimate; read-through only**) | **produto / competicao** | **mixed** | **THE MARKET MS IS SIZING: “Our analysis of the agentic identity market points to a ~$60bn+ opportunity over the coming years, DOUBLE the standalone market,”** on the premise that **“agent and non-human identities [are] expected to 100x humans.”** **MICROSOFT'S POSITION, PER MS'S OWN VENDOR MAPS — STRONG BUT NOT FIRST: in ACCESS MANAGEMENT (authentication / authorization / SSO / adaptive access) the key vendors are ranked “1) Okta, 2) MICROSOFT, 3) IBM, 4) Ping Identity, and 5) Cisco”; in IDENTITY GOVERNANCE & ADMINISTRATION they are “1) SailPoint, 2) IBM, 3) MICROSOFT, 4) Oracle, and 5) Broadcom.”** 🔴 **THE PRODUCT MS CREDITS MSFT WITH — AND IT IS A CONTROL-PLANE CLAIM, NOT A POINT PRODUCT: “Agent 365: Microsoft's control plane for AI agents,”** providing agent **observability** (“a centralized registry of agents … agent inventory, ownership gaps, unmanaged agents, agent status, publisher type”), **governance** (“discovery, lifecycle management, and IT-defined guardrails”) and **security** that **“extends Microsoft's existing security stack to agents — companies can continue working within Defender, Entra, and Purview, with agent insights and recommendations brought into those portals.”** MS names **Entra, Defender, Purview and Intune** as the underlying estate. ➜ **THE READ FOR THIS PAGE: MS's own thesis is that agents push identity toward PLATFORMS because “fragmented tools cannot keep pace with modern attacks” — which is structurally favourable to an incumbent that already owns the enterprise identity estate. But MS's explicit winners list is “PANW, OKTA, and SAIL … with most unrealized growth potential in OKTA,” and MSFT is NOT on it.** ⚠️ **Rival platforms are also building against Microsoft rather than around it: Saviynt/Zuma discovery explicitly spans “Microsoft Azure AI Foundry, Microsoft Copilot Studio” alongside AWS Bedrock and Google Vertex AI, and SailPoint governs agents “across platforms such as Microsoft, Copilot Studio, AWS Bedrock.” Microsoft is the substrate everyone is instrumenting — which is both the moat and the reason third parties can monetise on top of it.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/SOFTWARE_20260820_0401.html) |
| **08-20** | **Morgan Stanley · Fernanda Lima / Carolyn Campbell / Vishwanath Tirupattur / Vishwas Patkar / James Egan — "AI Debt Financing Tracker: The Summer Spreads Turned Wider" (Global Credit Strategy)** | **capital** | **mixed** | **Sector financing context, MSFT-specific only by membership — and it does NOT resolve the "laggard pre-funder" row below (JPM, 06-16), because MS publishes no per-name issuance split.** **HQ hyperscaler spreads ~30bp WIDER YTD, and supply has not slowed regardless: $54bn done in July, $41bn in August MTD, ~$445bn of issuance across credit channels YTD vs $217bn for FULL-YEAR 2025, FY26E $500-550bn.** MS expects a busy fall and continued spread widening, and notes HQ issuers *"will continue to flex their ratings muscles to provide credit support to others"* — i.e. the strongest balance sheets are being lent to the weakest links in the chain. **The capex frame: MS's 2027 estimate is ~$1.4 TRILLION, and 2027 combined capex estimates have NEARLY DOUBLED versus November 2025 estimates.** ⚠️ **The constituent list is the fact worth logging: AMZN, GOOGL, META, MSFT, ORCL and — newly — [[SPCX]].** Within YTD-2026 IG issuance ($271bn total): **HQ Hyperscalers $132bn** (monthly Jan→Aug 0, 20, 37, 25, 0, 0, 25, 25), **SpaceX $25bn**, Oracle $25bn, Semiconductors $42bn, Data Center Construction $43bn; **only Alphabet and Amazon have tapped non-dollar markets this year.** ⚠️ **Do NOT infer a per-name capex or issuance split — that exhibit is column-interleaved and cannot be read reliably from the text layer.** [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USCREDIT_20260820_2103.html) |
| **08-19** | 🔴 **Morgan Stanley · Ariana Salvatore / Daniel K Blake / Stephen C Byrd / Meta Marshall / Antonio Jaramillo et al — "AI Sovereignty: Policy, Market, and Economic Implications" (US Public Policy & Global Thematics, 73pp)** | **demanda / margem** | **mixed** | ★ **The framing MSFT bulls will quote and the sentence they will skip, in the same paragraph.** Heading verbatim: *"Sovereign AI Expands the Infrastructure TAM, but Value Accrues at Different Layers: **Microsoft Owns the Control Plane; Nebius and CoreWeave Supply Trusted, Increasingly Localized AI Factories**."* MS calls the Two-Worlds outcome *"a net demand positive for Microsoft, Nebius and CoreWeave"* (more domestic/regional capacity, provider redundancy, local-ownership and data-residency requirements), names MSFT the **"clearest full-stack beneficiary"** with *"Sovereignty Becomes an Incremental Cloud-Adoption Catalyst"*, and splits the value pool — MSFT monetises *"the software and governance layer"*, [[NBIS]]/[[CRWV]] monetise *"scarce capacity, infrastructure design and operating expertise."* ⚠️ **THE OTHER HALF: compute becomes *"less globally fungible"* while *"certification, customer verification, export-control compliance and country-specific deployment costs increase"* — a cost-to-serve and fungibility hit against the very efficiency levers management credited for the F4Q Azure beat; MS also warns fragmentation *"risks slowing overall enterprise AI adoption."*** House capex frame **"$860bn in the US this year alone"** (⚠️ **US-scoped; NOT comparable to the $877bn GLOBAL figure in MS's 07-14 "Power Struggle" note**), policy consequences named as further federal support plus continued US-China de-risking; Europe *"faces a substantial financing challenge"* on strategic autonomy, ASEAN opts for domestic infrastructure plus trusted partnerships. Conclusion: *"a larger and longer-lasting global AI capex cycle."* [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/USELECTION_20260819_1049.html) |
| **08-13** | 🔴🔴 **MICROSOFT IR CALL, post-2Q — Mary (Microsoft Investor Relations), Capstone attended (via BTG Pactual Global Access websession, 2pm BRT)** | **supply / demanda / margem / capital** | **bull** | ★ **THE CONSTRAINT IS RESTATED AND IT IS NOT SILICON — this is the most important line for the whole AI supply chain: the primary constraint on Azure growth is the availability of "POWERED-UP DATA CENTER SHELLS" (land, permits, build, equipment), NOT GPUs or any specific component. Constraints expected to last "AT LEAST THROUGH THE END OF THE CALENDAR YEAR." Microsoft is on track to DOUBLE its datacenter footprint over FY26/27, augmenting with leases where necessary.** ✅ **Directly corroborates the shells-not-chips framing already on this page and in [themes/ai-datacenter-power.md](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), now from IR rather than from a third party.** **DEMAND: "incredibly strong," spanning enterprise to SMB — >350 customers on the AI platform on track to process >1 TRILLION TOKENS this year.** The framing management pushes: AI is *"not just spinning up GPUs or calling APIs"* — it pulls the **full Azure stack** (storage for agentic memory, SQL DB / Cosmos DB to connect agents to enterprise data, traditional compute, migrations), so *"AI STRENGTHENS the case for moving to public cloud"* rather than signalling the end of "Cloud 1.0." **FOUNDRY / MULTI-MODEL: >11,000 models**, fine-tuning, developer tools and security — positioned as **higher-value than bare-metal infra**, with a deliberate multi-model stance (frontier + open-source + fine-tuned proprietary) for cost and business continuity. 🔴 **MARGIN — the answer to the page's standing question: long-term Azure margins expected to be "MATERIALLY SIMILAR" with AI ⚠️ [**quote check, 08-13 audit: "materially similar" is a PARAPHRASE — the verbatim is "we don't have a reason to believe now that Azure revenue Azure margins IN THE LONG TERM LOOK MATERIALLY DIFFERENT WITH AI IN THE PICTURE THAN WITHOUT, because of all these things that we're talking about." Same meaning, but it is a negative construction with no timeframe attached, not an affirmative margin guide — use the verbatim when quoting management**], via pricing levers (new SKUs, dynamic discounting) and efficiency gains across silicon, models, software and operations. And the mechanical one: the CESSATION OF OPENAI REVENUE-SHARE PAYMENTS BENEFITED COGS/MARGIN IN 2Q.** **M365/COPILOT: a "strong quarter" with record usage; the E7 offering driving millions of seats in months; the ROI question has shifted from "whether" to "HOW TO START."** **CAPEX: investing to the demand seen, explicitly refusing to "draw a line in the sand" on investment — while committing to remain FREE-CASH-FLOW POSITIVE for the year.** ★ **CAPACITY MATH, first-party: "we added TWO GIGAWATTS IN FY25… the last three quarters, we've added about a gigawatt. We didn't say in Q1, but FAIR TO THINK UPWARDS OF THREE ADDED IN THIS YEAR" — and on the two-year doubling: "we're HALFWAY THROUGH that, remain on track to do that." On schedule risk: "I WOULDN'T SAY THAT WE'RE DELAYED… if anything, we've even talked about REDUCING DOCK-TO-LIVE TIMES, bringing capacity online earlier." ⚠️ But capacity "DOESN'T NECESSARILY COME ONLINE LINEARLY" — large sites and lease deliveries land lumpily inside a quarter, so "the exact shape of it, hard to say."** ★ **REVENUE PER GIGAWATT — asked whether the ~$30-50bn/GW the questioner cited off recent [[GOOG]]/[[SPCX]]/[[ANTHROPIC]] deals is the going economics for Azure, IR confirmed no deal or pricing figure and reframed it as two levers: (i) PRICING — "every new deal that we sign, we're thinking about discounting… knowing what we do kind of now and supply and demand, DO YOU HAVE TO HAVE THE SAME DISCOUNT RATE?" and "every new SKU that comes on is priced with the current pricing environment in mind… think about new SKUs as having some of an EFFECTIVE PRICE INCREASE"; (ii) EFFICIENCY — "THE CAPACITY TODAY IS AS INEFFICIENT AS IT WILL EVER BE" (silicon, models, model routing, software, token throughput, "even operationally, like how fast you can plug in a GPU"). Conclusion drawn from both: "in the long term, LIKE CLOUD REVENUE WILL OUTPACE CLOUD CAPEX." ⚠️ Note IR also volunteered the hyperscaler discipline that caps the upside: "you don't often see hyperscalers PASSING THROUGH ALL PRICING TO CUSTOMERS. That's part of the benefit of being in the public cloud."** ★ **CONTRACT STRUCTURE — the falsifiable leg under the margin claim, and it corroborates the 07-30 UBS callback IR pushback already in the Margins row: long-term contracts are NOT fully price-locked. Negotiated discounts sit on top of whatever "the LIST OR SKU PRICE is," so new SKUs reprice inside the contract; and large infra contracts use TRANCHE DELIVERY — "here's the first batch of infrastructure services. Here's the agreed upon price. We'll deliver that. And then YOU GO THROUGH THAT SAME EXERCISE AGAIN. And when you do that, YOU PRICE FOR THE CURRENT ENVIRONMENT… so NOT ALL PRICE IS LOCKED IN for the entirety of the contract."** ★ **NEOCLOUD / POWERED-SHELL PARTNER CRITERIA: "a lot of it is HYPERLOCAL… where do we see demand, LATENCY REALLY MATTERS," so the test is speed-to-capacity in the right place at a workable price — "IT'S NOT BECAUSE WE THINK ANY OF THESE PROVIDERS CAN DO ANYTHING THAT WE CAN'T." Build-own-operate remains the stated first-best ("in an ideal world… you get the best TCO"); leases and neocloud capacity are the explicit second-best for the imbalance.** ⚠️ **IR commentary, not guidance; recap prepared from the call.** (Capstone note from the Microsoft IR call, 2026-08-13) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_13_ir_msft_post_2q.html) |

EPS FY26A $17.28 → FY27E $19.67 → **FY28E $23.85 (raised from $23.15, +3.0% — the only estimate change in the note)**; EBIT margin 46.8% → 46.7% → 47.2%; Adj. P/E 28.5x / 25.0x / 20.6x. Longer-dated: **18% EPS CAGR through FY31 on a ~19% revenue CAGR with flattish operating margins**, and CY25-29 revenue/EPS CAGRs of **20%/19% vs ~8%/15% for the S&P 500**. The re-rating case is a premium argument: MSFT at **~20x CY28 vs ~17x for the S&P**, a **21% premium vs a 43% historical (2020→) average**, and 24x vs **~18-19x for the tech comp set (AMZN, AAPL, GOOG, CRM, INTU, META)**. Supply-demand backdrop JPM underwrites it against (its own Comms Infra + Asia Hardware teams): **global AI capacity 60GW (2025) → 253GW (2031) vs demand 116GW → 532GW.** (JPM · Samik Chatterjee, 2026-08-13) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Microsoft_Accelerati_2026-08-13_5348619.html) |

Same note rescues the capex line and indicts the model/silicon position.** **Also introduces EXPERIMENT COMPUTE (architecture search, data mixture, hyperparameter sweeps, ablations) as a third demand category — "fewer chips… means an order-of-magnitude fewer attempts" — absent from every Azure capacity model here.** ⚠️ **Discipline retained, and it is the reason this is bull-tagged rather than promoted: "strategic necessity is not the same thing as shareholder return… when everyone must expand, the cost of expansion gets bid up." Scenario ladder — RSI ~2027 (most likely) / 2029+ (capex digestion, utilisation and depreciation decide who is hurt first, levered weak-cash-flow players forced out; "warrants serious weight") / longer-or-unsolved (impairment; tail). Scenario B IS the Redburn margin short and the Culpan late-binding thesis (08-02 row above), reached from the bull side.** **NOT re-added to the body: Azure +43%, the $100B annual mark and "demand continues to exceed supply," all of which FUNDA cites as return support and all of which this page carries from the 4QFY26 print (07-29) — primary wins.** (FUNDA "Deep\|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html) |

| 08-02 | **Culpium · Tim Culpan — "Microsoft Just Said the Nvidia Part Out Loud"** | supply | **bear** | **★ THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL QUOTE OF THE EARNINGS SEASON FOR THE WHOLE AI SUPPLY CHAIN, and it is on this page rather than on a semis page because Microsoft is the one who said it. Answering Bernstein's Mark Moerdler on how Microsoft protects itself "if there really is overcapacity and overbuilding of data centers or overbuilding of chips," Amy Hood: "You've seen our CapEx really pivot toward what I would call, and do call, SHORT-LIVED ASSETS, which really, that's CPUs and GPUs that have relatively shorter lead times… IF THE DEMAND ENVIRONMENT CHANGES, YOU JUST SLOW DOWN WHAT IS, IN FACT, THE LARGEST COMPONENT AND THE DRIVER OF COGS." And the summary line: "WHEN YOU HAVE THE ABILITY TO LATE BIND SOME OF THE MORE EXPENSIVE COMPONENTS IN SHORT TERM, YOU HAVE A BIG BOOK OF BUSINESS THAT'S FLEXIBLE."** ⚠️ **Culpan's read, which is the part worth arguing with: the largest buyer of Nvidia silicon has now stated publicly that it can slow-walk GPU purchases without damaging its business — "they can and will take their time." The mechanism he lays out: land and shells depreciate over 20+ years and contribute little to COGS, so build the sheds and keep them empty; the racks carry the GPU cost AND the short depreciation schedule, so "if you want to manage expenses without risking your capacity expansion, you hold off buying GPUs." Hence "a game of capacity chicken" in which executives are incentivised to PUBLICLY MAINTAIN LONG-TERM CAPEX TARGETS while fiddling with quarter-to-quarter compute installation.** ⚠️ **The direct read-through he names: bad for [[NVDA]]/[[AMD]] (capacity booked years ahead, short product cycles, "inventory on the shelf is poison") and bad for [[MU]]/[[SKHYNIX]]/[[SAMSUNG]] as price-taking commodity players. He cites [[SPCX]] and [[META]] both starting to offload their own compute to third parties as the tell that someone over-built.** **THIS PAGE SHOULD HOLD THE TENSION EXPLICITLY: the same Hood answer that the bulls read as prudent risk management (see the BofA/JPM rows below — "spend can be slowed if the demand environment changes," ~two-thirds of capex on short-lived assets) is here read as a pre-announcement of the mechanism by which the AI supply chain gets air-pocketed. The two readings are not reconcilable, and the arbitrating observable is whether Microsoft's shell-build and its GPU-install rates DIVERGE over the next 2-3 quarters. Watch reported capex mix (short-lived vs long-lived) rather than headline capex, which is now a deliberately uninformative number.** ⚠️ **Counterweight from the same week and the same call: Microsoft guided Azure to +45% and said demand continues to EXCEED capacity — a company slow-walking compute for demand reasons would not do that. The honest statement of the risk is that late-binding is an OPTION Microsoft has bought, not a decision it has made.** (Culpium, 2026-08-02, opinion; direct quotes from the 4Q26 call transcript) |

JPM's version: commercial RPO +84% q/q to $678bn, but +25% EXCLUDING OpenAI** (Cardoso, 2026-07-30). **Stock +17%, its biggest gain since 2008, adding ~$450bn of value — the most in market history.** Also from JPM: **C2Q26 capex +29% q/q / +69% y/y to $41bn (incl. leases), roughly TWO-THIRDS on SHORT-LIVED assets (GPUs/CPUs); the ~$190bn CY26 capex guide REAFFIRMED excluding an accounting change; FY27 capex expected to grow y/y; C3Q26 guided to $50bn+. Effective FY27, Microsoft is EXTENDING THE USEFUL LIFE of data centres and office buildings from 15 to 25 YEARS.** ⚠️ **Management's flexibility argument rests on the short-lived-asset mix: "spend can be slowed if the demand environment changes" — the useful-life extension works the other way, flattering reported depreciation.** |
| 07-31 | **Matt Velloso post-2Q26 expert call (CPO Docker; ex-Microsoft, DeepMind, Meta)** | competicao | **bear** | ⚠️ **The dissenting first-party view, and it is worth carrying precisely because it cuts against the consensus on this page. The claim: Microsoft's record market-cap gain is real, but the performance of its OWN AI products is questionable — much of what is credited to Microsoft is "a reflexo do sucesso da OpenAI" (a reflection of OpenAI's success) rather than Microsoft's own innovation. GitHub Copilot is described as "em implosão" now that the "subsídio absurdo" that made it viable has been removed, with its intrinsic quality challenged by aggressively discounted OpenAI GPT models and cheaper Chinese models; the generic Copilot is said to have no relevant market share among AI assistants.** Criticises Microsoft for not disclosing the numbers that would settle it. ⚠️ **Note the direct contradiction with management's own claim on the same product (GitHub Copilot using 10% fewer tokens than OpenAI/Anthropic models) — logged as a live disagreement, not resolved.** By contrast the speaker is bullish on **Meta's model progress ("passou a Google… performando melhor que o Gemini em algumas coisas" despite less data and a smaller team)** and implicitly critical of Microsoft's AI team for not matching that despite direct access to OpenAI. Also bullish on the infrastructure layer generally — Azure and AWS results are "fantásticos" and the reminder is that AI has to be hosted somewhere. ⚠️ Speaker bias disclosed in the source: he is CPO at Docker, so has professional interests in the developer-tooling layer. |
| 07-29 | **MSFT 4QFY26 call · Nadella/Hood** (PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT) | guidance | **bull** | **★ The primary source under every desk row below — and two things in it that the desk notes get wrong or omit. (1) THE EPS BEAT IS ~⅓ THE SIZE THE HEADLINE IMPLIES.** Hood: _"a benefit of **$0.27 on diluted earnings per share**"_ vs the April guide from discrete items — _"a **$3.2 billion gain from our investment in [[ANTHROPIC]]** and lower-than-expected expenses related to the voluntary retirement program, which were partially offset by severance expense and impairment charges in Xbox."_ **So against the $4.74 reported (+23% "when adjusted for the impact from our investment in [[OPENAI]]") the clean number is ≈$4.47 vs consensus $4.24 and UBSe $4.41 — a real beat, but the largest single component of it is a NON-CASH MARK ON A PRIVATE COMPANY, not operating income.** The same carve-out runs through OI&E: **$2.8B "when adjusted for the impact of our investments in OpenAI, driven by the gain on investment in Anthropic"** — Microsoft sizes the Anthropic gain and does **not** size the OpenAI drag. **(2) THE CAPEX NUMBER IS QUOTED ON TWO DIFFERENT BASES ON THIS PAGE AND ONE OF THEM IS THE CASH LINE.** The call: _"**Capital expenditures were $41 billion**… **total finance leases were $5.6 billion**… **cash paid for PP&E was $35.8 billion**"_ — the $41B is **all-in/lease-inclusive** ($35.8B + $5.6B = $41.4B); the **$35.8B in the UBS desk row below is the CASH PP&E line vs a cash-basis consensus, not the all-in figure** — do not compare the two. See the Capex/capital Signal row above for the full reconciliation against this page's computed ~$42.7B 10-K plug (same basis; ~$1.3-1.7B build-up residual, **not** a gross/net artifact). **~Two-thirds of Q4 capex was short-lived assets (CPUs/GPUs).** Other primary detail not in the desk notes: **RPO $678B +84% but +25% ex-OpenAI, with "all sequential commercial RPO growth… driven by commitments from customers outside of Frontier model companies," 2.3-yr WAD, ~30% next-12M (+37% y/y), beyond-12M portion +112%; bookings +18% ex-OpenAI vs +10%/+11% cc including OpenAI Azure commitments; FY26 cloud revenue >$214B "with nearly 90% from customers outside of Frontier model companies"; Microsoft Cloud GM 65% ("better than expected" vs the ~64% guide); FCF POSITIVE $19.6B on OCF $55.4B +30%; headcount −2% y/y; $10.2B returned in Q4 / >$43B FY26; MPC beat because "OEM and channel partners continued to build inventory given increasing component prices"** (a pull-forward the FY27 −high-teens Windows guide then pays for); **D365 "bookings growth at ERP remains healthy, while CRM continued to moderate with longer sales cycles"** (the primary source for the bears' CRM point in the "Uneven non-AI software" row above); **Xbox "expect to return the business to growth in fiscal 2027."** Capacity: **+1GW this quarter, 31 new data centers (88 for the year), "roughly double our overall capacity in just two years," dock-to-live −~50% over FY26, Copilot throughput 4x YTD**; Hood's constraint language plus a new tell — _"there are still constraints in the system… **you can even see it… in some of the pricing that's occurring in the spot market for assets**."_ Satya on the cycle: _"**All of us are reading this 1873 as the book to be read**… there will be ups and downs of what is the cycle here, but the secular shift is clear."_ Hood on ROI: _"I don't know that quite frankly, **my math has changed**"_ … _"**this is the grind work**."_ ⚠️ **Source-routing note: the auto-router tagged this transcript "UBS / 2026-07-30" — both wrong ("UBS" came from Karl Keirstead on the analyst roster; the call was Microsoft's own, held 2026-07-29).** Full six-row breakdown of the call (guidance / capital / competicao / produto / margem / supply) sits in the **archived Q3 FY26 block under `## Changelog`** where it was written before the block was rolled — not duplicated here. | 07-29 | **MSFT 4QFY26 call · Nadella/Hood** (PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT) | competicao | mixed | **★ The model-neutrality row above is directionally right but understates what management actually did: Microsoft benchmarked its OWN models against [[OPENAI]]'s and [[ANTHROPIC]]'s BY NAME, on cost, inside Microsoft's own flagship apps.** Catalog: _"the broadest model catalog in the cloud with **over 11,000 models**, including the latest from OpenAI, Anthropic, Mistral, X-AI as well as our own MAI family"_; **"5x increase since the start of the year in the number of customers building with models from multiple providers"** (successor to the 10,000+/+2x-q/q mark from MSFT IR 05-18); **Levi Strauss & Co "using models from OpenAI and Anthropic on Foundry" across "more than 1,000 domain-specific agents."** **The displacement claims, quantified: MAI Code 1 Flash on GitHub Copilot — "millions of developers… higher code acceptance rates and 10% lower median token usage while still having access to Frontier capabilities from OpenAI and Anthropic"; in Excel, MAI Code 1 Flash "delivering comparable quality to GPT56 [GPT-5.6] for the most common task while operating at significantly lower costs"; MAI Cyber 1 Flash "achieves better performance than much larger Mithos [Mythos] model, but at half the cost when combined with our multi-agent security harness" — Satya's mechanism: "50% less cost… because 90% of the tasks are done by the Cyber Flash One model and 10% of the tasks you still go to the frontier"; 89% GPU-cost reduction in Dynamics 365 (MAI Voice 2 Flash) and up to 84% in PowerPoint (MAI Image 25); MAI Thinking One, MSFT's first reasoning model.** **This is the first-party, numbered version of the 07-28 The Information row below** (MSFT's cheaper AI security model vs Anthropic's Mythos) — press framing upgraded to a vendor claim with a cost figure and a routing ratio. The architecture is the durable part: _"the **harness, context, memory, and action space are separate from any one model family**, thereby moving the frontier on the cost-to-outcome curve… **every model is substitutable**"_, justified on resilience via the **Hugging Face incident** (_"you can't be subject to the refusals of one model"_) and on principle (_"the models are an **input**, not some extraction of the knowledge of the enterprise"_; firms should own _"their human capital and their **token capital**"_). ⚠️ **Read against UBS's 07-26 reflexivity note on this page ("a strong majority of Microsoft's AI revenues are derived from OpenAI"): the down-tiering UBS found inside the OpenAI ladder is now being run by Microsoft itself with MAI as the cheap tier — good for MSFT gross margin, bad for the frontier labs' share of Microsoft's own token spend.** Two-sided offsets from the same call: **Maia 200 at "30% better performance per dollar than the latest generation hardware in our fleet" and "now supporting both OpenAI and MAI models"** (first Maia workload disclosure on this page — it nuances the "dead last on custom ASICs" / "No Model, No Chip" reads without overturning Fubon's ~1%-of-CoWoS mark), **Cobalt 200 in 25+ DCs with Cobalt VMs running Adobe/ARM/Elastic/OpenAI workloads**, MSFT **"among the first cloud providers to deploy… AMD Helios and NVIDIA Vera Rubin"** (corroborates Archera 07-13 on Rubin-first), a **Mistral partnership for Microsoft Sovereign Cloud**, and the role-reversal datapoint — Microsoft's new web-grounding layer for agents is **"already being used by the most popular AI assistants, including ChatGPT."** ⚠️ **DeepSeek V4 was not mentioned on the call at all** (cf. Stratechery 06-23) — MAI, not a Chinese open-weight model, is the cost defence Microsoft chose to present. (MSFT 4QFY26 earnings call, 2026-07-29) |

Then the conversion math: **30% of RPO converts to revenue in the next 12 months, up from 25% last quarter**, at 2.3-yr average duration → "**the revenues for the next 12 months are covered by 57% by the RPO. Then… 70, then… 90" — MSFT "is disciplined with CapEx… a much more measured spending profile" vs peers. | **09-11** | 🔴🔴 **MANAGEMENT — Microsoft IR fireside hosted by Jefferies (London)** ⚠️ **MANAGEMENT source, not Jefferies research** | **capital** | **mixed** | 🔴 **THE ROIC FRAME, AND THE TIMING CONCESSION INSIDE IT.** (Transcript says *"ROC"* — **ASR garble for ROIC**; management says "ROIC" in the same answer.) **The first-cloud-wave analogy:** in build-out *"you saw Capex growth higher than cloud revenue growth and then you saw those lines cross and then when they crossed… you saw really good returns coming through there"*, and *"We expect the exact same thing to be true in this build out phase, in this AI wave."* Long-lived vs short-lived capex mix is the mechanical explanation for depressed early returns — long-lived assets *"you're going to monetize for 25 years plus"*, revenue arrives sooner as mix shifts to *"the kits side."* ⚠️ 🔴 **THE CONCESSION, FROM THE COMPANY RATHER THAN A BEAR:** *"What's tricky for us is just the demand signal keeps ticking up. And as the demand signal continues to grow, you've got to then build ahead of that… **It just does push out that time frame when those lines cross**."* ➤ **And the source of return is fungibility, explicitly NOT anchor tenancy: *"ROIC comes from one stack, a fully fungible fleet… It's never been about one site, one workload, one customer name."*** ➤ **Read against the ~45%-of-RPO OpenAI concentration under `## Risks`: management is pre-empting the SINGLE-CUSTOMER objection, not the PAYBACK-PERIOD one.** |
| **09-11** | 🔴🔴 **MANAGEMENT — Microsoft IR fireside hosted by Jefferies (London)** ⚠️ **MANAGEMENT source, not Jefferies research** | **margem** | **bull** | 🔴 **THE OPENAI/ANTHROPIC ECONOMICS, AND A COPILOT MARGIN VARIABLE NOBODY MODELS.** ★ *"if you're in Copilot for example and you're using an OpenAI model, that's economically better than if they're using an Anthropic model **because we don't pay rents there**."* **The mechanism: Microsoft pays NO rev share to [[OPENAI]]; OpenAI still pays a rev share TO Microsoft; Microsoft pays no model rents because it owns the IP — *"it does give us a structural cost advantage"* — and gets all OpenAI IP free until the end of 2032.** ⚠️ **So the SAME Copilot seat carries a different COGS depending on model routing, inside Microsoft's own multi-model pitch.** 🔴 **EXCLUSIVITY CORRECTION, live on the call:** the questioner asserted Microsoft *"kept exclusivity on the stateless API"*; management — *"The stateless thing goes away. There's no exclusivity on stateless, stateful. That was one of the things that… went away"*; OpenAI is *"free to go source compute and have their API served wherever it makes sense."* **Retained instead: Microsoft *"remains their primary cloud provider"* and all OpenAI solutions *"need to ship first on Azure"*** — a lead-time moat, not a volume moat. **[[ANTHROPIC]]:** *"We don't have a big stake in Anthropic"* — a small equity stake taken alongside the deal announced *"back in November I think it was of last year"* with a **reported $30bn Azure contract**; *"much smaller than what we have in OpenAI"*, and on the October IPO *"I don't know that a ton changes"* beyond more disclosure. ⚠️ **STALE NUMBER, NOT A SUPERSESSION: management quoted *"the last number we gave you was we had a 27% stake in OpenAI"* (equity method) — the page's FY26 10-K mark of **~25% as-converted** is newer and GOVERNS; nothing was changed.** ➤ **Strategy answer is the router: >11,000 models in Foundry, Microsoft as the *"IQ rich harness"* letting customers toggle *"or you put on auto mode"* across Foundry, GitHub Copilot and *"Entra 65 Co-Pilot"* (**ASR-garbled**; Entra / Agent 365); *"we expect to continue to see leapfrogging take place."*** |
| **09-11** | 🔴🔴 **MANAGEMENT — Microsoft IR fireside hosted by Jefferies (London)** ⚠️ **MANAGEMENT source, not Jefferies research** | **produto** | **mixed** | 🔴 **E7, THE M365 ARPU BRIDGE, AND AN EXPLICIT WARNING AGAINST THE GITHUB ANALOGUE.** **E7** (launched *"at the beginning of May"*, list ~**65% above E5**): *"We had a much stronger launch of E7 than we've even anticipated"*; bundle = **Copilot seat + additional Entra identity capabilities + "Azure 365" (ASR-garbled; Agent 365)**; the pitch that resonates is **agentic observability/governance from the outset**, on the Azure spend-then-optimise analogy — customers now want it pre-empted (*"I really needs to be able to monitor, understand, govern, secure my agentic flow"*; transcript's *"genitive flow"* is an **ASR garble for AGENTIC FLOW**). ⚠️ **Management's own brake: *"it's an early start… we're weeks and months in here not years"*, and the early cohort is *"those customers who were already considering… a co-pilot license"* — E7 is converting the Copilot pipeline, not creating one.** 🔴 **NOT RESOLVED: UBS Evidence Lab partner checks (2026-07-26) have E7 bought at *"10-50% discount"* with *"the uplift vs E5 likely minimal to date"* — management addressed INTEREST, never REALISED PRICE. Both marks retained.** **ARPU bridge:** E3→E5 is *"the main motion"*, plus Copilot, plus E7 attaching Agent 365 and Entra, **offset by frontline workers and SMB *"at much lower ARPU"*** — *"push and pull on the ARPU."* 🔴 **THE WARNING: *"don't use the parallel of GitHub which had six months of basically unmetered usage that then suddenly… we turn on the consumptive billing on June 1st… That's not true in the M365 consumptive world. **It's more of a standing start**."*** Consumptive levers *"will take some time to become big drivers"* and are *"not sized specifically"*; the bar rises — *"They've got to be great on day one because customers will stop paying."* ⚠️ **SUPER-APP: Copilot Chat, Cowork and GitHub merge and will be reported *"in one KPI"*, but *"we're not saying it's a collapsing of the TAMs… we think it makes the TAMs bigger for us and not smaller."*** **SECURITY:** last disclosure *">20 billion"* was *"a number of years ago"* (*"we gave a 10, a 15, and a 20 and then we haven't given one since"*); *"you could trend some of those out… we are the largest security vendor in the world"*; **Project Perception** agents *"find vulnerabilities and patch them at mass scale"* and are claimed *"rated better than methos at a fraction of the cost"* (⚠️ ***"methos" is ASR-garbled and deliberately NOT identified here***). Security is the third of three agentic domains after coding and productivity, slowest *"for good reason… the bar is extremely high."* ⚠️ **No updated open-source-share-of-Azure stat; nuclear/low-carbon not yet a meaningful share (Three Mile Island *"a couple of years ago"*), *"you'd expect to hear from us"* when it is. ## Management commentary — evolution (last 7 quarters)

| Theme | Q2'25 (Jan-29) | Q3'25 (Apr-30) | Q4'25 (Jul-30) | Q1'26 (Oct-29) | Q2'26 (Jan-28) | Q3'26 (Apr-29) | Q4'26 (Jul-29) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Azure growth | +31% (13pts AI) | +35% cc (16pts AI) | +39%, beat | +39% cc, beat | +38% cc, beat | +40% cc, beat | **+43% (~42% cc), beat** (guide was 39-40% cc); Q1FY27 guided ~+45% cc |
| AI capacity / constraint | capacity-short, balance by FY25-end | short beyond June | demand > supply | constrained thru FY26 | demand > supply | constrained at least thru CY26 | **"demand continues to exceed available supply… you can even see it in some of the pricing that's occurring in the spot market for assets"**; +1GW added, ~double capacity in 2 yrs |
| Capex | $22.6B, FY26 growth slower | $21.4B, FY26 mix to short-lived | $24.2B, Q1 >$30B | $34.9B, FY26 growth > FY25 | $37.5B, ~2/3 short-lived | $31.9B; CY26 ~$190B (~$25B memory) | **$41B all-in** ($35.8B cash PP&E + $5.6B finance leases, ~2/3 short-lived); **CY26 ~$190B → ~$175B on a 15→25-yr useful-life LEASE RECLASSIFICATION — underlying spend unchanged**; Q1FY27 >$50B; FY27 "will grow y/y" |
| MS Cloud gross margin | 70% | 69% | 68% | 68% | 67% | 66% (Q4 guide ~64%) | **65%** (beat the ~64% guide); Q1FY27 "relatively stable q/q" |
| Commercial bookings | +67% (+75% cc), OpenAI | +18%, OpenAI | +37%, first >$100B | +112%, OpenAI | +230%, OpenAI+Anthropic | -4% incl / +7% ex-OpenAI | **+10% (+11% cc) incl. / +18% ex-OpenAI** |
| M365 Copilot | seats +10X over 18mo | adoption +3X y/y | record seat adds | 90%+ F500 use it | 15M paid seats, +160% y/y | >20M paid seats, +250% y/y | **>30M paid seats**, net adds more than doubling q/q |
| OpenAI partnership | big Azure commitment | further Azure commitment | AI labs as scaled customers | new deal: +$250B Azure, IP to 2032 | ~45% of $625B RPO | no rev-share to OpenAI; rev-share from to 2030 | **RPO $678B +84%, but ALL sequential growth from customers OUTSIDE Frontier model companies**; +25% ex-OpenAI; FY26 cloud revenue "nearly 90% from customers outside of Frontier model companies" |
_Source: MSFT earnings calls (dates above); management commentary, paraphrased._

> **Q4'26 column added 2026-07-31** from the 4QFY26 earnings call (2026-07-29) — the marks were previously parked in a warning note under this table because adding a column meant rewriting all seven rows. Two basis warnings carried forward: the reported EPS $4.74 includes $0.27 of discrete benefit (a $3.2B Anthropic mark-up), so clean EPS is ~$4.47; and the CY26 capex step-down is a **definition change, not a spending cut**, which makes every FY27 capex bogey struck on the old basis non-comparable.

> **Intra-quarter management colour, NOT a column (Microsoft IR investor-group meeting @ Communacopia week, 2026-09-09 — Capstone Notion transcript; IR source, not an earnings call):** `AI capacity / constraint` — the land → cold-shell → powered-shell staging model *"we have not been able to do"* at this demand, FY27 = pull kit dates forward, and *"we've even stopped trying to tell you when… we're going to be more in balance"*; `MS Cloud gross margin` — no guide beyond the next quarter, GitHub COGS-before-revenue named as the Q3/Q4 put-and-take (three full months of GitHub revenue in F1Q); `M365 Copilot` — *"from 15 million… to 30 in a couple quarters"*, the acceleration guide holds in old AND new hierarchy, Cowork metering live 2026-07-01 and inside the guide; `Capex` — *"no shift… from being cautious to being more aggressive"*, no equity, leases = debt-market access. Parked here so the 4QFY26 column stays pure earnings-call commentary.

> **Intra-quarter management colour, NOT a column (Nadella, All-In Summit interview, 2026-09-15 — public CEO interview, not an earnings call or IR meeting; full transcript archived `MSFT/transcripts/MSFT_All-In-Summit-interview_2026-09-15.md`):** `M365 Copilot` — his own TAM framing narrows the addressable base from ~450M total M365 seats to **"maybe 300... 250 even" million "real enterprise users,"** against which he cites **~30M paid seats "and it's growing"** — a richer denominator than the flat 450M this page uses elsewhere (cross-ref `## Debate`); `Capex` — mechanics explained as two asset classes, **long-duration land/power/cold-shell vs. short-duration "kit" (~60% of cost)**, built + leased + **rented for surge** ("we're even renting quite a bit... because we were short on supply"), with explicit intent not to concentrate on 2-3 customers; `AI capacity / constraint` — reiterates the **"massive... capability overhang"** framing (already logged 09-15 via the MS desk relay) as a diffusion-not-capability constraint. Parked here as a second non-earnings-call primary source, alongside the 09-09 IR colour above.

## In the inbox (Outlook — recent sell-side flow)
- **2026-06-22 — Bernstein, "Scaling Intelligence: The Generative AI Handbook":** Microsoft framed as one of the two biggest AI-software winners by revenue in 2025 (with NVIDIA), having reached an **AI run-rate >$20Bn in 2Q FY26** — because it **owns the entire AI stack (data center → app)** and monetizes it three ways: GPU rental (Azure), the vendor model (OpenAI on Azure) and Copilot. A stress test of the $30/month Copilot (heavy user, 40 inferences/day, 1.7T model) still leaves **~52% gross margin** — a structural advantage vs those renting model-as-a-service (MSFT cost per inference ~$0.012 vs ~$0.045/1k tokens on OpenAI’s API). In Mar-26 MSFT launched **Office 365 E7** (E5 + Copilot + agentic/security; fixed price with a usage component for agents). Bernstein argues AI IaaS-PaaS margins converge toward traditional Cloud’s over time (MSFT AI margins hit a temporary peak of ~40% in 2Q FY25 and should keep improving), and that **AI is additive — not disruptive — to the IaaS-PaaS TAM** through 2030. (Blackbook; used as a framework reference, not an earnings note.) [../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_Gen_AI_Software.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_on_Gen_AI_Software.html)
- **2026-07-02 — The Information (three items, press-sourced, not bank research):** (1) **SOTP valuation** — a sum-of-the-parts by business puts Microsoft's "real value" at **$3.5–3.8 trillion, ~$500/share**, above the current market cap — a bull framing that the AI/cloud + productivity + gaming segments are worth more in pieces than the whole. (2) **Copilot consolidation** — an internal memo says MSFT will **merge its Copilot chatbots** so each "earns the right to exist," i.e. rationalizing the sprawling Copilot surface area into fewer products. (3) **Layoffs** — MSFT plans **thousands of layoffs across sales, engineering and Xbox** (per The Information AM). Read: cost discipline + product rationalization as the AI capex bill scales — margin-supportive, execution-risk to watch. (The Information, 2026-07-02)
- **2026-06-23 — Stratechery (Ben Thompson), "Memory Chips and China, Microsoft and Chinese Models":** Microsoft is very incentivized to use Chinese models — under usage-based pricing (E7 = Anthropic+OpenAI at $99/employee/mo), an open-source model lets MSFT offer more bundled usage without ceding margin to the frontier labs; MSFT confirmed to Axios it is exploring a fine-tuned DeepSeek V4 (or another open-source model) for Copilot Cowork, available "in the coming weeks." Thompson's call: Cowork best-with-Claude, Copilot best-with-GPT, DeepSeek the cheap default; American software cos as the biggest beneficiaries of cheaper Chinese models. (Newsletter, not a bank.)

- **2026-09-03 (Microsoft company primary ingested — "FY27 Segments and Investor Metrics", 22pp investor deck, 2026-09-03; plus Jefferies "AI Deep Dive" 2026-09-02 and an unattributed BTM power call 2026-08-27).** Edits: **1 Snapshot rewrite (thesis-drift)**, 1 At-a-glance cell, 1 Current-state block, 1 Debate/thesis bullet, 1 Catalysts bullet, 1 dated intra-quarter RE-EVALUATION, **3 Signal-vs-management cell appends (Azure · Copilot/monetization · Story/disclosure quality)**, 3 Full-log rows (09-03, 09-02, 08-27), 1 Sources entry, this entry.
  🔴🔴 **THESIS-DRIFT — THE SEGMENT STRUCTURE IS SUPERSEDED, AND IT IS A DEFINITION CHANGE, NOT A NUMBERS CHANGE. OLD (in the `## Snapshot` opening line since this page was generated, 2026-06-18): *"Mega-cap software + hyperscale cloud, **three reporting segments: Productivity & Business Processes (M365/Office, LinkedIn, Dynamics), Intelligent Cloud (Azure, server, GitHub, enterprise services) and More Personal Computing (Windows OEM, Search/Bing, Gaming/Xbox)**."* NEW (effective FY27, announced 2026-09-03): **TWO segments — Agents and Infra** (Microsoft Cloud, productivity and server licensing, consulting and support; lines: Azure · Microsoft 365 cloud · Productivity and server licensing · Industry solutions · Frontier and support services) **and Devices and Consumer** (Windows, XBOX, advertising; lines: Search and advertising · XBOX · Windows OEM and devices).** The old three-segment text is preserved here verbatim and the body now carries the new structure.
  🔴🔴 **AND THE TRAP THAT MATTERS MORE THAN THE LABELS: AZURE IS REDEFINED. Out of Azure → GitHub cloud + other developer cloud services and Security Copilot (→ M365 commercial cloud) and Healthcare and Life Sciences cloud (→ the new Industry solutions cloud). **EVERY AZURE GROWTH NUMBER ELSEWHERE ON THIS PAGE — the +43% F4Q26 print, the ~45% cc F1Q27 guide, cons +40.2%, the 40.6% Street bar, BofA's 41.8% FY27E / 37.7% FY28E, MS's 40.0/42.3/43.0% FY26-28e, UBS's 42-43% exit rate — WAS COMPUTED ON THE OLD DEFINITION AND IS NOT COMPARABLE TO WHAT MICROSOFT WILL REPORT FROM F1Q27.** Those marks are LEFT IN PLACE as dated old-definition records; they are not superseded, they are re-based.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW ① — THE COMPANY QUANTIFIED THE COST OF ITS OWN REDEFINITION, WHICH IS THE ONLY REASON THIS IS TRACTABLE. Microsoft restated the **July 29, 2026** F1Q27 guidance into the new structure, *"mechanical adjustments only"*, holding everything else constant: **Azure ~45% cc → 44-45% cc** (FX <1pt) · **M365 commercial cloud ~15% cc → ~17% cc** (~16% → ~18% adjusted for prior-year in-period revenue recognition) · **Productivity and server licensing — decline low-single** · **Industry solutions cloud — growth high-single** · **Search and advertising ex TAC mid-single → mid- to high-single**. **No change to commercial bookings, Microsoft Cloud gross margin %, M365 consumer cloud, XBOX, Windows OEM and devices, total revenue $89.85-90.95bn, COGS, opex, operating margin, ~20% tax or capex (>$50bn in Q1).** New segment guide: Agents and Infra $75.15-75.75bn, Devices and Consumer $14.7-15.2bn. ➤ **So the haircut to Azure is 0-1pt and the gift to M365 is +2pts — materially smaller on Azure than the volume of plumbing suggests. That is the whole investor question and the company answered it itself.**
  🔴 **NET-NEW ② — RESTATED HISTORY NOW ON THE PAGE ($m, FY26/FY25): Agents and Infra revenue **268,127 / 218,783**, OI **136,365 / 110,814**; Devices and Consumer revenue **63,712 / 62,941**, OI **18,872 / 17,714**; totals **331,839 / 281,724** and **155,237 / 128,528** (unchanged, as they must be). Product/service lines: **Azure 101,938 / 72,610** · M365 cloud 100,299 / 84,605 · Productivity and server licensing 37,285 / 35,391 · Search and advertising 24,835 / 22,171 · XBOX 21,790 / 23,455 · Industry solutions 20,345 / 18,417 · Windows OEM and devices 17,087 / 17,315 · Frontier and support services 8,260 / 7,760. Restated Azure quarters FY26 22,384 / 24,129 / 26,008 / 29,417 vs FY25 16,021 / 17,337 / 18,540 / 20,712 → **implied y/y COMPUTED from the disclosed table (not a company-stated growth rate): +39.7% · +39.2% · +40.3% · +42.0%; FY26 +40.4%.**

  🔴 **NET-NEW ③ — THE HAIRCUT IS ALSO PROVEN ON FY26 ACTUALS, not just on the guide (restated vs as-reported investor metrics, GAAP % / cc %): **Azure FY26 40% restated vs 41% / 40% reported**, Q4 **42% vs 43%**; **M365 commercial cloud FY26 18% / 15% vs 17% / 14%**, Q4 **16% / 15% vs 14%**; **M365 commercial SEAT growth FY26 7% vs 6%** (paid GitHub seats); **Search and advertising ex TAC FY26 14% / 12% vs 12% / 10%**; **Industry solutions cloud FY26 12% / 10%** (new; nearest old lines were Dynamics 365 18% / 15% and LinkedIn 11% / 9%). ➤ **Consistent direction: away from the Azure headline, toward apps/agents and advertising.**
  ⚠️ **NET-NEW ④ — DISCLOSURE QUALITY MOVES BOTH WAYS AND BOTH HALVES ARE LOGGED. **GAINED:** Azure, Microsoft 365 cloud and Industry solutions become disclosed quarterly **dollar** lines for the first time (Azure previously sat undisclosed inside "Server products and cloud services," $129,425m FY26) — a partial close of the Jefferies/Thill "multiple growth engines, none of them sized" objection standing since 07-30. **LOST:** **LinkedIn revenue growth AND Dynamics 365 revenue growth are both RETIRED as disclosed investor metrics**, replaced by the blended Industry solutions cloud growth; **LinkedIn ceases to exist as a revenue line entirely** ($19,817m FY26 / $17,812m FY25 as reported). ➤ **This permanently removes the ability to audit the Dynamics 365 CRM-bookings moderation logged on 07-30 — the exact line [[CRM]] is read against.** Foundry and Copilot economics remain unsized.

The October-2025 $250B OpenAI agreement — "which we estimate at ~7GW IN TOTAL" — "HAS LEFT MICROSOFT HIGHLY COMPUTE-CONSTRAINED ON THEIR OTHER USE-CASES… UNABLE TO LEVERAGE THEIR ACCESS TO OPENAI MODELS FOR THEIR API BUSINESS FOUNDRY, OR FOR THEIR APPLICATIONS LIKE COPILOT.   🔴 **THE CONTRACT CHANGE THAT WOULD UNLOCK IT IS THE HIGHEST-PRIORITY VERIFICATION ITEM COMING OUT OF THIS RUN: "SATYA NAILED THE NEGOTIATIONS WITH OPENAI: THE DEAL REWORKED IN APRIL 2026 DROPPED THE OLD 20% REVENUE SHARE FROM THE EQUATION. Put simply, MICROSOFT HAS A GIANT INCENTIVE TO PROCURE AS MANY MWs AS POSSIBLE, AS FAST AS POSSIBLE."** ⚠️ **Check against the April 2026 disclosure before relying on it. If correct, it changes the unit economics of every megawatt Microsoft points at Foundry/Copilot rather than at OpenAI, and it is a bigger deal for MSFT's model than the 10GW headline.**

- **2026-08-06 (FUNDA "Deep|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", published 2026-08-05) — ADDITIVE ONLY. NOTHING SUPERSEDED: no number, rating, PT, estimate, guide or thesis on this page was changed or replaced.** **Added: 1 `## Current state` bullet · 1 `## Debate / thesis` bullet · 1 `## Catalysts / what to watch` bullet · 1 `## Risks` bullet · 1 Full-log row (08-05, capital/bull) · appended text to the "Capex / capital" and "Copilot / monetization" Sinal-vs-gestão rows and to the `**Síntese do quarter**` block · 1 `## Sources` entry.**
  **NET-NEW ON THIS PAGE — (1) THE TRANSMISSION CHAIN, with Copilot located at the CONVERSION step: "the transmission runs from rising token consumption to rising paid conversion for Microsoft Copilot, AWS Bedrock and Vertex AI, to higher cloud revenue and profit, to further capex increases." That reframes the >30M-paid-seat surprise as the monetisation of token growth originating in coding and coding-adjacent work rather than as standalone Copilot demand, and it makes the forward test the revenue MIX (seats vs consumption), not the seat count. (2) THE CAPEX-AS-OPTION FRAMEWORK: capex read as a dual investment in current cloud returns AND an option on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives — "for each frontier player, over-buying is the less costly error" — which is the first coherent statement on this page of why a payback-on-current-ARR test is mis-specified rather than merely harsh. (3) CONTINUAL LEARNING as the named milestone that unlocks enterprise adoption, with the TAM reference moving from a ~$1T software budget to a >$10T US white-collar wage bill and pricing migrating per-seat → per-task → per-outcome → per-unit-of-labor-replaced. (4) "EXPERIMENT COMPUTE" as a third demand category absent from every Azure capacity model here.**

Likewise FUNDA's Scenario B (RSI slipping to 2029+; digestion; utilisation and depreciation decide who is hurt first; levered weak-cash-flow players forced out) is the Redburn margin short and the Culpan late-binding thesis reached from the bull side — logged in `## Risks` for that reason.**

NOTHING SUPERSEDED; no rating, PT, estimate, guide or thesis changed.** Sources: **(1) AMD Q2 2026 earnings call, Bloomberg LIVE TRANSCRIPT (2026-08-04)** — MSFT is not the subject company; **(2) Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani et al., "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity" (2026-08-04)** — subject is the ERCOT audit and the miner/AI-infra cohort, not MSFT. **Added: 3 bullets in `## Current state` + 2 Full-log rows (08-04 produto/mixed off the AMD call; 08-04 supply/mixed off Bernstein) + 2 `## Sources` entries.**
  **What is net-new from the AMD call — the DEAL is not new, the RECORD is.** The 07-20 Azure/Helios announcement has been on this page since 07-20; what AMD added on 08-04 is that **Lisa Su named the workload and the customer scope in prepared remarks: "Microsoft will deploy Helios at scale on Azure for FRONTIER MODEL INFERENCING across Microsoft, its AI customers, and Azure AI services."** So: **inferencing, not training**, and a **three-part customer scope**. ⚠️ **LOGGED AS AN OPEN CONTRADICTION RATHER THAN RESOLVED: that framing is materially broader than the SemiAnalysis 07-24 read already on the page ("mainly for OpenAI as the end customer for Azure's MI455X racks"). AMD is the vendor and has an incentive to describe the widest scope; SemiAnalysis has been early on this relationship before. Neither was deleted or promoted; the named arbitrating observable is whether Azure exposes MI455X instance types to third-party customers.** Secondary, CPU-side: Microsoft named among the hyperscalers expanding EPYC across internal infrastructure and public cloud (with AWS/Google/Oracle), and **Turin now powers ~1/3 of the >1,600 EPYC public-cloud instance types globally**.
  **What is net-new from Bernstein — a price for hosted capacity, and it makes MSFT's existing Iren contract look cheap.** Exhibit 2: **IREN's revenue yield per IT MW +~50%, ~$10mn (Microsoft contract) → ~$15mn (NVIDIA contract); on a GROSS-MW basis ~$11mn (MSFT) → ~$14mn (NVDA) → ~$11mn (Enterprises)** — ⚠️ **two distinct bases, labelled on the page, never mixed.** Read: the ~$10B/~5yr Iren contract already on the page (Cantor 06-18) was struck at roughly two-thirds of today's clearing price per IT MW; renewals should be underwritten at the new level. Mechanism: **Abbott has directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit ALL data-center projects in the interconnection queue and ERCOT has PAUSED the Batch Zero review that was due to publish classification results on 2026-08-07**; the queue is **474 GW of REQUESTS with data centers ~90% of new requests** — ⚠️ **flagged on the page as a queued/requested figure, NOT comparable to energised / IT-load / facility GW (`_meta/assumptions.md` standing rule).** Bernstein's conclusion: the audit "throttles speculative data center pipeline and makes genuine sites with development history more valuable" → approved MW re-rate, time-to-power lengthens for new builds. **Miner tickers written as plain text (no wiki pages exist for WULF/CIFR/CORZ/CLSK/RIOT/IREN/MARA).** Cross-filed [[NVDA]], [[AMD]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).

- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — the full Azure driver decomposition, the backlog-ex-OpenAI split, and one dissenting first-party view on Microsoft's own AI products.** No rating or PT superseded. **The single most important datapoint for the whole AI complex this week: backlog $678bn with +$51bn of the q/q increase driven by a BROADENING CUSTOMER BASE — "real AI demand from NON-FRONTIER MODEL CUSTOMERS" — and JPM's independent cut, commercial RPO +84% q/q but +25% EXCLUDING OPENAI.** That separates genuine enterprise breadth from OpenAI-specific commitments, which is the objection the July de-rating was built on. **Azure's five drivers, net-new: ① 31 DATA CENTERS added in the quarter (~1GW), on track to double capacity in two years; ② a multi-model strategy (OpenAI, Anthropic, Mistral, xAI, potentially DeepSeek) plus own models (MAI-Think, Code-1 Flash, Image-2.5) optimised for MAIA 200 at ~40% better performance/watt, with a 5X YTD increase in customers using MULTIPLE model providers; ③ Maia 200 at ~30% better performance per dollar, and Microsoft among the first clouds deploying HELIOS and VERA RUBIN, with Cobalt CPU VMs running Adobe, Arm, Elastic and OpenAI workloads; ④ POSTGRESQL REVENUE +55%, ACCELERATING FOR A THIRD CONSECUTIVE QUARTER (the stickiness argument: "migrating away becomes expensive and disruptive"); ⑤ FOUNDRY >100,000 CUSTOMERS with revenue MORE THAN DOUBLED y/y, FABRIC at 40,000 customers +60% y/y. Applications: COPILOT SEATS MORE THAN DOUBLED SEQUENTIALLY, 5M → 10M** (BofA · Liani hosted call, 2026-07-30). ⚠️ **The competitive comparisons in that list are management's own claims (Copilot 10% lower token usage; Code1 Flash matching GPT-5.6 quality at lower cost; Cyber1 at half a rival's price) and are flagged as such.** **Cross-hyperscaler basis warnings added, and there are now three: (a) GCP's headline acceleration (+21pp vs AWS +9pp, Azure +4pp) benefits from a smaller base and easier comp; (b) IaaS/PaaS mix differs across the three so growth rates are not strictly comparable (Barclays · Lenschow, OW PT $512, 2026-07-31); (c) GOOGL books TPU system sales GROSS while AWS books Bedrock NET (MS, see [[AMZN]]).** Any cloud league table this quarter needs all three. **Capex facts: C2Q26 +29% q/q / +69% y/y to $41bn including leases, ~two-thirds on SHORT-LIVED assets; ~$190bn CY26 guide reaffirmed ex an accounting change; C3Q26 guided $50bn+; FY27 capex to grow y/y. ⚠️ Effective FY27 Microsoft is EXTENDING the useful life of data centres and office buildings from 15 to 25 YEARS — which flatters reported depreciation, and sits awkwardly beside management's flexibility argument that short-lived-asset mix means "spend can be slowed if the demand environment changes."** **Stock +17%, biggest gain since 2008, ~$450bn added — the most in market history.** ⚠️ **Dissenting first-party view logged rather than filtered out: Matt Velloso (CPO Docker; ex-Microsoft, DeepMind, Meta) argues Microsoft's AI success is largely "a reflection of OpenAI's success," that GitHub Copilot is "em implosão" once its subsidy was removed and is squeezed by discounted GPT models and cheaper Chinese models, and that generic Copilot has no relevant assistant market share — directly contradicting management's 10%-lower-token claim on the same product. Bias disclosed: he is CPO at Docker. Logged as a live disagreement, not resolved.** Cross-filed [[AMZN]], [[GOOG]], [[META]], [[OPENAI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[AMD]], [[NVDA]], [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).
