  **⚠️ THESIS-DRIFT — ONE GUIDE SUPERSEDED, and it is a basis change, not a spending change. OLD (original date 2026-04-29, Q3 FY26 call): "CY26 capex guided ~$190B, of which ~$25B is higher component pricing (memory)." NEW (2026-07-29): "approximately $175 billion."** The old $190B mark is recorded here for the record and is retained in place throughout the body as the *pre-reclassification* figure (it is the number every FY27 bogey on this page was struck against). Hood's exact mechanism: _"effective at the start of FY27, we are **extending the estimated useful life of our data centers and office buildings from 15 to 25 years**… The greater impact is on capital expenditures as **more of our future data center leases will shift from finance leases to operating leases**… **Finance leases are included in capital expenditures, while operating leases are not.** Outside of this useful life impact, **our calendar year 2026 CapEx investment expectations remain unchanged.** However, the shift from finance to operating leases adjusts our expectation to **approximately $175 billion**."_ **So committed spend is unchanged and only the reported line falls — which makes MSFT's capex non-comparable to the on-page FY27 bogeys (BofA $243.5B, UBS $255-260B, JPM ~$260B, MS/Altimeter CY27 $276B, all old-definition) and puts the 15→25-yr extension squarely on the Burry-style depreciation risk already under Risks (now on shells, not IT gear; management calls the FY27 OI benefit "minimal").** **NOTHING ELSE SUPERSEDED — no rating or PT changed by the call (MS OW $600, UBS Buy $480, DB Buy $550, Bernstein OP $646, Jefferies Buy $575, Redburn Neutral $400, Tigress Buy $680, JPM credit UW all stand as of this entry).** Estimates the print overtakes rather than supersedes (they were forecasts of exactly this quarter): UBS Q4E EPS $4.41 / cons $4.24 vs **actual $4.74 (≈$4.47 clean)**; UBS 4Q/Jun capex $41.8B vs **actual $41B**; UBS 4Q GM 66.2% / Cloud GM 64% vs **actual 67% / 65%**; UBS/BofA/MS Azure 40.7-41% vs **actual 43%**. All are left in place as dated marks.
  **⚠️ THE CAPEX-BASIS RECONCILIATION, RESOLVED AND FLAGGED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED (this was the explicit purpose of the patch).** The call: _"**Capital expenditures were $41 billion**… **Roughly two-thirds of our CapEx was for short-lived assets**, primarily CPUs and GPUs… **total finance leases were $5.6 billion**… **cash paid for PP&E was $35.8 billion**."_ **$35.8B + $5.6B = $41.4B, so the $41B is ALL-IN / lease-inclusive — verified off management's own component split, which is the answer to the open question ("does the $41B include finance leases or not?"): YES.** That makes it the **same conceptual basis** as the ~$42.7B this page computed from the 10-K on 07-29 (FY26 all-in ≈$140.6B = $115.9B cash additions + $24.6B finance-lease ROU obtained, **less** reported Q1-Q3 of $28.5B + $37.5B + $31.9B = $97.9B). **Therefore this is NOT the gross/net-basis artifact the UBS-$261B-all-in-vs-BofA-$243.5B spread on this page is** — the residual is **~$1.3B against the $41.4B components / ~$1.7B against the rounded $41B**, and the two most plausible causes are (a) the annual ASC-842 supplemental "**ROU assets obtained in exchange for finance-lease liabilities**" ($24.6B) being a broader measure than the sum of the four quarters' "total finance leases" management quotes on calls (Q4: $5.6B), and (b) rounding in each quarterly figure. **Resolution carried on the page: $41B is the reported 4QFY26 all-in capex (primary, management-stated, component-verified); the ~$42.7B remains explicitly labelled a COMPUTED PLUG from annual 10-K lines, not a disclosure. Both numbers stay on the page with their bases named — neither was deleted and neither was silently picked.**
  **The material new datapoints:** FY26 **revenue >$331B +18%, OI +21% to >$155B, Microsoft Cloud >$214B +27%, Azure through $100B +41%**. Q4: **revenue $90B +18% (+17% cc); GM 67%; OI +18%; OM 45% up y/y; opex +10%; headcount −2% y/y; EPS $4.74 +23% ex-OpenAI-impact — of which $0.27 is discrete vs the April guide ($3.2B Anthropic gain + lower voluntary-retirement expense, less severance + Xbox impairments), so ≈$4.47 clean; OI&E $2.8B "when adjusted for the impact of our investments in OpenAI," driven by the Anthropic gain; OCF $55.4B +30%; FCF POSITIVE $19.6B; $10.2B returned in Q4 / >$43B FY26. Azure +43% (cc ≈42%); Intelligent Cloud $39.3B +32%, OM flat at 41%; Microsoft Cloud $59.3B +27% at 65% GM (guide ~64%); commercial bookings +18% ex-OpenAI vs +10%/+11% cc incl.; commercial RPO $678B +84% / +25% ex-OpenAI, 2.3-yr duration, ~30% next-12M (+37% y/y), beyond-12M portion +112%, and "ALL sequential commercial RPO growth was driven by commitments from customers outside of Frontier model companies" with FY26 cloud revenue "nearly 90% from customers outside of Frontier model companies" — the sharpest management answer yet to the ~45%-of-RPO concentration bear. PBP $37.8B +14% (M365 CC +16% adj / +14% reported; M365 commercial products +19%; seats +6%; consumer cloud +24%; LinkedIn +12%; D365 +13% with ERP healthy / CRM moderating on longer sales cycles; OM 58%). MPC $12.9B −4%, ahead because "OEM and channel partners continued to build inventory given increasing component prices" (a pull-forward the FY27 −high-teens Windows guide then pays for); Xbox −10% with impairments, but management "expect[s] to return the business to growth in fiscal 2027."** Product/platform: **>30M paid M365 Copilot seats with net adds more than doubling q/q** (vs >20M at Q3, the 25M+ guide, MS's 6mn+ bogey, FUNDA's 37.0mn model); **>50k-seat customers +7x y/y; majority-of-information-worker deployments +~75% q/q; latency −25%; NHS England 505,000 seats, KPMG >276,000, HSBC 200,000, EY E7 to 400,000; E7 "millions of seats" across "hundreds of enterprise customers"; GitHub Copilot 50M users with Copilot revenue "accelerated over 60% quarter-over-quarter"; GitHub 225M users, 1 in 3 PRs involves an agent; 100,000 Foundry customers, revenue >2x y/y, 1T-token-run-rate customers +4x y/y; Agent 365 ~40M registered agents; 40,000+ paid Fabric customers +>60%; PostgreSQL revenue +55% accelerating a 3rd quarter; Cowork GA with usage-based billing live; Purview 50B+ audited Copilot interactions +~360%; Project Perception on consumption pricing; Microsoft Frontier Co (6,000 embedded experts, 330+ projects / 164 customers).** Capacity: **+1GW this quarter, 31 new data centers (88 for the year), "roughly double our overall capacity in just two years," dock-to-live −~50% over FY26, Copilot throughput 4x.** Silicon/models: **Maia 200 at 30% better perf/$ "now supporting both OpenAI and MAI models"** (first Maia workload disclosure here) **+40% better perf/watt on MAI; Cobalt 200 in 25+ DCs; first-mover on AMD Helios and NVIDIA Vera Rubin; 11,000+ model catalog; multi-provider builders +5x YTD; Levi Strauss on OpenAI+Anthropic across 1,000+ agents; MAI Code 1 Flash ~10% lower median tokens in GitHub Copilot and GPT-5.6-comparable in Excel for less; Second material item: **BofA's FY26-FY29 aggregate FCF forecast has been cut from $445B a year ago to $231B (-48%)**, with the trough at **$32B in FY27** before recovering to $51B/$86B in FY28/FY29 — still positive, which is the framing Fenske insists on. Third: **R&Co Redburn's roadshow feedback supplies the first published per-100bps GM elasticity on this page — every 100bps of mix shift toward Azure Gen-AI takes ~77bps off cloud gross margin** — inside a three-tier ladder (**~66% Cloud 1.0 → ~40% all-in incremental → ~12-13% pure Gen-AI**) that puts the ~13% Gen-AI margin already on file (07-20) into legible mix arithmetic. **Deliberately logged as two-sided, not smoothed:** the same Redburn note that is the most bearish mark on MSFT cloud margin is also the most dismissive of the token-substitution threat — **~98% of tokens in Haissl's own Claude Code usage were stored-cache reads (<2% fresh input)**, so open-weight models are "**only 2x cheaper when you adjust for cache**," directly contesting the headline all-in benchmark spreads now filed on [[ANTHROPIC]]. Azure-bogey spread also recorded: **UBS 41%/42% (FQ4/FQ1 guide) vs BofA 40.7%/41.4%** — a ~0.5pt gap, immaterial to the thesis but a reminder the "bar" is a range, not a number. Buy-side sentiment stays the weakest in UBS's preview set at **5/10**. Positioning read is contradictory across houses and left that way: BofA flows show **LOs trimming MSFT since ~Dec-2025**, MS has **HFs at a 12-month-low MAG7 exposure (10th %ile)** with room to re-engage, and in MS's own EU client poll **MSFT drew zero votes as a favourite MAG7 name** while being called "a compelling entry but needs more product proof points." MS models **Azure AI ~100% y/y (~18% q/q, ~$1,260mn q/q dollars added, ~19pts of contribution)**. **The framing worth carrying forward:** CFO Amy Hood has said Azure guidance is an **"allocated capacity guide"** — reported growth is governed by **datacenter buildout and GPU-deployment timing becoming revenue-additive, not by demand** — and incremental capacity must be split across **external Azure, first-party AI apps, and internal R&D/training**, which structurally caps the size of quarterly upside surprises. Baseline from last quarter: Azure **+39% cc** (above the ~38% high end), **~160bps y/y op-margin expansion**, **RPO +110% y/y to ~$625bn**, **cRPO +39% y/y**. Copilot reframed as a **three-pronged ARPU story — seats, the E7 SKU, and consumption pricing** ("one of the largest ARPU expansion opportunities in MSFT history"). Channel checks constructive across Azure/M365/Security, with **one partner flagging capacity constraints limiting deployment pace** (consistent with management) and **Copilot adoption "progressing gradually, with awareness continuing to outpace enterprise-wide deployment."** **MS 2Q26 CIO survey:** software spend accelerating to **+4.1% in 2026** (from +3.8%); MSFT the **highest forward spend intentions at +7.6%** (from +7.3%); **72% of CIOs increasing MSFT spend**; Azure the largest expected cloud share gainer over both 1yr and 3yr; MSFT the largest expected beneficiary of incremental GenAI spend and preferred platform for agentic automation and custom AI apps. **Two-sided item logged rather than smoothed over:** the same survey shows **both MSFT and AMZN ceding budget share to LLM providers** on 1-year and medium-term views — MSFT wins the vendor race while the category leaks to the model layer. On capex: **"Consensus capex estimates could move higher; we see less upside to our higher-than-street capex estimates,"** with **capex-growth moderation named as important for ROIC and the shares**, against a plan to **roughly double the datacenter footprint over two years**. Valuation: **~16x FY28 GAAP EPS**, and a 1.2x PEG / 25x PE (a discount to peers) supports **50% upside to the $600 PT**. **Counterweight added — JPM · Schilsky TECH SKETCH (07-23): "MSFT Q4 Preview – No Model, No Chip, No Hope?"** versus GOOGL where "Cloud [is] Now a TPU Story" — logged deliberately alongside the MS note because the two argue different things: MS underwrites **allocated-capacity Azure growth + Copilot ARPU**, while the bear questions **strategic position in the model and silicon layers**. The partial answer already on this page: the **07-20 Helios commitment** gives MSFT a second merchant accelerator source ([[AMD]]) and [[OPENAI]] supplies the model layer commercially. UBS calls this "new" vs 1-2 years of checks, can't source the cause (fewer capacity adds than the prior ~1GW/quarter vs more allocated internally), and cross-checks it against its own ~130-enterprise survey where <10% report material GPU constraints — i.e. Microsoft-specific, not industry-wide. M365 Copilot: >30M paid seats with net adds MORE THAN DOUBLING q/q — clears the 25M+ guide and the MS 6mn+-net-adds bogey, lands under FUNDA's 37.0mn channel model (07-27); >50k-seat customers +7x y/y, majority-of-information-workers deployments +~75% q/q, latency −25%, satisfaction at an all-time high, time-to-high-usage "from months to just days." E7 two months in: "hundreds of enterprise customers… millions of seats," EY at 400,000 employees. ⚠️ The contradictions on this page are NOT resolved and stay flagged: UBS's #1 partner still hit 14% of its Copilot quota, Partner 7 still says the M365 driver "is not actually Copilot" but E3→E5, E7 is still reported as discount-led with minimal ARPU uplift, and FUNDA still has multistep tasks at possibly 1-2% of usage and expects FY27Q1 Copilot sales materially weaker on seasonality — against which management guides M365 CC to ~+16% cc with "acceleration in commercial cloud revenue growth through this fiscal year." A print this strong on seats does not reconcile the partner-quota evidence; both stay on the page.** |

| 07-29 | **MSFT 4QFY26 call · Nadella/Hood** (the print) | capital | mixed | **★ CAPEX: $41B in the quarter, and the CY26 headline drops ~$190B → ~$175B on an ACCOUNTING RECLASSIFICATION, not a spending cut. THESIS-DRIFT: the ~$190B CY26 guide (Q3 FY26, 04-29) is superseded on a reclassified basis — old value in the Changelog.** Hood: _"**Capital expenditures were $41 billion**, including the impact from higher component pricing as noted in our guide. **Roughly two-thirds of our CapEx was for short-lived assets**, primarily CPUs and GPUs… **total finance leases were $5.6 billion**… **cash paid for PP&E was $35.8 billion**."_ **$35.8B + $5.6B = $41.4B → the $41B is ALL-IN (lease-inclusive), the same basis as this page's computed ~$42.7B 10-K plug** — see the dedicated basis-reconciliation bullet in Current state; the ~$1.3-1.7B residual is a build-up artifact (annual ASC-842 "ROU assets obtained" ≠ the sum of four quarters' quoted "total finance leases", plus rounding), **NOT** a gross/net difference. UBS modelled $41.8B — essentially on it. **The reclassification: effective FY27 MSFT extends the estimated useful life of data centers and office buildings from 15 to 25 years; "the greater impact is on capital expenditures as more of our future data center leases will shift from finance leases to operating leases… Finance leases are included in capital expenditures, while operating leases are not. Outside of this useful life impact, our calendar year 2026 CapEx investment expectations remain unchanged. However, the shift from finance to operating leases adjusts our expectation to approximately $175 billion."** ⚠️ **So the number falls while the obligation does not — and MSFT's capex line is now LESS comparable to the FY27 bogeys on this page ($243.5B BofA / $255-260B UBS / ~$260B JPM, all struck on the old definition).** The useful-life extension is also the Burry-style depreciation charge already under Risks, now taken on DC/office shells; management pre-empts with "minimal benefit to FY27 operating income." **Q1FY27 capex "over $50 billion, including the lease reclassification impact"; FY27 capex "will grow year-over-year given demand signals across our portfolio" — NO dollar figure, and no comment on the ~$20B component pre-buy in the 10-K or on debt issuance.** On overbuild (Moerdler/Bernstein Q), Hood makes the short-lived mix the hedge: _"if the demand environment changes, **you just slow down what is in fact the largest component**… land and data center builds… **a smaller percentage of the overall cost structure**… you have the ability to **late bind** some of the more expensive components."_ On component inflation: _"impacting everybody equivalently… the cloud offers tremendous benefits versus… servers on-prem yourself where the **price increases are even more hard for customers**… a lot of this obviously is also being sold in **newer contracts and we're able to have the pricing reflected**."_ Satya, cycle-aware: _"**All of us are reading this 1873 as the book to be read**… there will be **ups and downs of what is the cycle here, but the secular shift is clear**."_ On ROI (Borges/GS Q) Hood declines to claim improvement — _"I don't know that quite frankly, my math has changed"_ — and closes on _"**this is the grind work**."_ (MSFT 4QFY26 earnings call, 2026-07-29) |

| 07-29 | **MSFT 4QFY26 call · Nadella/Hood** (the print) | supply | mixed | **Capacity: another gigawatt added, 31 new data centers this quarter (88 for the year across five continents), "on track to roughly double our overall capacity in just two years"** — the ~1GW/quarter cadence UBS could not verify in its 07-26 checks is confirmed by management for this quarter. Two-sided: the *same* note dismisses the token-substitution bear — cost-per-task not sticker price, **~98% of tokens in his own Claude Code usage were stored-cache reads (<2% fresh input)**, open-weight models repeat failed tasks, so they are "**only 2x cheaper when you adjust for cache**." | 07-23 | MS · Adam Wood / Josh Baer / Jonathan Eisenson ("4Q26 Preview – Time to Take Back the Narrative") | guidance | **bull** | **OW, PT $600. The most detailed FQ4 preview on the page, with explicit bogeys.** Thesis: "**Low-40s Azure growth, accelerating Copilot monetization and disciplined opex should reinforce MSFT's durable high-teens total return profile**," and at **~16x FY28 GAAP EPS** risk/reward is "highly attractive" (a 1.2x PEG / 25x PE — a discount to peers — yields **50% upside to the $600 PT**). **The three bogeys:** (1) **Azure ~41% cc growth in Q4** — a **1pt beat vs the 39-40% cc guide high end** — against a 4pt tougher comp; consensus is **40.5% as-reported, ~$3.3bn q/q dollars added**. (2) **A reiterated F1H27 guide for "modest acceleration," i.e. 42%+ cc.** (3) **6mn+ Copilot seat net adds** on top of **20mn paid seats**. **The crucial framing to carry:** CFO Amy Hood has said Azure guidance should be read as an **"allocated capacity guide"** — reported growth is **governed by the pace of datacenter buildouts and GPU deployments becoming revenue-additive, not by demand**, and incremental capacity must be split across **external Azure, first-party AI apps, and internal R&D/training**. MS expects **Azure AI ~100% y/y (~18% q/q, ~$1,260mn q/q dollars added, ~19pts of contribution)**. Last quarter's baseline: Azure **+39% cc** (above the ~38% high end), ~160bps y/y operating-margin expansion, **RPO +110% y/y to ~$625bn** and **cRPO +39% y/y**. Copilot is now framed as a **three-pronged ARPU story — seats, the E7 SKU, and consumption pricing** — "one of the largest ARPU expansion opportunities in MSFT history." Channel checks support it: partners constructive on Azure, M365, Security and AI workloads, with **one partner flagging capacity constraints limiting deployment pace** (consistent with management), while **Copilot adoption is "progressing gradually, with awareness continuing to outpace enterprise-wide deployment."** MS's **2Q26 CIO survey**: software spend accelerating to **+4.1% in 2026** (from +3.8%), MSFT highest forward spend intentions at **+7.6%**, **72% of CIOs increasing MSFT spend**, Azure the largest expected cloud share gainer over 1yr and 3yr, and MSFT the largest expected beneficiary of incremental GenAI spend plus preferred platform for agentic automation and custom AI apps. **Two-sided item worth flagging:** the same survey shows **both MSFT and AMZN ceding budget share to LLM providers** on a 1-year and medium-term view — i.e. MSFT leads the cloud/GenAI vendor race while the *category* leaks to the model layer. On capex: **"Consensus capex estimates could move higher; we see less upside to our higher-than-street capex estimates"** — and MS explicitly names **capex-growth moderation as important for ROIC and the shares**, with MSFT executing a plan to **roughly double its datacenter footprint over the next two years**. |

Buy-side models cluster **low-40s for 2HC26** and Ruple hasn't heard anyone walking that back, so base case is a reaffirm; **visibility into the mid-40s by FQ2 (Dec) would be upside to models, "but not sure this would create durable follow through on the stock."** **Capex is the pressure point:** the guided **$190B CY26 implies ~$60B per quarter in C2H**; the buy-side has annualised that to **~$240-250B in FY27**, and — with memory prices "going straight up" — the expectation is now that Microsoft must either **raise the $190B CY26 number or guide FY27 above $240B**, which "raises concerns on both margin/ROI and financing." **M365 Commercial Cloud is the underrated swing:** any sign that growth is inflecting higher on Copilot "would probably be the most durable positive for the stock" — and UBS notes this is **neither expected by the buy-side nor in UBS's own model**. Copilot seat guide **25M+, up from 20M prior**; FQ4 capex guided **>$40B**; FY27 framing of double-digit revenue and operating-profit growth with headcount reductions and 4-9% opex growth is expected to stand. The bull/bear framing UBS lands on: bulls need MSFT to **stand up incremental capacity fast enough to feed internal and external monetisation** while absorbing token optimisation, open-source models and the evolving OpenAI arrangement; bears just point at capex stifling otherwise-improving growth. | **09-16** | 🔴 **MS · Adam Wood / Jonathan Eisenson / Kathleen Keyser — "Dividend Increases 8%, Adding to Attractive Total Return" · OW, PT $600** | capital | **bull** | **Quarterly dividend raised 7 cents (+7.7%) to **$0.98/share**, $3.92 annualised, ~0.8% yield (from ~0.7%). MS flags that this is BELOW the ~10% average of the prior five years and equals **~0.4x trailing operating-income growth of 21% in FY26 versus a ~1.1x long-term average** — read as a deliberate choice to retain balance-sheet flexibility against the AI capex ramp.** **Capital return in full: **~$17bn of buyback in FY26 (from $13bn in FY25)**, **>$40bn remaining of the $60bn authorisation** as of 2026-06-30, **$76.8bn of cash and short-term investments** at FY26-end.** **And the forecast underneath the call: **~21% revenue CAGR through CY28**, gross profit +~18% despite higher AI COGS, **opex growing only ~11%**, driving **~19% EPS CAGR** and a ~20% total-return profile.** ⚠️ The dividend is the smallest part of that; the load-bearing assumption is opex growing at half the rate of revenue while capex stays elevated |

**Quarter synthesis:** the print was a "clearing event" for Azure (Citi) — organic acceleration via efficiency is confirmed. The capital debate dominates (CY26 ~$190B capex, FY27 $240-276B, FCF negative, bond supply in C2H26). Right after the print, Barron's (Serwer, 05-18 issue) crystallized the single cleanest Street split of the quarter: **Ackman built a new "core holding" the same week Chris Hohn/TCI sold most of an $8B, 10-year MSFT stake on AI-competitive-position uncertainty** — plus two risk items not otherwise on file, the **Musk v. OpenAI suit threatening the ~27%/$230B OpenAI stake** and an internal MS Research paper flagging **agentic-delegation document-corruption risk**. On Jun-24, QCOM HBC on Azure + Jalapeño IP ownership through 2031 added structural silicon advantages. New from 06-27: (1) **Azure high-40s tracking** — if @SouthernValue95's color is right, the Sep quarter beats guidance, removing the deceleration bear thesis; (2) **Anthropic Head of Infra hire + Foundry external sales** — converts the Anthropic cloud commitment into active distribution, a new monetization vector; (3) **GitHub reliability improved** (SemiAnalysis) — removes the downtime friction point for enterprise Copilot scale; (4) **Xbox memory price hike** — confirms both the scope of DRAM inflation and MSFT's willingness to pass through costs. On 06-29, Barron's flagged Chevron's signed **20-year data-center power supply deal** with MSFT — firms up the datacenter-power catalyst beyond the CCGT FID already on file. Net: execution improving on the product/distribution dimensions; capital outflow and the OpenAI-stake legal/competitive overhang remain the dominant constraints on the multiple. **06-30:** The Information reports Anthropic bringing a **Claude agent to Teams** — MSFT taking a platform/app-store posture (Teams as the agent surface even for a Copilot rival): bull for Teams engagement/Graph-data moat, a nuance on ceding the agent layer. **07-06:** The Information quantifies the layoffs first flagged 07-02 — **~4,800 cuts (~2% of workforce)**, mostly sales + Xbox/gaming, 4-5 gaming studios sold, and (with ~1,600 more gaming cuts over the next year) **~20% of the gaming unit** gone; new gaming chief Asha Sharma calls the gaming business "not healthy." Cost discipline supports the flat-FY27-op-margin commitment, but is also a candid admission of gaming weakness within More Personal Computing. **07-08:** GS (Borges) frames a **positive risk/reward into the 7/29 4QFY print** — MSFT "best-positioned in coverage to compound AI product cycles" — expecting 4QFY Azure 40-41% cc (>guide) and 1QFY guide 40-41% vs Street 41%, and raising FY28-30 capex; the stock's -5%-since-3Q-EPS underperformance is pinned on three bear worries (capex-up-without-Azure-revision, NVIDIA/memory exposure, Office 365 disintermediation from Claude Cowork with Copilot perceived to lag) that a clean print can neutralize. Minor: MS/Wigg listed "MSFT tokenmining" among TMT headlines (07-08, color); JPM's Chips for Breakfast desk flagged **IG credit downgrades of MSFT/META** (07-09) — a marginal credit datapoint that dovetails with the "laggard pre-funder"/C2H26-bond-supply theme. **07-12:** BofA's buyside bogey poll (Fenske, "The Big Bogey Edition") shows the live Azure debate has moved — the buyside Azure bogey has ratcheted from ~38.4% into the April print to **4QFY26 40.7% / 1QFY27 guide 41.4%**, so even GS/Borges's expected 40-41% 4QFY print now only meets (rather than clears) the bar; buyside capex bogeys FY27 $243.5B / FY28 $276.9B / FY29 $289.0B (EPS $19.95/$22.87 FY27/28) corroborate the capex-up/FCF-negative capital debate. **07-20 (hyperscaler-cross-note flow into the 7/29 print):** three same-day desks frame the setup — **Rothschild & Co Redburn (Haissl/Han) reiterates Neutral with a $400 PT** on a "Margin Call" thesis (Intelligent Cloud GM -680bp since Dec-24 → the Gen-AI book implies ~13% margin vs an assumed 30% floor; RDBN EPS below consensus); **JPM (Anmuth) DOWNGRADES MSFT credit to underweight** — the "next up" hyperscaler to issue debt for the first time in ~a decade, with CY26 $190bn capex implying a ~$250bn FY27 run-rate; and **BofA (Post) frames it mixed** — Azure guide 39-40% cc in line, but the stock needs RPO→revenue conversion (25% of $630bn over NTM) and Copilot >5M net adds to re-rate, with FCF compressing $25.5bn→$14.4bn. Counterweight on the buy-side: **Octahedron Capital (07-15 LP call) built a new 5-10% position**, calling MSFT the "cheapest multiple I've seen in history" (~16.5x CY28 vs ~17.8x cons). Net: the margin/credit/FCF bear case has sharpened right into the print even as a fundamental long steps in on valuation. **06-08 (primary IR):** the Jefferies-hosted IR call with IR head Jonathan Neilson (the source behind the 06-12 JEF NDR recap already on the page) is a straight confirmation of management's own framing — Copilot demand-signal improving (guided >5M Q4 net seat adds, weekly engagement = Outlook, "headless office" per-user+consumptive as the best-ARPU TAM); Azure capacity-constrained with accel efficiency-driven and capacity balanced *within* Azure between the AI labs (OpenAI $250B / Anthropic $30B) and the sticky enterprise long-tail; power/shell space the #1 constraint "at least through end-CY26"; capex $190B "same ballpark as Google/Amazon," no cash-flow line, AAA-rated and "open to any tool"; memory the bulk of the $25B component add (levers: efficiency, less discounting, new-SKU pricing incl. an Intel V7 CPU SKU priced above V6, list price); OpenAI rev-share removal in COGS (not the Azure-accel driver) and no longer AGI-tied; GitHub "a touch late" to token pricing and explicitly anti-"token maxxing"; and own-models progress ("we don't pay model rents to OpenAI today. But we will eventually"). **07-24 (SemiAnalysis batch — three reads, one of them a genuine reframe):** the 07-20 AMD "Helios" diversification headline gets materially thinner on closer inspection — SemiAnalysis notes MSFT **dropped AMD as a customer after MI300X in 2023** (unreliable Samsung 2023 HBM + poor software quality) and **skipped both MI325X and MI355X**, and believes the new MI455X Helios deployment is "**mainly for OpenAI as the end customer for Azure's MI455X racks**," i.e. anchor-tenant capacity rather than a second-sourcing of the Azure fleet (SemiAnalysis's belief, not company disclosure). That squares with the 07-13 Archera read (Azure most constrained on third-party inference *because* it is Nvidia-only with no ASIC to offload internal demand) and lends weight to JPM Schilsky's 07-23 "No Model, No Chip, No Hope?" framing — a new **Accelerator diversification** row has been added to the Signal-vs-management table at **⚠ nuance** to hold this. Second, the harshest independent mark on the applications leg: SemiAnalysis (07-09) writes that "**Microsoft has completely blown their early lead with GitHub copilot and failed to effectively leverage their access to OpenAI IP**," inside a piece framing frontier AI as a two-horse race between OpenAI and Anthropic — logged as attributed opinion, and set explicitly against MS's own CIO survey showing GitHub Copilot penetration going **42% → 58%** (07-16) and Bernstein's ~1B → ≥14B commit ramp (06-22), so the disagreement stays visible rather than reconciled. Third, light: SemiAnalysis (07-06) notes colocation operators would rather write **10-15-year offtakes with a hyperscaler like Microsoft directly** than rent to a neocloud, and flags the paradox of "**Neoclouds with a Microsoft offtake with the capacity ultimately being used by an AI lab**" — the financing-side mirror of the Azure-margin-dilution point already on file. Two backfill markers were added as clearly-dated history, **not** current reads: as of **Q2-2025, "all of OpenAI's >$10B cloud spending is booked by Azure"** and "Azure's AI outperformance over peers is **entirely** driven by its OpenAI partnership" (Anthropic's cloud spend then >2x smaller than OpenAI's) — a clean 100%-capture baseline against which today's OpenAI multi-sourcing can be measured; and a floor-setting marker that hyperscaler custom-silicon programs incl. **Maia** run **$300-500M+/yr of cycle-uncorrelated EDA spend** with systems companies now ~45% of EDA demand (which sizes the commitment, not the outcome — the "Maia dead last" read is unchanged). **07-27 (the UBS 07-26 note read in full for the first time — `ued48088` — and the field work changes one verdict):** the PT/capex/M365 headlines were already on this page from the Outlook relay; the primary PDF adds the ~dozen-conversation Evidence Lab base underneath them, and it is more cautious than the marks. **Azure moves from ✓ confirms to ⚠ nuance**: for the first time in UBS's checks, capacity rationing is landing on *external* customers — "no capacity in certain regions or it is 20% higher cost" for all-in-Microsoft accounts, F500 customers "get pushback" on expansion, and one partner's (explicitly non-Microsoft-sourced) read is that MSFT is "reserving capacity for AI rather than enterprise customers," with that same partner not raising its 2H Azure targets and "really pushing Google to diversify against Microsoft." The honest cross-check is on the page too: UBS's own ~130-enterprise survey has **<10% flagging material GPU constraints**, so this is Microsoft-specific, and UBS left its Azure numbers unchanged. This is the primary-evidence version of the same constraint Business Insider (07-27) and Archera (07-13) describe. Second, the two Copilots now diverge and should be modelled separately: **GitHub Copilot's June-1 credit repricing is beating plan hard** (a large bank's spend "up 10-20x — yes, 10-20 times and not %"; FY27 revs raised $1.57b → $1.95b) even as the product loses developer mindshare ("it's mostly Claude Code and Codex"), while **M365 Copilot is steady-not-inflecting** — the partner ranked #1 on Microsoft's own attainment scoreboard hit **14% of its Copilot quota** — and Partner 7 argues the M365 growth driver "is not actually Copilot" but the E3→E5→E7 ladder. Which points at the note's real thesis: **the M365 upside is a pricing event, not a product event** — 468.4m paid O365 seats × a 13.8% weighted-average July-1 increase = **227bps of O365 Commercial / 160bps of M365 CC / 43bps of total revenue growth in FY27**, with the Nov-2025 EA-discount elimination (5-15% net) on top and unmodelled. Third, a definitional fix that matters for the bogey debate on this page: **UBS's $261B FY27 capex is all-in ($209.0bn cash + $51.6bn capital leases)**, so it is not comparable with BofA's $243.5B buy-side bogey or the $230-235B sell-side consensus, and it is why UBS models FY27 FCF at ~break-even **+$3bn** rather than April's -$21bn. Fourth, UBS supplies a direct counter to the Redburn margin short: 3Q/Mar GM fell only **110bps y/y on $37bn (11% of revs) of AI revenue**, and "we've made the same observation about the margin performance at AWS, Oracle and others — on the surface, AI capex/revs are not landing at the abysmal margin levels that the bears feared" — while conceding the 4Q guide (GM 66.2%, Cloud GM 64%) is worse than it looks, because it already includes the **OpenAI rev-share removal, which sat in COGS rather than contra-revenue** and is therefore a GM *tailwind* from 4Q/Jun.

**Síntese do quarter — POST-PRINT (4QFY26 reported 2026-07-29 AMC; MSFT 4QFY26 earnings call, Nadella/Hood):** **the print resolved the quarter's central debate in the bulls' favour on the axis the page said mattered most, and moved the bear case onto a different question.** **Azure printed +43% (cc ≈42%) against a 39-40% cc guide, a 40.7% BofA buyside bogey and a 41% UBS/MS mark — and then guided Q1FY27 to ~+45% cc against a 41.4-42% bogey. That is a beat AND a ~3pt raise, i.e. the outcome UBS explicitly did *not* underwrite ("capacity constraints on external customers and early token optimization efforts could limit the potential for material Azure guidance upside") and the one it had pre-committed to reading benignly in its weaker form.** Hood's causal account is the same efficiency mechanism the page has carried since April — CPU/GPU fleet efficiency plus faster dock-to-live, _"quickly monetized in the quarter"_ — with **~1GW added again this quarter (31 new data centers, 88 for the year) and dock-to-live down nearly 50% over the fiscal year**, which confirms the ~1GW/quarter cadence UBS could not verify in its checks. **Crucially the constraint is reaffirmed, not retired** (_"demand continues to exceed available supply… you can even see it… in some of the pricing that's occurring in the spot market for assets"_), and the call is silent on the external-customer rationing evidenced by UBS's Partner 6/Partner 2 and by Business Insider — **so that evidence stands unresolved rather than refuted: Azure grew 43% anyway.** **On the two Copilots the page had split into a divergence, both printed strong: M365 Copilot >30M paid seats with net adds more than doubling q/q (above the 25M+ guide and MS's 6mn+ bogey, below FUNDA's 37.0mn model), and GitHub Copilot revenue accelerating >60% q/q on the June repricing.** That is the company-level corroboration of UBS's "up 10-20x" bank anecdote — **and it leaves the ugly partner-quota evidence (the #1 partner at 14% of Microsoft's Copilot plan; "regular Copilot is stale"; E7 bought on 10-50% discounts) unreconciled and deliberately still on the page.** **The strategy narrative BofA said would decide the re-rating was answered with MAI, not DeepSeek** — 11,000+ models with multi-provider builders up 5x, MAI Code 1 Flash at ~10% lower median tokens inside GitHub Copilot and at GPT-5.6-comparable quality in Excel for less, MAI Cyber 1 Flash beating Anthropic's Mythos at half the cost via a 90%-cheap/10%-frontier routing split, and 84-89% GPU-cost reductions in PowerPoint/D365 — with the durable framing being that **the harness, context, memory and action space sit outside any one model family so "every model is substitutable."** Read against the bears: this is not a frontier model (SemiAnalysis 07-09, JPM/Schilsky 07-23 remain literally correct) but it **is** a cost structure MSFT is willing to aim at its own suppliers, which is bullish MSFT margin and bearish [[OPENAI]]/[[ANTHROPIC]] share of Microsoft's own token spend. **Maia also got its first real workload disclosure — 30% better perf/$ and "now supporting both OpenAI and MAI models"** — which nuances but does not overturn Fubon's ~1%-of-CoWoS mark or the "dead last" read. **The concentration bear lost ground on disclosure: RPO $678B +84% but "all sequential commercial RPO growth was driven by commitments from customers outside of Frontier model companies," +25% ex-OpenAI, and FY26 cloud revenue "nearly 90% from customers outside of Frontier model companies," with bookings +18% ex-OpenAI vs +10% including it — OpenAI is now the drag on the reported rate, not the flatterer.** **Margins compressed as guided but beat the guide everywhere (Cloud GM 65% vs ~64%, company GM 67% vs 66.2%, OM 45% up y/y vs 44.0%, IC OM flat at 41%), FCF stayed POSITIVE at $19.6B on OCF $55.4B +30%, and FY27 is guided FCF-positive with operating margins "down less than a point"** — which removes the near-term catalyst from Redburn's margin short (Haissl's stated buy trigger, "when capex guidance gets cut," did not fire) without disproving the ~77bps-per-100bps elasticity. ⚠️ **Two things to carry forward as the new live debates, because both are basis/disclosure questions rather than growth questions.** **(1) The capex headline is now an accounting artifact: CY26 goes ~$190B → ~$175B purely because the 15→25-year useful-life extension pushes future DC leases from finance leases (in capex) to operating leases (not in capex), with "calendar year 2026 CapEx investment expectations remain unchanged."** Q4 capex was **$41B all-in** ($35.8B cash PP&E + $5.6B finance leases — verified against management's own components, hence **the same lease-inclusive basis as this page's computed ~$42.7B 10-K plug, with the ~$1.3-1.7B residual an annual-vs-quarterly build-up artifact, NOT a gross/net difference**), Q1FY27 is guided **">$50B including the lease reclassification impact,"** and **FY27 carries no dollar figure at all — only "will grow year-over-year given demand signals."** So every FY27 bogey on this page ($243.5B BofA / $255-260B UBS / ~$260B JPM) was struck on a definition Microsoft has just changed, and **the useful-life extension is itself the Burry-style depreciation charge already under Risks, now taken on shells rather than IT gear.** **(2) The EPS headline needs unpacking every time it is used: $4.74 (+23% ex-OpenAI-impact) includes $0.27 of discrete benefit vs the April guide — a $3.2B gain on the Anthropic investment plus lower voluntary-retirement expense, less severance and Xbox impairments — so the clean number is ≈$4.47 vs UBS $4.41 and consensus $4.24.** A real beat, roughly a third the size the headline implies, and one whose largest single component is **a private-company mark, not operating income.** Net: **execution and the guide are unambiguously better than the setup priced (stock had been -21% YTD, sentiment 5/10, HFs at 12-month-low MAG7 exposure, MSFT drawing zero votes as a favourite in MS's EU client poll) — and the bear case now has to be made on capex definition, depreciation policy and the quality of the OI&E line rather than on Azure deceleration or Copilot failure.**

EPS 06/26E $16.95 unch / 06/27E $19.49 → $19.26 / 06/28E $22.68 → $22.12; equity FCF yield 0.1% in 06/27E; net cash → net debt by 06/28E. Built on ~a dozen customer/partner conversations plus the ~130-enterprise Evidence Lab AI survey: external-customer Azure capacity rationing flagged as "new" (<10% of survey respondents report material GPU constraints, so Microsoft-specific); GitHub Copilot consumption repricing running 10-20x at one bank (FY27 revs $1.57b → $1.95b); M365 Copilot quota attainment weak at partners (#1 partner at 14% of plan); Cowork constructive but consumption pricing "a non-starter"; E7 bought on 10-50% discounts. The M365 case is a pricing case: 468.4m paid O365 seats × 13.8% weighted-average July-1 increase = 227bps O365 / 160bps M365 CC / 43bps total revenue growth in FY27.

Capacity: **+2GW in FY25, ~+1GW in each of the last three quarters, "fair to think upwards of three added in this year," "halfway through" the two-year doubling, not delayed, dock-to-live times falling** — but **the base footprint has never been disclosed**, so the doubling cannot be converted to an absolute GW level. Demand "incredibly strong," **>350 customers on track to process >1 trillion tokens this year**, pulling the full stack (agentic-memory storage, Cosmos/SQL, migrations) so "AI strengthens the case for moving to the public cloud"; IR refuses the AI/non-AI split. Margins: **no reason to believe long-term Azure margins "look materially different with AI in the picture than without"**, via pricing levers (discount-rate discipline, new SKUs at current pricing, tranche repricing inside long contracts — "not all price is locked in") and efficiency ("the capacity today is as inefficient as it will ever be"), concluding "cloud revenue will outpace cloud capex"; **the OpenAI revenue-share cessation came out of COGS and is already "embedded in… fiscal year Q4 or calendar year Q2"** (⚠️ not an incremental FY27 tailwind). Foundry: **>11,000 models**, fine-tuning, "it's not just about the model, it's really about the harness"; multi-model justified by cost-per-outcome + business continuity. Copilot: record usage, **E7 "only been in market for a couple of months and we already have millions of seats,"** ROI question shifted from "is this worth it" to "what's the right way to start," ~85% monthly-active ramp compressing from months to days. Capex: **"what I would not think about that as is us putting like a line in the sand"** — invest to demand/usage/unit economics — while **FCF (defined as operating cash flow minus cash paid for PP&E, so unaffected by the lease reclassification) stays positive for the year**. ⚠️ IR commentary, not guidance; no revenue-per-GW, no Foundry pricing uplift, no guide beyond the quarter. ⚠️ Transcript is ASR — one M365/Copilot seat figure is garbled and was not adopted (see `## Changelog`).

Morgan · Samik Chatterjee — "Acceleration in AI Infra and Copilot, Along with Balanced Mix, to Position Shares Favorably Within Peer Group" (2026-08-13)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_Microsoft_Accelerati_2026-08-13_5348619.html) — **Overweight maintained, Dec-27 PT $625 (prior $550), px 12-Aug $492.43; $625 = 24x P/E on CY28 EPS of $26.05** vs ~18-19x for the tech comp set (AMZN, AAPL, GOOG, CRM, INTU, META). Estimates (FYE Jun): revenue **FY26A $331.8bn → FY27E $392.8bn (+18.4%) → FY28E $475.1bn (+20.9%)**; Adj. EBIT $155.2 → $183.4 → $224.1bn (margin 46.8/46.7/47.2%); **Adj. EPS $17.28 → $19.67 → $23.85 (FY28E raised from $23.15, the note's only estimate change)**; Adj. P/E 28.5/25.0/20.6x; OCF $182.9 → $222.6 → $261.2bn; capex $115.9 → $197.1 → $268.5bn (22.9→34.9→50.2→56.5% of sales); **FCFF $67.0bn → $25.5bn → −$7.3bn**. Thesis: **PBP ≈ one-third of revenue but ~45% of company profit in the out-year at a 60% operating margin (FY26 PBP revenue $140bn, +16%)**; **M365 Copilot TAM $24-41bn realised (30-50% attach at $30/user/mo, 50% realised-vs-list) vs ~$5bn run-rate at 30mn seats = 7% of a 450mn+ commercial seat base**; Azure "supply constrained, not demand constrained," past **$100bn in FY26**, cc growth 34%→43% over eight quarters, **~45% cc guided for F1Q27**; **commercial RPO $678bn (+84%, +$51bn q/q), ~30% (~$200bn) recognisable within 12m, duration 2.3 yrs vs AMZN 6.4 / ORCL ~3.3, largest and fastest-converting hyperscaler book (ORCL $638bn, GOOG $520bn, AMZN $496bn)**; **OpenAI ~45% of the $625bn F2Q26 balance (≈$281bn), $24.1bn of FY26 revenue (~7%), ~90% of $214bn FY26 Microsoft Cloud revenue from non-frontier customers**; capex ~$145bn FY26 (record $41bn in F4Q, ~2/3 short-lived assets), CY26 ~$190bn → ~$175bn on the 15→25-yr useful-life lease reclassification ("a reclassification rather than a reduction"). Margin ladder: Intelligent Cloud GM 67% (FY23) → 58% (FY26), Microsoft Cloud GM ~72% → 66%, **incremental GM stabilising low-40s**, IC operating margin resilient ~41%. Silicon: **Maia 200 live in Iowa/Arizona, >30% better tokens per dollar vs merchant silicon, 40% better perf/watt**, MAI cost cuts (89% Dynamics voice, 84% PowerPoint imagery, MAI-Code-1-Flash −10% median tokens, 40% inference-throughput gain), **Maia 300 unveil "this fall" with Asia supply-chain demand for 300K chips in 2027** (⚠️ volumes/fleet share undisclosed — JPM says the 30% cannot yet be sized into margin). Re-rating case: **20% revenue / 19% EPS CAGR CY25-29 vs ~8%/15% for the S&P 500; MSFT at ~20x CY28 vs ~17x for the index; a 21% premium vs a 43% historical average**. Industry backdrop from JPM's Comms Infra + Asia Hardware teams: **global AI capacity 60GW (2025) → 253GW (2031) vs demand 116GW → 532GW**. ⚠️ Cover-vs-body inconsistency on the prior PT's base year — see `## Changelog`.
